# Delivery baseline suite — the result-comparison gate

Replaces byte-identical s18 md5 as the gate for **behaviour-changing** work (routing-liveness, gateway/cross-layer, etc.). Gate on the **delivery breakdown**, not the byte stream.

> remote-admin, channel-crypt + location), the pending owner decisions, and the method lessons.

## ★ THE MANDATORY GATE — run for EVERY change (all three; a pass on one does NOT substitute for another)
1. **Delivery baseline suite** (the table below): no regression vs the reference numbers + `leaks == 0`. This is the gate for behaviour-changing routing/gateway/cross-layer work (it supersedes the raw s18 md5 *there* — s18's delivery legitimately shifts).
2. **Mobile / team self-checking scenarios** — `s21_mobile_dm_milestone`, `s22_mobile_team`, `s23_mobile_team_multihop`, **`s24_static_and_team_multihop`** (static↔team + dual-relay separation), **`s25_two_team_separation`** (team↔team separation), `s26_team_reroute`, **`s27_cross_layer_mobiles`** (30+1 asserts incl. the voluntary S6.4-C re-home pin), **`s28_mixed_team_channels`** (41 asserts — mixed homed/off-grid team, channels all planes, home-death survival, team DM-by-hash both directions; PROMOTED 2026-07-20c at full green), **`s29_mixed_leaf_team`** (14 asserts — MIXED-LEAF team: members homed onto layers 20/23 + off-grid third nibble, shared PHY; cross-nibble team DV/DM/hash/channel + containment; PROMOTED 2026-07-20d), **`s30_team_dad_mediation`** (15 asserts — hidden-terminal same-team-id collision, third-party mediated DENY, loser re-pick, winner no-flap, static containment; PROMOTED 2026-07-21d): **0 assertion failures**, each. **NOT optional** — mandatory for ANY change, and especially any change touching the mobile / team / E2E-crypto planes. These are as required as the delivery suite. (s24/s25 are GREEN as of the team-plane routing-parity work — spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-15-team-plane-routing-parity-design.md`.)

**Run (per scenario):**
```
lus -e meshroute simulation/<s>.json /tmp/<s>.ndjson
python3 tools/dm_delivery_breakdown.py simulation/<s>.json /tmp/<s>.ndjson --failures
```

★ **Cross-layer is SEED-SENSITIVE → gate on a MULTI-SEED MEAN, never the single configured seed.** s15 over seeds {1522, 1, 42, 100, 7, 2024, 999} = **13 / 21 / 21 / 17 / 20 / 20 / 21 = mean 19/21 ≈ 90%** (range 62–100%). The scenario's baked-in seed **1522 is the worst case (13/21)** — do NOT gate on it alone (pre-fix it read 0/21, which is what made it look like a collapse). s15 channels = 218/224 (97%), `leaks 0`. Apply the same multi-seed discipline to the other cross-layer scenarios where feasible.

**What changed vs the old 2026-06-17 baseline (and why) — read before trusting a diff against history:**
- **s16 cross 15% → 71%** and **s15 cross 67%(single-seed) → ≈90%(multi-seed)**: FIXED. The gateway-window beacon **align** (`3dbaa14`) carries the 2-layer dense case (s16); the **reactive route-pull** (a gateway fires REQ_SYNC + defers, instead of dropping, on a bridged-leg route-miss) carries the 3-layer case (s15). Root cause of the old lows: dirty-only steady-state beacons never re-advertise stable routes to a time-multiplexing gateway (reactive-pull spec §1; the byte-budget/truncation theory was investigated and **ruled out**).
- **s18 same 108 → 98** and **s17 same 30 → 26**: down-shifts from the intervening gateway-rework cluster (Phase-4 / Gateway-fixes / bitmap-deffer, `93fa6c5`…`b15586a`) — airtime/timing chaos, not the cross-layer fixes (s18 multi-seed mean ≈101, keystone seed 157373 = 110 ≥ 108). ⚠ Recorded as the new reference; lifting them back is a **separate** investigation, not a cross-layer-fix regression.
- **s18 same 98 → 101 (2026-06-29): asymmetric-link-aware routing IMPROVED it.** s18 is the realistic Seattle mesh (`SEAMESH`/`First_Hill`/`Fremont`) with genuine **directional links** (topology `bidir:false`). The new bidirectionality plane — heard-set census + the `one_way` route penalty (commit "Slice 5") — detects ~478 of these asymmetric link-instances per run and re-routes around them → **+3 delivered**, deterministic, `leaks 0` (the configured seed rises to ≈ the prior multi-seed mean). The slow-reprobe (Slice 6) is **delivery-neutral on the idealized sim** but reclaims airtime: node-72 9-node 16-seed A/B, doomed `rts_tx` to the isolated nodes (204/247) **−21%** (149→118), total channel load −16%, delivery flat. The lucky-marginal delivery tradeoff (sim has the isolated nodes at 0%, metal ~40%) is **metal-tuned** via `link_reprobe_ttl_ms` (60 s seed) + the `bidi_penalty_one_way_q4` 640→192 fallback — not a sim judgment.

★★★★ **PICKING THIS UP? READ `docs/2026-08-01-agent-handover.md` FIRST** — it supersedes the 07-31 handover and carries the
open queue, the standing rulings and the method rules. This file is the **evidence store**: one note per slice, **newest at
the top**, and **the newest note naming a scenario is the authoritative anchor** — never an md5 quoted from a historical one.

**Notes / how to read the gate:**
- `s16` 71% and `s15` ≈90% are **post-fix** numbers (the old 15%/67% are superseded). Both still have headroom (s16 is a deliberate overload; s15's worst seed lands 13/21). The gate is **don't regress below these** + `leaks == 0`.
- `s17_metro` cross-layer is **inert** on meshroute (untranslated source = single-layer gateways; *translating* it degrades the dense scenario — channels 101%→33%). So gate `s17` on **same-layer 26/30 + channels leaks 0 + scale**, not cross-layer. (Cross-layer coverage comes from s09/s10/s15/s16.) **Follow-up:** cross-layer-at-scale window tuning — separate from the baseline.
- `s19_singlelayer_multihop_chain` is the suite's **multi-hop** coverage (s18/s17 same-layer are single-hop). Redundant 3-hop chain `A-{L,L2}-{R,R2}-B`, no A↔B direct link ⇒ every A↔B DM relays ≥2 hops (verified: A→L2→R2→B = 3 hops; routes converge at hops 2–4). Two disjoint paths ⇒ also the **liveness-reroute base** (kill one relay → reroute via the parallel path; the `t96` Phase-2 gate builds on this). Gate on **delivery 8/8** + the **mean_hops** column (A↔B **3.0**, A↔R **2.0**) — deterministic (seed 42, lossless links). (`dm_delivery_breakdown` counts hops via distinct `data_rx` receivers, which carry `origin`, rather than the origin-less relay `data_tx` — fixed 2026-06-17 so same-layer multi-hop is measured. Cross-layer hop-count is still uncounted — its records key on the gateway wire-dst; cross-layer *delivery* is measured by the `cross-layer DMs` line.)

⚠ **Object count is printed beside every row on purpose** (the trap that produced a 0-object "green light wired to nothing"). Every cell is a CLEAN build — `rm -rf .pio/build/<env>` per env, one `pio run` at a time, 22 builds total, all rc=0. `-Wswitch` **0 on all eleven, both arms.** ELF md5s were captured and are **worthless as usual** (they move on every arm) — except `native`, which reproduced `9a63fa3a` on both arms and across the earlier session, because it carries no `__DATE__`/`GIT_REV` banner.
⚠ **`GIT_REV` was controlled, not hoped for:** the tree was already dirty on both arms (B61 + four doc edits), so `git_rev.py` injected the identical `39c5224-dirty` into all four `xiao_sx1262`-family images. ⚠ **And trap ① reproduced for a third time:** my BEFORE arm reads `xiao_sx1262` Flash **512020** where the 2026-08-03 eleven-env grid reads **512052** — the same −32 banner-packing step B61 measured, on an unchanged source. **Same-session pre/post is the only valid byte comparand; the recorded grid is an object-count / warning-count reference only.**

★★★★★ **THE THREE-ENV DELTA, ATTRIBUTED TO THE BYTE — AND IT IS 15, NOT 192.** On all three Heltec envs the *only* differing sections are `.debug_line` **+15** and `.debug_str` **+15**. `len("variants/heltec_v3/board_ui.cpp") − len("src/board_ui.cpp") = 31 − 16 = **15**`. ⇒ the entire cost of the move on the envs that actually compile the file is **the source path being 15 bytes longer, recorded twice in DWARF**. **Flash-bearing sections, RAM and the 11232-symbol multiset are IDENTICAL.** The flashed image is functionally the same firmware.

★★★★★ **THE ⚠ ROWS ARE THE REAL FINDING, AND THE CAUSE IS NOT THE DIRECTORY — IT IS LINK-SET MEMBERSHIP OF A ZERO-BYTE OBJECT.** `+<board_ui.cpp>` was listed in **three** base filters, not one (`xiao_sx1262:117`, `heltec_v3:205`, `xiao_esp32s3:253`); `MR_FEAT_OLED` defaults **0** (`lib/core/mr_features.h:39-41`) and is set only on `heltec_v3`, so on the two XIAO families the TU compiled to an object with **`.text` 0 / `.data` 0 / `.bss` 0** — but it *was* a link input (`board_ui.cpp.o` present in every build dir). Once the file leaves `src/`, no glob in those filters can match it (`platformio/fs.py:156-186` — `match_src_files` is `glob.glob`, so a dangling `+<…>` silently matches **nothing**), so **A0 changes the link inputs of 10 of 11 envs and only `native` is structurally untouched.** Per-env section arithmetic, exact:

| env | `.dram0.bss` | `.flash.rodata` | `.flash.text` | `.xt.lit` | `.xt.prop` | = pio RAM / Flash |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `xiao_esp32s3` | **−8** | −8 | **+176** | −8 | −12 | −8 / +168 ✓ |
| `gateway_esp32s3` | **−8** | −8 | **+56** | — | — | −8 / +48 ✓ |
| `xiao_esp32s3_mobile` | **−8** | −8 | **+132** | −8 | −12 | −8 / +124 ✓ |

The ~30 differing symbols are **all framework** — `WiFiGenericClass::mode` (0x3dc→0x3ec), `STAClass::connect`, `APClass::create`, `NetworkClient::write`, `UpdateClass::_decryptBuffer`, `WiFiScanClass::_scanDone` … **not one symbol of ours changes size, appears or disappears.** Same xtensa link-order relaxation signature the 2026-08-03 noise-floor table recorded — reached here by *removing* an object rather than by moving one.

★★★ **DECOUPLED BY PROBE, NOT BY ARGUMENT — this is what kills the "+192 B is the cost of leaving `src/`" premise.** **P-A0-2:** put the same file back into `xiao_esp32s3`'s filter **from its new `variants/heltec_v3/` path** (link-set restored, directory changed) → **every section identical to the BEFORE arm, symbol multiset identical, RAM 211196, Flash 1208036.** ⇒ **the directory is irrelevant to code size; link-set membership is the whole effect.** The earlier +192 B was a property of moving `device_ota.cpp` — a 123-line TU with a 227 KB object — i.e. of reordering **non-empty** objects, not of crossing the boundary. ⚠ And the surprising half: the object A0 removes is **zero bytes of code, data and bss**, and removing it still moves `.flash.text` by up to **+176 B** and resizes 30 framework functions. **On ARM the identical removal is free (0 sections, identical symbols, identical RAM+Flash on all four nRF52 envs).** ⇒ *a future size gate must treat "an object entered or left the link set" as an xtensa-only ±200 B event even when the object is empty.* Registered **B63**.

★★ **THE POSITIVE CONTROLS, because every claim above is a 0 or an equality.** ① **P-A0-1 (the instrument fires):** point the heltec filter at a non-existent `board_ui_PROBE_NOMATCH.cpp` → `heltec_v3` **rc=1**, `ld: undefined reference to '_Z10mr_ui_initv' / '_Z10mr_ui_tickm' / '_Z13mr_ui_on_pushRKN9meshroute4PushE'`, objs 194→193. ⇒ **"heltec_v3 linked" is real evidence that the new path matched** — with `MR_FEAT_OLED=1` the three hooks are declared non-inline (`lib/hal/mr_ui.h:18-21`), so a silent no-match is a hard link error, not a green build. (Serial, so the failure is genuine and not the concurrent-`pio` archive corruption.) ② **Seam still wired:** `nm` finds exactly **3** `mr_ui_*` symbols in each Heltec ELF and **0** in every other env (the `#else` inline no-ops), and the compiler's own `.d` at the new location resolves `mr_features.h`/`mr_ui.h`/`command.h`/`protocol_constants.h` from `lib/core` + `lib/hal`. ③ **`production`'s warning count −1 (18917→18916) is an attributed control, not noise:** that env re-defines `MR_CONSOLE`, so `"MR_CONSOLE" redefined` fires **once per TU** — 284→283 occurrences. The warning instrument is TU-sensitive and saw exactly one TU leave. ④ **Restore controls:** `platformio.ini` md5 returns to `2c42e116` after each probe and a fresh clean rebuild of `heltec_v3` **and** `xiao_esp32s3` reproduces the AFTER arm's **entire** section table — i.e. two builds of identical source in one session are exact, on xtensa as well. ⑤ **`lus` rebuild:** md5 `8cb1f0f5` unchanged, and the "make had nothing to do" reading was **proved not to be a broken instrument** — `touch lib/core/node.cpp` made it recompile both core libs and relink `lus`, which then reproduced `8cb1f0f5` and s18 `1cd21235` again.

⛔⛔ **FOUR PREMISES OF THE DISPATCH THAT MEASUREMENT DISPROVED** (spec §0 / plan ⛳ A0 / the brief — **report only, none of those files edited**):
1. **"rewire the `build_src_filter` of the three Heltec envs"** — there is **one** such line. `gateway_heltec` and `heltec_mobile` declare no filter; they inherit `heltec_v3:205` via `extends` (as this file's own 2026-08-03 note already recorded: only 4 of 11 envs declare one).
2. **"+ `-I`"** — **not needed, and adding it would be dead config.** Measured from the compiler's dependency file, not argued: `board_ui.cpp`'s four headers all resolve from `lib/core/` + `lib/hal/`, **none from `src/`**, at the old path and the new one alike. The `-I variants/heltec_v3` the plan asks for becomes load-bearing only at **Phase A Task 5**, which creates `board_ui.h` *in that directory* and includes it from `src/firmware_ui.cpp` (plan:967/1022/1085). ⇒ **it belongs to Task 5, not A0.**
3. **"the other EIGHT envs must be EXACTLY untouched"** — only `native` is untouched by construction. **Seven** of the eight lose an object from their link set, three of them measurably. The correct statement of the gate is *"the four nRF52 envs and native are exactly equal; the three xiao_esp32s3 envs move by an attributed link-relaxation delta with no symbol of ours affected."*
4. **"expect ~±200 B flash and framework function motion on the Heltec envs; a rename inside `src/` is free, crossing the boundary is not"** — inverted. Crossing the boundary while staying in the link set is **free to the byte** (P-A0-2); the ±200 B lives in link-set membership, and it landed on the envs the brief listed as the safe half.

arm in the same session as its AFTER arm.** Diffing against this table would misattribute ±32 B of banner packing to the
file move, on top of the ~192 B the move genuinely costs. The eleven rows remain valid as an *order-of-magnitude* record
and as the object-count / warning-count / `-Wswitch` reference.

★★★ **READ THAT TABLE BEFORE WRITING A GATE FOR THIS SLICE.** Two conclusions, both measured, both load-bearing:
1. **The prescribed controls are the right ones and their noise floor is ZERO** — sections, symbol multiset, RAM and the warning multiset all reproduce exactly across independent clean builds, and the round-trip row proves the tree really was restored. The **ELF md5 is worthless** (it changes on an *unchanged* tree), and so is any flashed-image byte comparison (**71 bytes** move with zero source change; a mere rename moves **996336**).
2. ⛔ **…but a CORRECT, semantics-preserving file move VIOLATES them anyway.** Moving one 123-line TU out of `src/` with **zero content change** shifted `heltec_v3` flash **+192 B** and RAM **−8 B** and changed the size of **14 Arduino/ESP-IDF framework functions** (`WiFiGenericClass::mode`, `STAClass::connect`, `UpdateClass::_decryptBuffer`, `NetworkClient::write`, `APClass::create`, …) — **none of them ours**. Attribution is decoupled and clean: the added `-I src` was proven **innocent** (row 2: 0 section deltas, identical symbols), and a rename *within* `src/` reproduces a smaller ±8 B version of the same effect ⇒ the cause is **object link order shifting the layout, and the xtensa linker re-relaxing call sites across it**. ⇒ **"byte-identical per env" is UNACHIEVABLE for this slice on the ESP32 targets**, and the plan's step-0.2 gate wording (*"each byte-identical or its delta attributed"*) survives only in its second half — where "attributing" means explaining framework code motion, per env, per moved file. **Budget for that, or gate on something else.**

### ★★ THE DERIVED CONDITIONAL SET — reproduce it, never quote it

Mechanical scan of every `#if / #ifdef / #ifndef / #elif` **site** in `src/` + `lib/hal/` (whole-word match, continuation lines joined):

| axis | **sites** | note |
|---|---|---|
| total conditional sites | **124** | the denominator |
| name a board macro (`BOARD_XIAO_WIO_SX1262` / `BOARD_HELTEC_V3` / `BOARD_XIAO_ESP32S3`) | **26** | …of which **23 also name a chip-family macro** |
| ★★ name **ONLY** a board macro — i.e. genuinely board-discriminating | **3** | `src/firmware_commands.cpp:340 / :342 / :344` — **one** `#if/#elif/#elif` chain, the body of `board_name()` |
| nRF52 family (`ARDUINO_ARCH_NRF52`, `NRF52_SERIES`, `NRF52_PLATFORM`, `NRF52840_XXAA`) | **20** | |
| ESP32 family (`ESP32`, `ARDUINO_ARCH_ESP32`, `ESP_ARDUINO_VERSION_MAJOR`) | **13** | |
| ★ **host-vs-device** (`ARDUINO`, `MESHROUTE_NATIVE`) | **10** | **a FOURTH axis the spec's table omits** — and the one the native tests depend on |

★ **Positive control for that "3":** the same instrument correctly flags `device_ota.cpp` as wholly ESP32-gated and finds all 26 board sites, so a 3 is "3", not "cannot reach".

**Where board-specific detail ACTUALLY lives: `platformio.ini` build_flags — 56 `-D`, not 26 `#if`.** Declared per board: `xiao_sx1262` **25** · `heltec_v3` **16** · `xiao_esp32s3` **15** (pins `LORA_PIN_*` / `P_LORA_*`, RF wiring `SX126X_*`, and per-board capability switches `MR_FEAT_OLED`, `QSPIFLASH`, `NRFX_QSPI_ENABLED`, `CFG_TUD_*`, `LFS_NO_ASSERT`).

### ⛔ THE TWO FALSIFIED PREMISES (spec §0 ① and ②) — this is why nothing moved

**Ⓐ `boards/` IS TAKEN — by PlatformIO, and this time the ownership is real.** `boards_dir` is a first-class PlatformIO project option, *"A storage for custom board manifests"*, default `${PROJECT_DIR}/boards` (`platformio/project/options.py:297`), scanned in `platform/base.py:143-157`. It already holds **`seeed-xiao-afruitnrf52-nrf52840.json`** (named by `board =` at `platformio.ini:97`) and **`nrf52840_s140_v7.ld`** (`board_build.ldscript`, `:98`). Subdirectories survive (the scan is a non-recursive `*.json` filter), so `boards/heltec_v3/` would *build* — but it plants our source tree inside a tool-owned namespace, **which is the exact objection §0 ① raised in order to reject `variants/`.**

**…and the grounds for rejecting `variants/` do not hold.** §0 ① says *"the nRF52 core **searches that tree** for `variant.h`"*. It does not, in this project: `adafruit.py:262-264` uses `$PROJECT_DIR/<build.variants_dir>` **only when the board manifest sets `build.variants_dir`** — and `boards/seeed-xiao-afruitnrf52-nrf52840.json` **does not set it**, so `variants_dir` resolves to `FRAMEWORK_DIR/variants`, which contains **no `Seeed_XIAO_nRF52840` at all** (14 Adafruit variants, verified). Our `variants/Seeed_XIAO_nRF52840/` is reached **exclusively** by the two hand-wired lines the spec cites as evidence of core ownership: `platformio.ini:117` (`build_src_filter += <../variants/…/variant.cpp>`) and `:137` (`-I variants/Seeed_XIAO_nRF52840`). ⇒ `variants/` is **ours already**, and it is already "one directory per board" containing exactly one board.

★ **The constructive consequence — UNTESTED HYPOTHESIS, owner ruling needed:** adding `"variants_dir": "variants"` to the board manifest hands that directory to the toolchain, which then compiles `variant.cpp` as `FrameworkArduinoVariant` and puts it on CPPPATH by itself ⇒ **`platformio.ini:117`'s `../variants/…` term and `:137`'s `-I` both DROP** (they must drop together, or `g_ADigitalPinMap` is defined twice). That is "adding a board = adding a directory" delivered through the toolchain's own hook instead of a parallel convention. **Not built, not gated — do not adopt on my word.**

**Ⓑ ★★ THERE IS NO BOARD TIER TO ABSORB.** §0 ② tabulates *"board — 25 uses — absorbed by a per-board dir ✅ yes"*. The 14 + 11 reproduce exactly as token occurrences, but **23 of the 26 sites are chip-family OR-chains in which the board macro is a redundant alias**, e.g. `device_nv.h:353`:

```c
#if defined(ARDUINO) && (defined(NRF52_SERIES) || defined(ARDUINO_ARCH_NRF52) || defined(NRF52840_XXAA) || defined(BOARD_XIAO_WIO_SX1262))
```

Moving those into `boards/<board>/` would move **chip-family** code into a **board** directory — precisely the U1 duplication §0 ② was written to prevent, achieved by the mechanism it prescribes. ⚠ **The table also double-counts:** those same 23 sites are counted once under "board" (via the alias) and again under "chip/arch" (via the three chip macros), so the "25 vs ~65" ratio is a measurement artefact. Real site counts: **33 chip · 3 board.** And `BOARD_XIAO_ESP32S3` (2 occurrences, `device_fault.h:20`, `firmware_commands.cpp:342`) is missing from the table altogether.

### ⛔ AND THE CHIP TIER CANNOT BE `git mv`-ed — the architecture is header-inline, deliberately, and tested

Step 0.1 is *"`git mv` only — no content edits"*. Measured against the tree, **exactly one file qualifies**: `src/device_ota.cpp` (123 lines, wholly inside `#if ESP32…#endif`, listed only in the two ESP32 base filters). Every other chip-conditional file is **one portable-interface header with the per-backend arms inline, including a HOST arm**:

| file | lines | arms | why it cannot move |
|---|---|---|---|
| `src/device_nv.h` | 528 | nRF52 LittleFS `:353` · ESP32 NVS `:430` · **no-backend/host `:462`** | included by **6 files incl. the native `test/test_device_nv.cpp`**; its header comment records that the policy was hoisted **above** `#if defined(ARDUINO)` (the NV1 cleanup) *so a host test could reach it* |
| `src/device_rng.h` | 85 | nRF52 · ESP32 · else | included by 5 files |
| `src/device_ble.h` | 205 | nRF52 impl + **inert stubs** for ESP32/native | the stub arm is the documented pattern (`CODE_GUIDELINES` "inert stubs so callers stay stable") |
| `src/device_inbox_store.h` | 366 | nRF52+QSPI · else | |
| ★★ `src/device_fault.h` | 254 | nRF52 `:29` · ESP32 `:183` — **and it DEFINES `MRFAULT_HW`/`MRFAULT_ESP32`** | **single-TU** (only `fw_main.cpp` includes it) and `fw_main.cpp` has **6** `MRFAULT_*`-guarded sites. Moving any of them into a `platform/*` TU **green-compiles the `#else`** — the silent regression `CODE_GUIDELINES` already names |
| `src/fw_main.cpp` | 1422 | ~29 arch sites | interleaved *inside* functions (`fw_reboot`, `do_ota`): extraction, not relocation |
| `lib/hal/device_radio.h` | 340 | 1 nRF52 site, nested in `MR_RADIO_CANARY` | and it is sim-compiled ⇒ any move is s18-live |

⇒ splitting these is an **interface/impl restructure**, a content edit, and C1 puts it in a different slice from a file move. Per the brief's own instruction — *"if a conditional cannot move cleanly without changing meaning, leave it and record it"* — it is recorded, not done.

⇒ ⛔ **`src/device_nv.h` must NEVER land in `platform/nrf52/`**: it would orphan the ESP32 and host arms and break a native test. It is not chip code; it is a portable interface.

### Two more corrections to the gate's own framing

- **11 envs, but only 4 declare `build_src_filter`**: `native:73`, `xiao_sx1262:117`, `heltec_v3:205`, `xiao_esp32s3:253`. The other 7 inherit via `extends`. And **`native`'s filter contains no board code at all** (`+<sim_main.cpp> -<fw_main.cpp>`), so a board/platform reorg does not change native's link inputs. The 11-env gate is still right — the 7 role envs inherit the changed filters — but not for the stated reason.
- **3 physical boards + host, not "~4 boards"**: `xiao_sx1262` → {`xiao_sx1262`, `gateway`, `production`, `xiao_mobile`} · `heltec_v3` → {`heltec_v3`, `gateway_heltec`, `heltec_mobile`} · `xiao_esp32s3` → {`xiao_esp32s3`, `gateway_esp32s3`, `xiao_esp32s3_mobile`} · `native`. §0 ③'s *principle* (one dir per board, several envs) reproduces exactly.
- ⚠ **NEW GATE HAZARD, not in the brief: `GIT_REV` embeds `-dirty`.** `tools/git_rev.py` appends `-dirty` when the tree differs from HEAD, on `xiao_sx1262` (inherited by `gateway`/`production`/`xiao_mobile`). A BEFORE built on a clean tree vs an AFTER built with moves staged differs in `.rodata` by the **6 bytes of `"-dirty"`** for a non-code reason. ⇒ **both arms must be equally dirty.** (This baseline: dirty on both, tracked doc edits pre-existed.)

### What a corrected Phase 0 needs (report only — spec/plan NOT edited, per instruction)

1. **Owner ruling on the top-level name**, since `boards/` is PlatformIO's. Free and verified unowned: `platform/`. Already ours: `variants/`.
2. **Drop steps 0.1–0.3 as scoped.** They target a tier of 3 sites. Step **0.4** (`capabilities.h` per board) is the only step aimed at the real defect — and the real defect is the **56 `-D` in `platformio.ini`**, not the `#if`s.
3. **If the chip tier is still wanted**, it is a *pattern change* (portable header + per-chip TUs) with its own slice, its own spec, and `device_fault.h`'s single-TU/`MRFAULT_*` trap called out as the first hazard. It is not a `git mv`.
4. **For the actual goals** — Heltec V4 and the OLED port — neither needs this: V4 needs the `IBoardRf` seam (spec 2026-08-01) plus a pin/PA table, and the OLED needs `board_ui.cpp`, which §0 ④ correctly measures as **32 lines, one `MR_FEAT_OLED` guard, zero board conditionals** (reproduced). ⇒ **Phase 0 is not a prerequisite for Phase A on the evidence available.**

**★★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-08-02 §id-hash S4b (`§id-hash S4b` — spec §5 stages 3-5: the by-id `reqpubkey` becomes ONE command. **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL, and this time the 0/36 is PROVEN rather than argued** — UNCOMMITTED):** an unresolved, plane-selected `reqpubkey <id>` now arms a **bounded `resolve-id-for-pubkey` intent** (`PendingIdPubkey[4]`, Node-global) beside its stage-1 BY_ID query; the ordinary id→hash answer **consumes** it in `on_hash_bind_response` and emits the pre-existing HARD `WANT_PUBKEY` query **by the returned hash**; a bounded sweep on `kAgingTimerId` gives up loudly. ⚠⚠ **READ THIS FIRST — THE TREES OVERLAPPED, AND WHAT THAT DOES AND DOES NOT COST.** S4b was dispatched while S4a was still finishing, so both slices edited `node.cpp` / `node_hashlocate.cpp` / `node.h` / `command.h` / `protocol_constants.h` concurrently. **Every number above is nevertheless S4b-ISOLATED, and one measurement is what earns that claim:** the comparand was built by taking the live tree, reverting **only** the S4b hunks in a scratch COPY, and building `lus` from it — **it reproduces `ca18b8fd`, the exact md5 BASELINE records for S4a's final state.** ⇒ the BEFORE arm *is* S4a, verified rather than assumed, and the delta is S4b's alone. ★ The same copy-and-revert produced the board BEFORE figures, and the un-reverted copy **reproduced the live tree's RAM/flash on all three envs to the byte** — that calibration is what makes the ΔFlash numbers readable at all. **S4a's own figures are NOT re-derived here and must be read from its note.**

★★★★★★ **THE HEADLINE IS A DISAGREEMENT WITH THE DISPATCH, AND IT IS ABOUT WHAT AN ACKNOWLEDGEMENT MAY CLAIM — the same question that cost this arc three review rounds, arriving one level up exactly as the brief predicted.** The brief said: *"B55 closes — `reqpubkey_sent` with `hash:0` is the app-visible symptom of the two-stage split; after S4b that case should disappear."* **IT DOES NOT DISAPPEAR, AND IT MUST NOT.** `hash == 0` is the **honest report of a real stage-1 acceptance**: the frame the TX path took was the *id→hash* query, and the hash is precisely what it went to ask for — no synchronous `CmdResult` can carry a value that does not exist yet. Making it disappear would mean either suppressing a true event (re-creating the silence S1b removed) or inventing a hash. ⇒ **the BYTES are unchanged and the app's INSTRUCTION is inverted**: `hash:0` used to mean *"re-issue `reqpubkey <id>` once the binding lands"*, and now means *"do not re-issue — the node does stage 2 itself."* ★ **B55 therefore MUTATES rather than closes** (entry rewritten in place, not ticked), and the contract paragraph it owes is now a *correction* rather than an addition — an app built against the S4a wording will fire a redundant duplicate flood. ⚠ **This is a live app-contract hazard on a QA-owned file; reported, not written.**

★★★★★ **THE 0/36 IS PAIRED WITH THREE CAPABILITY PROBES, BECAUSE "the corpus cannot reach it" IS A CLAIM UNTIL YOU MAKE IT FIRE.** The corpus genuinely cannot ORIGINATE a by-id request (`NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp` parses `reqpubkey <hex>` only and hard-sets `dst_id = 0`), so nothing can arm an intent — but that argument covers only the ARM. Each of S4b's other two sites was poisoned to fire unconditionally, and **all three of them are emphatically live in the corpus:**

| probe | what it forces | movers | assertion failures | what it establishes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P-S4B-CONSUME** — `id_pubkey_intent_consume`'s match test defeated | escalate on EVERY id→hash answer | **7/36** (s15 · s15_metal · s24 · s25 · s26 · s28 · s34) | 0 | ★★ the consume hook is on a hot, corpus-reachable path — a clean 0/36 means *"no intent ever matches"*, never *"dead code"* |
| **P-S4B-SWEEP** — the age-out's `id == 0` guard defeated | report on every aging tick | **36/36** | 0 | ★★ the timeout sweep runs on **every node of every scenario**; the periodic hook is not merely compiled, it executes |
| **P-S4B-PREFLIGHT** — the `by_id` gate widened to every `reqpubkey`, refusing | refuse the command arm | **1/36** (s22) | **3** | ★ the command arm is corpus-reachable and `by_id` is provably the only thing that is never true there — **and the assertion counter goes 0 → 3, so the instrument is not vacuous** |

⇒ **restore control: the copy re-synced and rebuilt returns `lus` to `50a2f604` and the 36-row sweep to byte-identical with the clean run.** ★★ **EVERY PROBE RAN IN A SCRATCH COPY AGAINST `-DMESHROUTE_DIR`, NEVER IN THE REAL TREE** — the simulator's CMake takes the firmware path as a cache variable, so the whole poison matrix is reachable with the working tree untouched. That structurally removes the hazard that destroyed a slice's work two notes ago (`git checkout --` on a tree carrying five uncommitted slices). **Use this method; do not poison in place.**

⚠⚠ **TWO INSTRUMENT FAILURES OF MINE, BOTH CAUGHT BY A RESTORE CONTROL RATHER THAN BY CARE — and they are opposite halves of the same trap the dispatch named.**
· **`rsync -a` PRESERVES mtimes, so `make` skipped the rebuild entirely** and my first "restore" reported `ca18b8fd` when the sources said `50a2f604`. The control caught it in one line; without it every probe after that point would have been measuring the same stale binary.
· **`touch` IS A NO-OP FOR PLATFORMIO** (content signature, not mtime), so my first board warning A/B compiled **0 objects** and reported **0 warnings** on all three envs — a green light wired to nothing, the exact shape of the `"ok":false` grep two notes ago. The fix is `rm -rf .pio/build/<env>/{src,lib*}`; the tell is the **object count**, which is now reported beside every warning total (221 / 221 / 141, equal on both arms).
⇒ ★★ **the two build systems fail in OPPOSITE directions — ninja/make trusts mtime and ignores content, PlatformIO trusts content and ignores mtime — so one restore recipe cannot serve both. Report the object count and the binary md5, never "I rebuilt it".**

★★★★ **A REAL DEFECT S4a SHIPPED, FOUND BY ASKING WHAT STAGE 2 NEEDS THAT STAGE 1 DOES NOT CHECK — and it is the brief's own rule pointed forwards.** `emit_hash_query` gates `_crypto_ready` on `want_pubkey` (`node_hashlocate.cpp`), and **stage 1 passes `want_pubkey = false`**. ⇒ a node with NO crypto identity was told `queued` + `accepted`, flooded the by-id query, and could only discover at stage 2 — in an RX callback, ~25 s later, with the ack long returned — that the MUTUAL exchange is impossible. **An ack may not claim what it cannot know; it equally may not DEFER a failure it already can.** ⇒ the by-id arm now pre-flights `_crypto_ready` and returns the existing `err_no_identity` (remedy: `regen`) **before any airtime**. ⚠ **This CHANGES S4a's behaviour on an identity-less node** — `queued/accepted` → `err_no_identity` — deliberately, and it is the one behaviour delta S4b makes to an already-landed slice.
ⓘ The other four stage-2 outcomes were enumerated and are **not** knowable at stage 1: `degenerate` depends on the answer, `tx_dropped` is transient, `encode_failed` cannot occur (the buffer is sized for the larger frame), and `no_return_route` is evaluated identically at both stages (S4a already widened its guard to `by_id`), so a stage-1 pass implies a stage-2 pass.

★★★ **THE ESCALATION IS *NOT* THE AUTO-RESOLUTION `§no-auto-reqpubkey` FORBIDS, AND THE ARGUMENT IS IN-SOURCE AT THE EMIT SITE** (`id_pubkey_intent_consume`), not in a doc a future reader will not open. That ruling (owner-ratified 2026-07-29) forbids a **send** silently escalating into a WANT_PUBKEY locate, because it would substitute on-air TOFU for the QR ceremony on a message the user marked `-e`. Here the operator **typed `reqpubkey`** — the verb whose entire purpose is that on-air fetch — so stage 2 is the *completion of the command they issued*, it is bounded, and it is reported. The four `want_pubkey = false` locate sites the ruling names are untouched and keep their tags. ★ **A future slice that deletes this as "auto-resolution" would be reversing a distinction, not restoring a ruling.**

★★ **THE TIMEOUT REUSES THE AGING SWEEP AND DOES NOT PRETEND TO BE TIGHTER THAN IT IS.** `age_out_pending_id_pubkey` sits on `kAgingTimerId` directly beside `age_out_parked_sends` — the same sweep for the same question ("a hash-locate that never came back") — so **no timer id is consumed** (the wheel is at `kCap 91` with 0 free). Budget = `park_reflood_retry_ms` (25 s = ONE flood round-trip by that constant's own derivation), **not** `hash_locate_giveup_ms` (175 s), which prices park + six refloods this intent does not perform. ⇒ the honest bound is the **interval** `[25 s, 25 s + rt_aging_check_period_ms] = [25 s, 85 s]`, stated in `protocol_constants.h` and not rounded away. The report is an unconditional **`!!`-prefixed `_hal.log`** line beside its `MR_EMIT`, reusing S1d/QA-P2's fix verbatim: `MR_EMIT` is device-stripped and `fw_main`'s sink is otherwise trace-gated, so an unprefixed report would be **invisible on metal under `debug off`** — a "no silent loss" claim that was already false once in this arc.

★★ **THE RING'S BOUND IS AIRTIME, NOT RAM, AND THAT IS WHY IT IS 4 AND WHY IT REFUSES.** Every armed intent is one H flood already on the air with a second queued behind it, so four concurrent unresolved by-id questions is already an aggressive load on this plane; the ring is a de-facto rate limit rather than a memory budget. A full ring **refuses loud** (`CmdCode::err_resolve_pending_full`, appended — the exhaustive `ord()` walker enrolled it automatically, which is the S1c instrument working) and **never evicts**: evict-oldest would silently kill a request the operator was told had been accepted, which is the entire class this arc exists to remove. Re-issuing the same `(id, plane)` **refreshes** rather than consuming a second slot (`park_resolve_request`'s precedent). ⚠ **U1-checked and `err_ack_ring_full` deliberately not reused for the third time in this arc** — same shape, different ring, wrong remedy.

| env | ABI / profile | RAM before → after | ΔRAM | ΔFlash |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `xiao_sx1262` | ARM, full | 166980 → 167044 | **+64** | +1344 |
| `gateway` | ARM, `MR_FEAT_TEAM 0` | 191900 → 191964 | **+64** | +1328 |
| `xiao_mobile` | ARM, `MR_FEAT_REMOTE_MGMT 0` | 166500 → 166564 | **+64** | +2256 |

★ **+64 on every cell, none disagreeing** — the placement holds on both real ABIs, where native's 8-byte alignment would have hidden a shift. ⓘ The ring is **not** feature-gated on purpose — the by-id `reqpubkey` on the STATIC plane is the bench defect this whole arc opened with (spec §0) — so both `MR_FEAT_TEAM 0` gateways pay the 64 bytes too, and that uniformity is what the grid confirms rather than assumes. ⚠ ΔFlash carries the ±16 B build-noise band S4a measured; `xiao_mobile`'s +2256 is the outlier and is codegen (that profile inlines the reqpubkey arm differently), not extra state — its ΔRAM is the same +64 as everywhere else.
⚠ **AND AN INSTRUMENT CORRECTION I NEARLY REPORTED WRONG:** the `sizeof(Node)` `static_assert` is inside `#ifdef MESHROUTE_NATIVE`, so **no board build can ever trip it**. My first attempt at a per-target reveal set it to `== 1` and read six clean builds as "the env did not compile" — the opposite of the truth. The per-target proof has to be the **linked RAM figure**, which is what the grid above is.

⚠ **THREE RESIDUALS, REGISTERED RATHER THAN SILENTLY ACCEPTED (B56 / B57 / B58 in the register).**
· **A stage-2 failure never reaches the APP.** `reqpubkey_sent` is written only on the synchronous BLE command path (`src/fw_main.cpp`); stage 2 fires from an RX callback with no command in scope, so its failures reach the operator console and telemetry only. Success is app-visible via the existing `peer_key_cached` push. Closing the gap needs a **new PushKind** — an app-contract decision, and the same documentation pass that owes B52/B55.
· **A BEACON that resolves the id does not complete the workflow.** Only an H answer consumes the intent; a beacon-learned binding leaves it to time out (after which a re-issued `reqpubkey <id>` resolves immediately). The hook would sit on the hottest corpus path in the tree and re-anchor every stream, for the case the by-id query exists precisely because it does *not* cover (routable but never heard).
· **The two bounded rings cannot both be exercised by one command**, because the intent ring refuses *before* the airtime — which is the correct order and is recorded because it is also a test-design constraint: the LBT-drop fixture has to fill the defer ring through the **by-hash** door.

★ **TWO TEST-DESIGN TRAPS, BOTH FOUND BY WRITING THE TEST THE WRONG WAY FIRST AND BOTH LEFT DOCUMENTED IN-FIXTURE.** A positive control that re-uses an id whose binding an earlier probe already ingested **passes while proving nothing**: `peer_book_by_id` now resolves it (B53's claimed floor), so the command takes the ONE-stage by-hash arm and the two-stage path is never exercised. Both affected controls now use a fresh id and say why. ⇒ **on a fixture that mutates a shared table, a "same command, now it works" control must not reuse the key the negative arm just poisoned.**
ⓘ **Instrument note for the next agent:** `pio run -e native` **overwrites `.pio/build/native/program` with the non-test application binary**, which then prints a usage line and no doctest summary. If the count is missing, re-run `pio test -e native` before blaming the tests.

★★★★★★ **THE HEADLINE, AND IT CONTRADICTS BOTH THE SPEC AND THE DISPATCH: SPEC §6 PREDICTED "re-anchors" AND THE BRIEF SAID "expect movement and attribute it". NEITHER HAPPENED — 36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL — AND THE INTERESTING PART IS *WHY*, WHICH IS NOT "the corpus cannot reach it".** Instrumented at the two new call sites: the team ingest fires **26 times** across **five** scenarios — s24 6 · s25 6 · s26 8 · s28 4 · s34 2 — split **13 destination (`h_query`) / 13 relay (`h_relay`)**, and **every single one arrives on an AUTHORITATIVE frame type**, i.e. every one is a case where the static plane writes `authoritative` and the team plane deliberately writes `claimed`. Instrumenting `team_key_set` under them: **13 REFUSED by S3's D5c① rule** (a claim landing on a first-hand beacon row), **6 NEW claimed rows inserted**, **7 claimed→claimed refreshes**. ⇒ **the input class is produced, the rows are stored, and the streams do not move because every reader is either behind the default `authoritative` floor or lives in `src/` (outside the sim build).** ★★ **That is CONTAINMENT, measured — and it is the strongest thing a 0/N can ever be made to say.**

★★★★★ **PROVEN IN TWO ARMS, BECAUSE "the floor contains it" IS A CLAIM UNTIL YOU REMOVE THE FLOOR.** **P-FLOOR** — S4a live, both team floors (`team_key_of_id`, `team_id_of_key`) made to ignore `min`: **3/36 move**, s24 `6e92f8fd`→`32ccbd77` (1574→**1408**), s25 `00813e38`→`2709d217` (792→**638**), s26 `110269a7`→`6483613e` (1045→**854**). ⓘ The counts *FALL*, which is the honest reading: a cache that answers is a locate that does not re-flood. **P-FLOOR-ISO** — the identical poison with the two `team_key_set` ingest arms reverted: **all 36 streams byte-identical to CLEAN.** ⇒ the entire P-FLOOR delta **is** S4a's producer and nothing else, and S3's floor is what stands between it and the live team paths.

⚠⚠ **AND THE DISPATCH'S OTHER HALF WAS RIGHT ABOUT THE FLOOR AND WRONG ABOUT THE SLICE.** It said S4a "inherits a WORKING corpus gate, not 'native only'" (from S3's beacon-stamps-claimed probe moving 5/36). **True of the INGEST — false of the ORIGINATOR.** The simulator console has no by-id `reqpubkey` form at all (`NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp` parses `reqpubkey <hex>` and hard-sets `dst_id = 0`), so **no by-id H frame can exist in the corpus**. ⇒ ★ **name the LAYER, not the slice: S4a's receiver half is corpus-gated, its transmitter half is native-only, and one sentence cannot cover both.**

**THE POISON MATRIX (6 probes; restore control = `lus` md5 back to `ca18b8fd`, native binary back to `528095ff`):**

★★★★ **THE SWEEP SET WAS WRONG AGAIN, AND ONLY A BOARD BUILD CAUGHT IT — the arc's sweep-scope meta-bug, next instance.** I derived the rename's blast radius from the TYPE NAME (`grep -rn 'h_in\b\|h_out\b' lib/ src/ test/`) and it returned 7 files. **The right set is every `pack_h(`/`parse_h(` CALLER**, and it contains an eighth: **`lib/core/frame_trace.h:136`**, which reads `h->key_hash32` off an `auto` — so the type name never appears in it. That file is `#if defined(ARDUINO)`, i.e. **compiled by neither the native suite nor the simulator**: native was green, `lus` was green, 36/36 was green, and the tree did not build for any board. ⇒ ★★ **derive a sweep from the OPERATION (who calls the function) and not from the SPELLING (who names the type)** — and D1's "every board env" is not ceremony, it is the only gate that reaches this file. Fixed in place: the trace now prints `BY_ID id=114` instead of `hash=72`, which is the same honesty the `query_key32` rename buys in memory.

★★★ **THE `query_key32` RENAME IS THE POINT, NOT TIDYING (spec §4).** Bytes 2-5 now hold a hash **or** a zero-extended id, and `f_in.ttl_or_next_hop`'s precedent licenses overloaded *wire* storage — it does not license a struct field called `key_hash32` that holds an id. The accessors `query_hash()` / `query_id()` each **answer 0 in the wrong mode**, and that is a fail-safe rather than politeness: 0 is the "no hash" / "unprovisioned id" sentinel everywhere in this codebase, so a mis-read can never name a real target. Pinned both directions in `test_frame_codec.cpp`.
★★ **THE CANONICAL GATE IS ONE PREDICATE SHARED BY THREE SITES** (`h_by_id_key_canonical`, frame_codec.h): pack refuses and parse rejects ids 0 / 255 and any non-zero byte 3-5, and `emit_hash_query` re-uses it for its degeneracy test. ⚠ **The three could not be allowed to drift** — a receiver that "cleans up" a non-canonical spelling re-opens the dedup aliasing the bit exists to close. ⓘ **The corpus validates BEHAVIOUR and never FORMAT** (one `lus` drives both ends of every link), so every canonical assertion is a unit test, by necessity and not by omission.

★★ **§7-O6 IMPLEMENTED AS RULED, AND THE RECEIVE SIDE IS TESTED BY TAKING THE BIT AWAY BY HAND.** Pack SETS `HARD` under `BY_ID` (consistency — D3 already makes it owner-only, which is *stronger* than HARD); parse must NOT require it, so the test hand-clears bit 0 on a packed frame — a combination `pack_h` can no longer emit — and asserts the owner still answers. `hard` stays in the dedup key untouched.
★ **AND ONE COMBINATION IS REFUSED OUTRIGHT, BEYOND WHAT THE SPEC ASKED: `BY_ID | WANT_PUBKEY`.** Spec §5 says the one-round form (hash *and* pubkey together) is *"not designed here"* — and it is not merely undesigned, it is **actively wrong**: the pubkey answer's receiver (`on_hash_bind_pubkey`) writes **no id binding at all**, so the combination would spend airtime and answer the question asked with silence. `pack_h` returns 0 and `parse_h` returns nullopt, both with the drop-either-flag control asserted. ⓘ A judgment call, flagged as one.

★★★ **TWO DEFECTS THE SPEC'S OWN D9 WOULD HAVE SHIPPED, both found by writing the tests rather than by reading:**
· **an explicit `-t` on a node with `team_id == 0` would have AIRED A STATIC BY-ID QUERY WEARING THE OPERATOR'S `-t`.** `emit_hash_query` can only stamp `team_scoped` when it has a team_id, so the frame packs as a plain by-id query and the answer comes back on the wrong plane. D9 never says a selected plane must EXIST. ⇒ a `plane_usable` guard keeps S1's `err_no_binding` for exactly that case (C2: refuse, do not answer a different question), and the operator hint now names the real remedy.
· **`emit_hash_query`'s no-return-route guard tested `want_pubkey` ONLY**, and a by-id query carries `want_pubkey = false` — so an **off-grid mobile**'s `reqpubkey <id> -s` would have flooded an H whose origin is its unroutable LOCAL id. **That is B47's class arriving through a brand-new door**, four rounds after B47 was closed. Widened to `(want_pubkey || by_id)`; the refusal is `err_no_gateway` with a same-node TEAM control that does fly.
ⓘ **D9 bullet 4 ("unresolved id on a dual-plane node") is finally implementable and implemented** — S1 recorded it as having *"no distinguishable outcome"* and deferred it here, correctly. With nothing resolved the plane comes off CONFIGURATION: `team_id != 0` ⇒ team-capable, `!is_mobile || mobile_registered()` ⇒ static-capable (the exact mirror of the guard above, U1), both ⇒ `err_ambiguous_plane` **with no airtime spent**.

★★ **THE TEAM PLANE LANDS `claimed` EVEN FOR AN OWNER'S AUTHORITATIVE BY-HASH ANSWER — a DELIBERATE ASYMMETRY vs the static line, and the reason is `on_hash_bind_snoop`'s reason (2), the ONE refusal reason S3 left live.** `_team_keys` feeds the team-DAD L2a mediation comparator (`node_beacon.cpp` compares `team_key_of_id(b.src)` against a beacon's own key and sends a mediated DENY on a mismatch), and that comparator reads at the DEFAULT `authoritative` floor. Writing `authoritative` from ANY on-air source would let transit-derived rows manufacture spurious DENYs against legitimate teammates. The static plane has no analogue of that comparator, which is why *its* tiering can follow the frame TYPE and this cannot. ⇒ **the heard beacon remains the only writer of an authoritative team row**, exactly as before, and spec §3-D5b's flat `claimed` is right for a reason the spec did not spell out. **The corpus exercises this 26 times.**

★★★★★★ **THE ONE MEASUREMENT THAT MATTERS MOST: THE CORPUS IS *NOT* BLIND TO THIS SLICE, AND THE BRIEF SAID IT WAS.** The dispatch inherited S2b's finding — *"the corpus produces ZERO claimed bindings in 304 885 samples ⇒ the 36-scenario corpus is structurally blind to the tier you are building"* — and instructed me to report a 0/36 as meaningless. **That is true of the `claimed` TIER and FALSE of the FLOOR that governs it.** ⇒ **P-CAP, the capability probe**: make the one producer (the heard beacon, `node_beacon.cpp:839`) stamp `claimed` instead of first-hand. **5 of 36 scenarios move — s22 · s29 · s30 · s34 · s37 — and s30 goes from 0 to FIVE assertion failures** (`team_dad_mediated` never fires, `team_dad_adopted` 1 of an expected 2, two DM pushes lost). ⇒ the beacon site is live, corpus-reachable, and the floor is genuinely enforced on the live team paths. ⇒ **P-CAP-ISO, the isolation**: the same poison **plus** both team floors disabled returns **all 36 streams byte-identical to CLEAN**. ⇒ ★★ **the entire P-CAP delta IS the floor and nothing else in S3 is corpus-visible** — and S4a, which finally writes a claimed row for real, therefore **inherits a working corpus gate rather than the "native only" one it was promised.** Budget it that way.

⚠⚠ **AND MY OWN SWEEP INSTRUMENT WAS VACUOUS UNTIL THAT POISON EXPOSED IT — the most transferable line here.** My harness counted assertion failures by grepping the ndjson for `"ok":false`. **`lus` never writes assertion results into the ndjson at all**; it prints `FAIL expect[...]` lines and a `lus: N assertion failure(s)` summary to **stdout**. So my "0 assertion failures in 36/36" was a string that could never have matched, on either arm — a *green light wired to nothing*. It was caught only because P-CAP made s30 fail and the counter still read 0. Re-derived from the saved runner logs: **BEFORE 0 · AFTER 0 · P-CAP 5**. ⇒ ★★ **before reporting any probe, poison the INSTRUMENT too, not only the code — "it stayed green" and "it cannot go red" are indistinguishable until you make something fail on purpose.**

★★★★★ **THE SPEC NAMED THREE READERS AND THERE ARE FOUR — and the fourth is the one the VIEW actually reads.** §3-D1 lists `key_hash_of_id` / `team_key_of_id` / `team_id_of_key`, and derives the sweep from prose. The mechanical sweep (`grep -rn 'key_hash_of_id\|team_key_of_id\|team_id_of_key\|_team_keys'` over `lib/ src/ test/`) returns a fourth `_team_keys` reader the prose set does not contain: **`Node::team_id_of_key_freshest` (`node_hashlocate.cpp:474`, private)** — the FRESHEST-wins alias resolver that fills `PeerBookRow::team_id` on the join path and in walk pass (2). ⇒ **`team_authoritative` was UNFILLABLE from the spec's own reader set**, i.e. D6's *"display a claim LABELLED as a claim"* could not have been implemented as briefed. It gains a `conf_out` and, deliberately, **NO floor**: its only callers are the display, whose D6 floor is `claimed`. ★ It also does **not** re-rank by trust — freshest still wins and the tier is merely reported, because putting a trust decision inside a display resolver is exactly the §AB3 de-dup mistake that cost this arc a review round (S1b/QA-P2).

★★★★ **"DEFAULT THE FLOOR EVERYWHERE AND S3 IS INERT" IS TRUE AT EVERY CALL SITE BUT ONE, AND THAT ONE WOULD HAVE PLANTED A BUG IN S4a.** `peer_book_walk` pass (4) (`node_hashlocate.cpp:609`) uses `team_key_of_id` as a **DEDUP** against what pass (3) emitted — and pass (3) resolves through the floor-less `team_id_of_key_freshest`. At the `authoritative` default a CLAIMED row would answer *"absent"* to pass (4) while pass (3) had already emitted it with its hash, so **`peers all` would print that teammate TWICE** the moment S4a lands. It now passes `IdBindConf::claimed` explicitly. ⓘ Inert today (no claimed producer), and it is the identical trap §id-hash S2 recorded for pass (2b): ★ **the dedup must read the TABLE, not a FILTERING accessor** — reached here through a brand-new door, the floor parameter itself.

★★★ **THE D5c OPEN QUESTION — "can ONE `last_seen_ms` serve both meanings?" — ANSWERED YES, NO SECOND FIELD, and the reasoning is the deliverable.** The stamp is read for the 48 h freshness gate in `team_key_of_id` AND for the eviction order in `team_key_set`. One field suffices because the two D5c rules remove the only conflict: (a) a claim can no longer touch an authoritative row's stamp, so inside the authoritative cohort it still means *"last FIRST-HAND evidence"*; (b) a claimed row's stamp is the age of the claim, which is simultaneously the right LRU key and the right TTL input for it; (c) the two cohorts are never LRU-compared, because the claimed one is drained first. A second `uint64_t` would have cost 8 B × 16 slots × `MR_N_LAYERS`.
★★ **AND THE SIZE PREMISE HELD FOR ONCE — MEASURED BY `offsetof`, NOT INFERRED.** `sizeof(TeamKey)` **16 → 16**: `offsetof(source)==1`, `offsetof(confidence)==2`, `key_hash32` still 4, `last_seen_ms` still 8 — both bytes land in the 3-byte hole that already sat between `id` and the 4-aligned hash, opening none. `sizeof(Node)` **221024 unchanged** and **ΔRAM +0 on all three boards**, so the placement holds on the board ABIs too, not just native alignment. ★ The asserts are **not vacuous**: `sizeof == 17` and `offsetof(source) == 2` each fail the build (P-N1/P-N2).

⚠ **A RESIDUAL IN D5c's EVICTION RULE, flagged with its number rather than silently widened (register B54).** The spec says eviction must *"prefer a claimed victim over any authoritative row"*, and it does — but on a table of **16 first-hand rows and no claim yet**, the FIRST claimed insert still evicts the oldest beacon row (the fallback). Every claim after that consumes only the previous claim, so a query storm costs **exactly one** beacon row, re-learned on that teammate's next beacon. Pinned by test both ways. Refusing the first insert instead is a stricter policy the spec did not ask for; it belongs to S4a/the owner, not to a slice whose contract is inertness.

⚠ **THE JSON BOOK IS NOW PLANE-ASYMMETRIC AND THAT IS A REGISTERED FINDING, NOT AN OVERSIGHT (B52).** `write_peer_row` gains `"team_auth"` beside `team_id` — always together, never emitted-only-when-false, because an absent marker reads as *authoritative* to every already-shipped consumer, i.e. a claim silently promoted at the app boundary. But the JSON has **never carried the STATIC plane's tier**: `static_authoritative` exists on the row and is rendered by the TEXT console only, so `static_id` still reaches the app unlabelled — and the static plane **does** produce claimed rows today (a relayed soft H answer). Adding `static_auth` is a second contract change and a separate slice (C1). ⓘ **`ios-companion/INBOX_SYNC_CONTRACT.md` is QA-owned and was NOT edited: the `team_auth` line is OWED there.**

⚠ **`peer_book_by_id` DELIBERATELY KEEPS THE `authoritative` DEFAULT ON BOTH ARMS, and the reason is scope, not omission.** Spec §3-D6 puts the `hashof`/`peers`/`reqpubkey` floor at `claimed`, but lowering it **would not be inert**: claimed STATIC rows exist in the live tree today, so `hashof <id>` would begin answering for them and — since S1 — `reqpubkey <id>` would begin spending **AIRTIME** on them. That is a behaviour change and S3's contract is inertness (spec §6's own gate row: *"by construction (defaults)"*). ⇒ **OWED TO S4a**, one argument per arm, and it must move on **both** arms together or the resolver rebuilds spec §1-C's plane asymmetry. `actual` is already read and propagated into the row rather than hardcoded, so the display cannot start lying the moment the floor moves. A test pins the current boundary so S4a's change is a visible diff.

ⓘ **THREE `file:line`s IN THE DISPATCH WERE STALE OR WRONG (V2 — re-check before relying).** The `team_key_set` beacon call is **`node_beacon.cpp:839`**, not `:831` (831 is the DAD tiebreak's `winner` line). The `on_hash_bind_snoop` header is at **`:1390-1410`**, not `:1240`. And the model for D5c's no-liveness-refresh rule is **`id_bind_set` (S2b, `node_hashlocate.cpp:153-181`)**, NOT `peer_key_set` as briefed: `peer_key_set` (`:332-343`) skips the refresh only for a **PINNED** row and otherwise refreshes `last_seen_ms` *before* the upgrade test — the opposite of what D5c needs.

★ **NO TELEMETRY WAS ADDED, and that is a decision with a reason.** S3 ships **no producer** of a claimed team binding — the heard beacon is the only writer of `_team_keys` and it writes `authoritative`, so an emit on the refusal paths could not fire at all. The slice that creates the producer (S4a) is the one that can judge whether a refused claim is worth an event. Stated in-source next to each refusal so the silence is not read as an oversight.
ⓘ **`on_hash_bind_snoop`'s three-reason refusal header was UPDATED IN PLACE (V1), not left to rot:** reason (1) *"`_team_keys` has NO confidence dimension"* is now ✅ **answered and built**, reason (3) *"cache-on-pass would fake liveness and evict beacon rows"* is ✅ **structurally neutralised** by D5c, and reason (2) — the team-DAD L2a mediation comparator — is **⚠ STILL LIVE and now load-bearing alone**, which is why `node_beacon.cpp:829` keeps the default `authoritative` floor. Its `✖ MISSING` line is rewritten to name exactly what S3 did **not** build: the ingest (§3-D5b / S4a).

**★★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-08-01 §id-hash S2b (`§id-hash S2b` — register **B46**: `id_bind_set` is UPGRADE-ONLY **on both axes**, so neither a same-row claim nor a claimed REHOME can displace a first-hand binding; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — UNCOMMITTED):** the matching-row write is gated on `authoritative || !existing_auth`, **and** a claimed observation may no longer evict an authoritative holder of the same hash under another id.
