★★★★ **IT SHIPPED HALF-FIXED FIRST, AND THAT IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE LESSON HERE.** The first version guarded the three writes on the matching row and was closed as done. **An independent implementation assessment re-opened it the same day (P1a):** `id_bind_set` also enforces the REVERSE rule — one hash to one node_id — through `id_bind_evict_other_hash_holders`, and **both accept paths called it without looking at confidence**. So authoritative `{id=10,H}` followed by a relayed claimed `{id=20,H}` took the NEW-node_id path, **evicted the authoritative row**, and inserted the claim: the identical demotion class, through a different door. ⇒ ★ **a "guard the write" fix is not done until every OTHER path that mutates the same invariant is checked** — here the invariant had two enforcers and only one was guarded.
★ **The fix REFUSES the whole write** (named `addr_rehome_refused`) rather than inserting-without-evicting: skipping the eviction would leave two rows for one hash and break the bijection `id_bind_find_by_hash` depends on — strictly worse than the bug. Same policy and telemetry family as the same-id conflict arm 20 lines above (U3). **Owner ruling: claimed → claimed stays NEWEST-WINS** (no trust ordering between two claims; keeping it makes this a fix, not a redesign — C1). An **authoritative** rehome still evicts and is held as the positive control; an **expired** authoritative holder does not block, because it is already invisible to every reader.

★★★★★ **THE BYTE-IDENTITY IS A PROPERTY OF THE CORPUS, AND THE RE-PROBE PUT A NUMBER ON *EACH DOOR SEPARATELY*.** Both poisons alone move **0/36** (same-row demotion; cross-id claimed rehome) — useless on their own, because the clean corpus produces **zero** `claimed` bindings (5 444 NEW-row inserts, all authoritative: 4559 `bcn` · 858 `self` · 14 `h_relay` · 13 `h_query`). So a **capability probe** was built: force the corpus's own `h_relay`/`h_query` learns to land `claimed` — the exact input class the rule protects against. That alone moves **3/36**, and *under* it, instrumented across **304 885** `id_bind_set` calls:
· **same-row demotion — 15 occurrences**, so the guard genuinely executes; yet reverting it under the same poison still moves **0/36**, because the corpus re-asserts those rows from a first-hand beacon **296 397 times** and the demotion window closes before any reader looks. **A measured masking mechanism, not an absent site.**
· **cross-id claimed rehome — 0 of 304 885**, i.e. **structurally unreachable even with claimed bindings forced**, because no corpus soft answer ever brings a hash in under a SECOND id. ⇒ ★★ **that is exactly why the original poison matrix could not have caught P1a, and it is the honest statement to inherit: this door has NO corpus gate at all, only native.**
⇒ ★★ **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE REST OF THE ARC, budget it now: S3 and S4a are ABOUT the `claimed` tier, and the corpus cannot produce that tier.** They will be byte-identical for equally uninteresting reasons unless a scenario is authored that emits a relayed soft H answer. Do not expect the corpus to gate them.

★★ **A `claimed` SIGHTING ALSO MUST NOT REFRESH `last_seen_ms`, and the consequence is intended rather than tolerated.** On an authoritative row that stamp means *"when we last had FIRST-HAND evidence"*, and it is load-bearing twice (the TTL gate in `key_hash_of_id`/`id_bind_find_by_hash`, and the `id_bind_age_out` sweep) — refreshing it on hearsay would fake exactly the liveness `node_hashlocate.cpp` names as the hazard for `_team_keys`. ⇒ **an authoritative row that only ever gets re-CLAIMED now ages out at 48 h instead of living forever on relayed claims.** Pinned with a same-timing CONTROL (the identical sequence with an *authoritative* re-sighting does extend the lease), because otherwise "it expired" proves nothing about why. `source` is frozen with them for a third reason: keeping `authoritative` while writing `source = h_relay` would label a relay as the authority — the provenance mislabelling §3-D8 exists to refuse.

⚠ **AMENDMENT TO THE SPEC, requested: §6's S2b row says "+ `IdBindSource::manual`" and it was DELIBERATELY NOT ADDED.** Its only producer is `confirmid`, which is S5. An enumerator with no writer is precisely the `PeerKeyConf::overheard` smell the spec itself criticises in §2.4 — shipping a second instance of it inside the slice that cites the first would be self-refuting. It lands with its writer or not at all. *(The assessment's P3 independently agrees the slice row is the stale part, not the decision.)*
ⓘ **One named emit was added (`addr_rehome_refused`) and it is corpus-inert by construction** — it fires only on a `claimed` write, and the corpus produces none; verified by the 0/36 above. The matching-row guard still emits nothing, for the original reason: a refused demotion is ROUTINE, and an emit there would re-anchor streams and make the behaviour change unattributable in the same run (C4 applied to telemetry).
**Non-vacuity:** restoring the pre-S2b unconditional write reddens **2 cases / 5 assertions**; removing the rehome guard reddens **2 cases / 12**. The conflict arms (authoritative rebind wins, claimed conflict refuses) and the self-defence gate are asserted untouched as controls.

**★★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-08-01 §id-hash S2 (`§id-hash S2` — register **B44 + B45**: `peers all` gains the missing STATIC pass and stops listing US; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — UNCOMMITTED):** `peer_book_walk` gains pass **(2b)**, an `_rt` sweep that is the exact twin of the team pass (4) it has lacked since §AB3, and pass (2) gains a self-skip. ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 unmoved; 0 movers of 36.** Boards 3/3 **ΔRAM +0**, warning sets identical, `-Wswitch` 0. *(Accepted unchanged by the 2026-08-01 implementation assessment.)*

★★★ **THE 0/36 IS STRUCTURAL, AND THE PROOF IS A GREP BEFORE IT IS A PROBE: `peer_book_walk` has exactly ONE caller in the tree — `src/firmware_commands.cpp` — and `src/` is not in the sim build at all.** So no probe *inside* the walk could ever move a scenario. The honest control is therefore a same-**FILE** liveness probe: poisoning `key_hash_of_id` (same TU, corpus-live) to return false moves **19/36**. ⇒ `node_hashlocate.cpp` is emphatically compiled, linked and executed by the simulator; the walk's invisibility is about its call graph, not about deadness. Coverage lives in native, where it belongs.

★★ **THE SELF-SKIP PREDICATE IS THE SELF-BINDING, NOT THE ID — and getting that wrong would have destroyed the most diagnostic row this dump can carry.** The skip is `node_id == _node_id && key_hash32 == _key_hash32`, which is `id_bind_set`'s own self-defence test verbatim (U1, one spelling of "this row is us"). `node_id == _node_id` alone — the obvious reading of *"skip self"* — would ALSO hide a **foreign key claiming our id**, i.e. an address collision, the exact condition `addr_conflict_self_defended` exists to surface.
★★ **AND THE DEDUP MUST READ THE TABLE, NOT THE ACCESSOR.** Pass (2b) tests `_id_bind` **membership** directly, because pass (2) emits a row for EVERY `_id_bind` entry while `key_hash_of_id` filters out `claimed` rows and hash-0 rows — deduping through the accessor would print those ids **twice**. A test pins exactly that (a `claimed` binding for id 48 ⇒ still one row, with `key_hash_of_id(48)` asserted false in the same breath so the trap is visible). This is the walk's own stated idiom — *"every later pass can ask the earlier pass's TABLE directly"* — not a new one.
★ **A RENDERER LIE, found by building the row and reading the output shape:** `peers_text_row` printed `(auth)`/`(claimed)` for every `static_id`, so an id-only row would have read `static_id=48(claimed)` — **a claim nobody made**. The suffix now prints only when the row carries a hash to be authoritative *about*; a bare `static_id=48` reads correctly as *"we route to it, we cannot name it"*. This also cleans up pass (2)'s hash-0 rows.
**Non-vacuity:** removing the self-skip reddens **3 cases / 7 assertions**; disabling the `_rt` pass reddens **2 cases / 14**. The JSON book is asserted UNCHANGED with ten live routes present (`include_id_rows=false` returns the `_peer_keys` count), which is the §2.6(a) bound the BLE transport depends on.

**★★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-08-02 §id-hash S1/S1b/S1c/S1d + §tx-admission TX1/TX2/TX3 (register **B42 + B47 + B48 + B49 + B50 + B51**: `reqpubkey <id>` resolves on BOTH planes at BOTH sites, and it no longer claims floods that did not happen; **6 ATTRIBUTED RE-ANCHORS, all §TX3** — UNCOMMITTED):** the arm resolved through `team_key_of_id` alone, so on a static node the verb **could not succeed for any id**. It now goes through `Node::peer_book_by_id`; `-s` exists, `-s`/`-t` are exclusive, a bare id ships as plane 0/AUTO and `on_command` picks per §3-D9. Native for the whole arc **1094/71859/0 → 1120/72234/0** (+26 cases, +375 assertions). ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 unmoved; 0 assertion failures in all 36.** ⚠ **NOT byte-identical overall — §TX3 re-anchors SIX scenarios** (listed below with their per-scenario attribution); S1..TX2 were 36/36 byte-identical. ⚠⚠ **TX3 RE-ANCHORS SIX SCENARIOS — the first change in this arc that moves WHEN a beacon side effect fires — and every one is attributed:** s15 `266d32e2`→**`3dae1b82`**/52488 · s15_metal `f5c6f2b1`→**`f4ef243e`**/52386 · s17 `f930899d`→**`b56ab583`**/1181343 · s28 `0116cafd`→**`dd96c809`**/4018 · s29 `a9987eaa`→**`2e81833c`**/1943 · sim_9node_base `94b6ad0a`→**`04dc4ca0`**/4959. `lus` `907c8524` → **`da02e17f`** (3 warnings = BASELINE's 3). Boards 3/3 **ΔRAM +48** (gateway / xiao_sx1262 / xiao_esp32s3 — ALL of it §TX3's `DeferredLbt::digest_ids`, and the board figure EQUALS the native `sizeof(Node)` delta exactly, so the `offsetof` measurement holds on both ABIs; S1..TX2 were ΔRAM +0); cumulative ΔFlash for the arc **+3472 / +4128 / +3240**; warning sets **byte-identical to pre-arc** ×3, `-Wswitch` 0. ⚠ **TX1 is a SEPARATE COMMIT** — it changes a function every TX caller uses, so its attribution must not be folded into the reqpubkey work (C4). `sizeof(CmdResult)` **16 → 20** — a return temporary, never a `Node` member, so it costs no RAM; `sizeof(Node)` **220976 → 221024 (+48, §TX3)** — the assert in `node.h` carries the derivation.

★★★★★★ **AND THE SEMANTICS WERE THE REAL BUG — THREE ROUNDS WERE SPENT CHASING AN UNSATISFIABLE CLAIM.** S1b closed four pre-transmit bails; S1c found a fifth in the LBT defer ring; TX1 found a sixth in `tx_with_retry`, which **discarded `_hal.tx`'s `TxResult`** while `DeviceHal::tx` answers `busy` on a full 8-entry ring, bumps `txq_drops` and **does not retain the frame**. Each round the same shape, one layer deeper. ⇒ ★★ **OWNER RULING (2026-08-01) that ended it: `reqpubkey_sent` means "the TX path ACCEPTED the frame — nothing rejected it", NOT a claim of airtime.** The stronger wording is **unsatisfiable synchronously** — a deferred frame reaches the radio when a timer fires, long after `on_command` returned — so no amount of propagation could ever have made it true. `CmdResult::aired` is renamed **`accepted`** and says so. ⇒ **the durable lesson is not "follow the value down"; it is CHECK THAT THE CONTRACT IS SATISFIABLE BEFORE CHASING IT.** Two of the three rounds were spent making a false statement true one layer at a time.

★★★★★★ **TX3 — THE OWNER REVERSED THE +64 B CALL, AND THE RE-ANCHOR IS THE PROOF THE BOUNDARY MOVED.** TX2 committed the channel digest when a beacon ENTERED the LBT ring. **Owner ruling 2026-08-02: for channel-digest accounting "sent" means ACCEPTED BY THE TRANSMITTER/DeviceHal** — the strongest boundary this architecture can observe, and explicitly **not** literal RF airtime. ⇒ the ids now ride the deferred slot and the commit happens when the timer reaches `_hal.tx` and it answers ok; a ring-full drop or a HAL rejection leaves **both** the `bcn_ad_count` and the dirty flag untouched. ★ **Why the earlier reasoning was incomplete and the owner's is right:** the `reqpubkey_sent` ruling settled an **app event**; digest retirement is an **independently load-bearing state machine**, so extending one to the other was a design decision, not an implementation detail. ⚠ **WHAT THE BOUNDARY IS NOT:** a later `pump_tx` radio-start error drops the frame AFTER admission and is **outside** the guarantee — said in every comment that states the rule.
★★ **THE COST CAME IN UNDER THE ESTIMATE, MEASURED BY `offsetof` NOT INFERRED: +48 B, not the briefed +64.** `sizeof(DeferredLbt)` **164 → 176 (+12/slot × 4)**; `offsetof(digest_ids) == 12` and `offsetof(buf)` 12 → 24, i.e. the array lands in the 4-aligned run before `buf` and opens **no new hole**. `sizeof(Node)` **220976 → 221024**. The 16 bytes were bought back by dropping the count byte: **`digest_ids[0] == 0` terminates**, because a live channel id can never be 0 (`channel_msg_id_mint` packs `origin >= 1` into the high byte) so `channel_buffer_find(0)` always misses.
★★★★ **THE RE-ANCHOR, ATTRIBUTED PER SCENARIO AND MECHANICALLY — 3824 beacon defers corpus-wide is the exposure.** s28 is the cleanest reading and settles the mechanism for all six: **the ENTIRE delta is TWO lines — one `channel_dirty_cleared` moving from `t=820265` to `t=820380`**, same node, same id, same `channel_id`, same `ad_count:1`, same `reason:"seen"`. **115 ms later = exactly the LBT defer delay.** Per scenario: s15_metal **32/32 lines are `channel_dirty_cleared`**, s28 **2/2**, s29 **2/2**, sim_9node_base **6/6** — pure retiming, event counts identical. s15 **29/33** and s17 **50/104** carry a SECOND-ORDER tail (`beacon_rx`, `link_bidi_confirm`, `link_census_complete`), which is the expected consequence and not a separate change: an entry that stays dirty ~115 ms longer is **re-advertised in the next beacon**, so that beacon's digest content differs and its receivers' lines shift with it. s15 is +1 event = one extra advertisement round. ★ **s18 keystone UNMOVED** (no channel digests), and every static-only scenario is byte-identical.
⚠ **A CONSEQUENCE OF TX3 THAT COST B51 ITS OBSERVABLE, and it is worth knowing:** once ring entry commits nothing, **a ring-full drop and an accepted defer have IDENTICAL digest outcomes**, so the digest can no longer discriminate `tx_flood`'s ring-full return — poisoning it reddened nothing. The surviving discriminator is `beacon_tx.result` (0 admitted / 2 dropped), now asserted, and with it the step-6 poison reddens **1 case / 2 assertions**. ⇒ **fixing one layer can silently remove the observable another layer's test depended on.**
**Non-vacuity (TX3):** committing regardless of the deferred result reddens **1 case / 4 assertions**; restoring commit-at-ring-entry reddens **1 / 5**; the B51 ring-full poison reddens **1 / 2**.

★★★★★★ **TX2 — THE SWEEP ITSELF WAS SCOPED WRONG, AND THAT IS THE LESSON, NOT THE BUG.** TX1's dispatch said *"enumerate every `tx_with_retry` caller"*. **Wrong set.** `tx_flood` does not go through `tx_with_retry` at all — it calls `_hal.tx` **directly** — so the sweep could not have found it, and the register's claim that `tx_with_retry` is *"a function every TX path goes through"* was **false** and is corrected in place. The right set is **every direct `_hal.tx` caller**, and there are **exactly FOUR**: `rts_duty_defer_fire` (OK — the CTS-wait timeout it arms is the recovery), `tx_flood` (**broken**), `tx_with_retry` (TX1), `retry_stashed` (OK — retry-eligible, MAC timeout recovers). ⇒ **one real defect beyond TX1, and it was the one the wrong sweep structurally could not reach.** Next instance of the arc's sweep-scope meta-bug, after directory-vs-file, verb-prefix, predicate-vs-pattern and transport scope.
★★★★ **AND IT WAS NOT TELEMETRY — IT BURNED CHANNEL STATE.** `emit_beacon` takes `tx_flood`'s boolean as `sent` and gates `commit_channel_digest_advertised` on it, under the comment *"burn an ad_count … ONLY for advertisements that ACTUALLY AIRED … the air-honesty fix"*. That commit does `++e.bcn_ad_count` and on horizon `e.dirty = false`. ⇒ **a beacon dropped by a full LBT ring or refused by the radio queue consumed the advertisement horizon and could retire a digest nothing ever received.** The air-honesty mechanism was defeated by two discarded returns. Fixed at **ZERO bytes**: both sites return the real result and the commit follows the **acceptance** boundary — the same one the owner ruled for `reqpubkey_sent`.
⚠ **RESIDUAL, FLAGGED NOT DECIDED:** an accepted defer commits at acceptance, so a beacon that dies later at the radio queue has already burned one count. Rolling back needs the digest ids carried in the defer ring — **MEASURED +16 B/slot × 4 = +64 B of `sizeof(Node)`** — against a residual of **one** count of a horizon *backstop* (primary retire is holder coverage) in a sub-second window. Owner ruling, with the number.
⚠⚠ **COVERAGE IS PARTIAL AND THE POISON SAYS SO.** Immediate HAL rejection: tested with a paired positive control, poison reddens 1 case. **Ring-full beacon: fixed but UNTESTED** — my fixture measured `DEFER=0` (the beacons were *skipped*, `wait > flood_lbt_max_defer_ms`, not deferred), the ring never filled, and poisoning that site reddens **nothing**. I removed the fixture rather than ship a green test that proves nothing. **OWED: a fixture that asserts `tx_lbt_defer` fired before filling the ring.**

★★★ **P2 — MY OWN "NO SILENT LOSS" CLAIM WAS FALSE ON METAL, AND QA CAUGHT IT.** I wrote that `_hal.log` is *"the one operator-facing channel `lib/core` has on metal"*. It is not — `fw_main`'s sink was `if (g_mr_trace_on) mrcon.println(m)`, so under normal `debug off` the deferred-loss report was **completely silent on hardware**. `_hal.log` is a DEBUG channel and I described it as an operator channel. ⇒ operator-critical `lib/core` messages now carry a `!!` marker and the sink prints those **regardless of trace**; the report is genuinely unconditional. ⓘ A prefix rather than a new `Hal` method: `log()` is the only text channel `lib/core` has, and widening the interface would force every implementation (device, sim, three test HALs) to grow a method for one message. Other equally-critical existing `_hal.log` sites ("beacon pack failed", "team M-frame with no team_id") are deliberately NOT re-marked (C1) — the mechanism is there for them.
ⓘ **The `-Wmisleading-indentation` warning at `node_hashlocate.cpp` PREDATES this arc** — count is **1 at `ea4be0c`** (before "id to hash part 1") and **1 now**; it originates in `546bb46` and sits in the mobile-reg name loop, untouched here. It is already inside the accepted pre-arc board warning baseline, which is why every A/B in this arc reads "identical".

★★★★★ **TX1's OBVIOUS FIX WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A REGRESSION, and the enumeration the dispatch asked for is what caught it.** The instruction supposed `tx_with_retry` might be all-discard, in which case TX1 would be inert for its callers. **It is neither `void` nor all-discard:** it already returned `bool`, and **THREE callers read it** — `duty_defer_fire` and both `do_data_tx` arms — each deciding *"arm the post-TX state?"*. Their existing `false` means **"not sent, but a re-send timer IS armed"**. Folding a HAL rejection into that same `false` would have suppressed `start_ack_timeout()` on a dropped DATA and left the flight with **no recovery at all** — for a retry-eligible frame the MAC timeout IS the recovery. ⇒ the return is **three-way** (`handed` / `deferred_retry_armed` / `rejected`) and the readers branch on `!= deferred_retry_armed`, which is **bit-for-bit their old `true`** — pinned by two `static_assert`s beside them, so a future fourth enumerator on the wrong side is a build failure rather than a silent DATA regression.

★★★★ **AND THE HARDWARE LAYER WAS UNREACHABLE BY EVERY AUTOMATED GATE — which is why it survived three reviews.** This repo's test HAL returned a hard `ok`; the simulator's `FirmwareNode::simTx` pushes onto an **UNBOUNDED vector** and can only answer `too_long` at len > 255, which no packer can produce. ⇒ a **scriptable `TxResult` was a prerequisite, not a nicety**. Clean corpus: **0** `tx_hal_rejected`, **0** `tx_deferred_lost` — structurally, and now stated with the mechanism. **Capability probe** (force the HAL to reject every flood): **7123** rejections reached, and reverting the propagation under that same poison returns **all 36 streams byte-identical to clean**, so the poison is otherwise inert and every delta is the fix. The behavioural isolation is s22's `"OK reqpubkey queued"` → `"OK reqpubkey error"`.
★★ **THE DEFERRED RESIDUAL, and the mechanism was a choice worth recording.** A frame ACCEPTED into the defer ring can still meet a full HAL queue when its timer fires, and unlike DATA an H query has **no MAC timeout** to recover it — so without a report the operator waits forever on an answer that can never come. Now `tx_deferred_lost` **plus an operator-critical `!!`-marked `_hal.log`** — ⚠ see the P2 paragraph below: my first version of this claim was FALSE on metal (the sink was trace-gated) and the marker is what repaired it. ⚠ **A bounded re-defer was REFUSED**: it would air an H query at an unbounded later time, after the operator has been told and plausibly retried by hand — duplicate airtime for a stale question — and it needs retry state plus a second timer path for a case no gate can reach. **Reporting the death is the honest product; re-sending a stale query is not.** A per-command push was refused too: correlating it needs a handle the frame does not carry, and `send_failed{ctr:0}` is precisely the uncorrelated shape B39 exists to fix (owed to B39, C1).
⚠ **`err_tx_ring_full` → `err_tx_queue_full`, one day old and renamed anyway:** **two** bounded queues can reject this command, so a name — or an operator hint — fingering one of them is a wrong diagnosis half the time. Same reasoning that kept it out of `err_ack_ring_full`, applied to over-narrow naming. Free under M3.
**Non-vacuity (the new arms):** discarding the `TxResult` again reddens **2 cases / 9 assertions**; `lbt_complete` swallowing it **2 / 7**; removing the deferred-loss report **1 / 2**. Both HAL fixtures pair their rejection with a same-fixture `TxResult::ok` flight.

★★★★ **THE BRIEF AND THE SPEC WERE BOTH WRONG ABOUT THE CORPUS, IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS, AND ONLY THE MEASUREMENT SETTLED IT.** Spec §6 says *"⚠ verify the corpus issues no `reqpubkey`, don't assume"*. **It DOES** — `s22_mobile_team` runs `reqpubkey 38e8b9f4 -t`. But the sim's console has **no by-id form at all**: `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp` parses `reqpubkey <hex>` and hard-sets `dst_id = 0`. ⇒ the arm is **executed** by the corpus and the **by-id sub-branch is unreachable in it**. Probe **P-A** (make the by-id branch refuse) → **0/36**. Same-arm control **P-B** (`hard=true`→`false` at the flood) → **1/36 = s22**. Strictest control **P-C** (`dst_hash ^ 1` — the statement IMMEDIATELY above the change, unconditional, no intervening branch) → **1/36 = s22, 1804 → 1730 events, 3 assertion failures**. ⇒ P-A's 0/36 means *"the corpus cannot construct the input"*, provably not *"dead code"*.

★★★★ **S1b — THE VERB WAS STILL LYING ABOUT SENDING, IN FOUR WAYS RATHER THAN THE ONE I REGISTERED.** I recorded only the off-grid-mobile case (B47); the implementation assessment (P1c) found `emit_hash_query` is `void` with **four** silent early-outs — degenerate/self, **no crypto identity**, no return route, and a `pack_h` codec failure — and `on_command` answered `queued` through all of them, which BLE rendered as `{"ev":"reqpubkey_sent"}`, the contract's *"the request was flooded"*. ★ **The no-identity case also disproved my own source comment and the contract line claiming that path "keeps its existing error ack" — there was no such ack.** ⇒ the originator now returns `Node::HQueryOutcome{sent, degenerate, no_identity, no_return_route, encode_failed}` and the arm maps it: `err_unsupported` / **`err_no_identity`** (new code, per §err-reason/B32 — its remedy is specific) / `err_no_gateway` / `err_too_large`, each still echoing `dst_hash` + `plane`.
★ **The "preferred" fix did NOT become refactor-plus-fix (C1), and that was worth checking before choosing it: widening `void` → the enum changed ZERO call sites**, because all four other callers already discarded the absent return and there is no `[[nodiscard]]`. The diff is the function's own returns plus one reader.
★★ **`CmdResult::aired` is NOT redundant with `code == queued`, and one case is the whole reason it exists:** the **hosted-mobile local cache hit** is a genuine success that airs nothing — it reports through its own `peer_key_cached` push — so `reqpubkey_sent` is keyed on `aired`, not on acceptance. It costs **zero bytes** (existing tail pad; `sizeof(CmdResult)` stays 20).
★★★★★ **S1c — AND S1b WAS STILL ONE LAYER SHORT. A FIFTH BAIL POINT, BELOW THE FOUR IT CLOSED.** `tx_initiating` was itself `void` and **discarded** `schedule_lbt_defer`'s `bool`, which is `false` when the 4-slot LBT defer ring is full — its own comment says *"ring full -> drop loudly"*. ⇒ frame **dropped** → `emit_hash_query` still returned `sent` → `aired = true` → **`reqpubkey_sent`**. Identical false-success class, one call deeper, breaching spec §5.1's *"must not be reachable from any bail point"*. ⇒ `tx_initiating` returns `bool`; `HQueryOutcome::tx_dropped` maps to a new **`err_tx_ring_full`**. ⓘ **U1 checked and `err_ack_ring_full` (9) deliberately NOT reused:** same shape, different ring, and the shipped contract documents it as the pending-E2E-ack ring — reusing it would hand the app a wrong diagnosis and a wrong remedy. ⇒ ★★ **the durable lesson: "propagate the failure" is not done at the first `void` you fix — follow the value all the way to the metal.** Two rounds of review, two layers, the same defect shape each time.
★ **SCOPE, and it needed an explicit ruling to stay narrow: a SUCCESSFUL defer is still `aired`.** A deferred frame is scheduled and will fly, so the contract's claim holds for it; only the ring-full DROP is false. Widening this to *"aired means already on air"* would make every deferred send report a failure — a different and wrong semantic. `lbt_complete`'s own early returns are excluded on inspection, not by omission: both are RTS-only (a stale-flight CANCEL of a superseded flight, and a duty DEFER that does fly), and neither is reachable from `emit_hash_query`, which always passes `LbtKind::flood`.
ⓘ **The widening changed ZERO call sites AGAIN — verified the same way as `emit_hash_query`'s:** 22 callers across 8 files, all discarding, no `[[nodiscard]]`; `git diff` shows the six files holding 18 of them were not touched at all. U3: `tx_flood` in the same file has answered exactly this question with a `bool` since R4.5.
⚠ **The `h_tx` emit deliberately STAYS BEFORE the hand-off.** Moving it after the call would reorder it against `tx_lbt_defer`/`tx_lbt_defer_dropped` and re-anchor streams — a telemetry re-anchor folded into a behaviour fix is what C4/C1 forbid. The drop is reported by the next event and by the outcome.

★★★★ **THE DROP-PATH COVERAGE, and the three probes say three different things — which is the point.** ① The clean corpus hits `tx_lbt_defer` **7377 times** and `tx_lbt_defer_dropped` **0 times**: the defer path is heavily exercised, the ring simply never saturates. ② **Capability probe** (make the ring permanently full): **4058 drops, 32/36 movers** — yet the pre-fix and post-fix arms are **byte-identical to each other**, because the corpus's single `reqpubkey` never coincides with a busy channel, so it never takes the defer path at all. ③ **Isolating probe** (force the reqpubkey arm down `tx_dropped`, frame still flying): **1/36 = s22, event count unchanged at 1804, and the sole delta is `"OK reqpubkey queued"` → `"OK reqpubkey error"`.** ⇒ **the corpus can OBSERVE the branch but cannot REACH it.** A bare 0/36 would have said none of that. Native is the gate: a ring-full fixture asserts the `CmdResult`, the `aired` bit and the BLE-visible disposition, with an idle-channel flight before it and a recovery flight after it (the recovery is what makes "transient" a tested property rather than a claim).

⚠⚠ **AND A SECOND INSTRUMENT WAS SILENTLY BLIND — register B49.** The `CmdCode` self-labelling loop (the only detector for §err-reason/B32's *"every refusal token begins with `err_`"*, which `src/fw_main.cpp` relies on to print the token BARE) was bounded by the LITERAL `v < 10`. It had stopped covering enumerators 10/11/12 the moment the enum grew — **while its own comment claimed it "cannot go stale unnoticed" because `-Wswitch` guards the walker.** True of the sibling walker; false here, because `-Wswitch` cannot reach a literal. ⇒ the bound now DERIVES from `ord()`, so listing an enumerator in the guarded walker enrolls it automatically. **Bumping the literal was rejected — it fails again identically at 13**, and a `_count` sentinel was rejected too (it would put a non-value enumerator into every `switch` and tax the instrument that works to fix the one that does not). **Demonstrated, not argued:** mis-naming enumerator 12 is caught by the derived bound and is **invisible (0 failures)** with `v < 10` restored.

★ **Tests now assert the BLE-VISIBLE DISPOSITION, not `h_tx` absence.** A test-local `ble_claims_sent(r)` mirrors fw_main's predicate verbatim; every negative branch is paired with a same-fixture successful flight. ⚠ **The old no-identity test discarded the `CmdResult` entirely — that is precisely how the false success got through**, and it is fixed (that fixture now provisions an identity and asserts `aired`, with the no-identity path given its own test).

★★★ **THE SECOND SITE IS NOT FIXED BY CALLING THE RESOLVER TWICE — IT IS FIXED BY DELETING THE SECOND LOOKUP.** The brief said to *"route the BLE echo through the same resolver"*; doing that literally leaves two independent resolutions that can drift, which is how this defect survived §AB3's fix in the first place. Instead `CmdResult` CARRIES the answer — `dst_hash` = the hash the query actually flew for, `plane` = which plane resolved it — and `fw_main`'s hand-rolled `team_key_of_id` call is **gone**, not repointed. ⓘ It also lights up the USB console for free: the existing generic ack site already prints `dh=0x…`, so `reqpubkey 186` now answers `> queued ctr=0 depth=0 dh=0x61CD83EA plane=static`.
★ **`plane` IS APPENDED LAST AND THAT IS DELIBERATELY SUB-OPTIMAL:** the 4-byte pad after `queue_depth` would have absorbed it for free, but **17 sites brace-initialise `CmdResult` POSITIONALLY**, so a middle insert means a 17-site mechanical sweep bundled into a behaviour fix (C1). Every serialiser **omits `plane` when 0**, so all three pre-S1 `write_ack` lines are byte-identical — pinned by a test, because that is the whole compatibility argument for appending a field to a shipped ack.

★★★ **A DISPLAY DE-DUP IN A SHARED RESOLVER WAS MAKING AN AIRTIME DECISION (assessment P2, register B48).** `peer_book_by_id` suppressed the TEAM presence bit whenever both planes resolved the **same hash** — a tidiness choice from §AB3, harmless while only `hashof` read it. S1 made that mask select a query plane, and there it silently picked STATIC instead of D9's ambiguity, and made an explicit `-t` return **`err_no_binding` for a team binding that exists**. My D9 test covered only DIFFERING hashes, so the exact-duplicate branch was untested. ⇒ the resolver now reports **presence per plane and nothing else**; hash equality never made the two planes' routes, return paths or flood scope equal, which is exactly what the flag selects. ⇒ ★★ **the durable rule: a shared resolver returns facts — a "tidy display" filter inside one will eventually be read as a decision.**

★★ **A SPEC ARM THAT CANNOT BE BUILT YET, reported rather than faked: §3-D9's fourth bullet — *"no flag, UNRESOLVED id on a dual-plane node → require a flag rather than guess"* — has NO distinguishable outcome in S1.** An unresolved by-id `reqpubkey` refuses (`err_no_binding`) **before** any plane is selected and before anything flies, so a flag would change nothing observable. Implemented as `plane = 0` = *"neither plane"*, which matches `hashof`'s own wording; a test asserts the refusal spends no airtime and names why the bullet is deferred. **It becomes live in S4a**, where an unresolved id does fly a by-id query.

Rewritten around the new contract; the hash form's planes are re-asserted unchanged as the residue.

Every fixture now installs an identity and every "no airtime" assertion sits beside a same-fixture flight. ⓘ The same trap, one layer up, is what P1c turned out to be in the firmware itself.

**★★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-08-01 B39 (`§b39-ctr0` — the `ctr == 0` sentinel is documented at the seam; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** with a **bit-identical `lus`** — QA GO; committed):** the minimum interim, per the entry's own two options — **comments plus one test, nothing returns anything different.** Native **1093/71851/0 → 1094/71859/0** (+8 assertions = **exactly** the new test). ★ **Boards ΔRAM 0 AND ΔFlash 0 EXACTLY on all three — not even the ±32 B `__DATE__` noise**, which is the right signature for a comments-only slice. `lus` **bit-identical** (`907c8524…`) after 4 TUs recompiled ⇒ the strong proof. s18 keystone unmoved.

★★★★ **THE ENTRY'S OWN PREMISE WAS TOO STRONG, AND THE THIRD PRODUCER IS A *SUCCESS*.** The register said *"`ctr == 0` IS the sentinel [for not sent]"* and named two producers. **There are three.** `node.cpp:1565-1573`: on a **registered mobile**, a plain or `-g` **GLOBAL** post takes `do_send_channel_delegated`, which returns **`true` after a real MOBILE_SEND DM actually flew** — but the **HOME** mints the channel ctr, so `ctr` is never assigned. ⇒ **a SUCCESSFUL delegated global post already answers `> queued ctr=0` on metal today**, indistinguishable from blocked or seal-failed, with **no channel-level push at all**. QA-verified in source. ⇒ ★★ **the sound reading is "this node minted no channel ctr", not "not sent":** `ctr != 0` ⇒ originated locally with that handle; `ctr == 0` ⇒ **no local handle exists, and whether anything flew is NOT answerable synchronously.** ⚠⚠ **AND IT CHANGES THE REAL FIX: a discriminated result that only splits accepted / blocked / refused would classify producer (3) — a success — as a FAILURE.** The missing piece is **a local handle for a remote mint.** ⇒ **a "minimum interim" that documents a seam is worth doing precisely because writing the contract down is what exposes the case the contract cannot express.**

★ **The sentinel now rests on a TESTED invariant, which was the weaker half.** `next_ctr` never yields 0 (all three exits ≥ 1; the wrap is `node_mac.cpp:23`). A direct pin was added anyway, and **poisoning the wrap to yield 0 reddens BOTH** (the new test 7 of 8, `§b40`'s 2) ⇒ the incidental cover was real, and the intent is now explicit. **An invariant load-bearing for a documented contract should be pinned by a test that SAYS so.**

ⓘ Also folded in so the note cannot be over-read: `-t -g` reports the **TEAM** copy only (`gctr` discarded), and `queued, 0` is **routine** elsewhere — `join`/`resolve`/`reqpubkey`/`peername` mint nothing, and the hash-addressed `send` arm's 0 can mean **parked behind an H resolve**, i.e. **sent later**.

★★ **THE A/B LANDED EXACTLY: s28's `send_channel_b` team half `false`@43.56 s → `true`@2.42 s, global half byte-identical.** All four s28 team posts collapsed from full-budget exhaustion to first confirm (**44.32→1.49 · 45.41→2.28 · 43.56→2.42 · 45.00→1.31 s**). ⓘ One correction to my framing: the global half is **not the same node** — a homed mobile delegates, so **S1 mints and reports it**. Same command, two nodes.

★★★★ **THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST, AND IT LIMITS THE FIX: ON A FULLY-1-HOP TEAM, `relayed` IS `false` BY CONSTRUCTION AT 100 % DELIVERY.** I predicted 4 movers; **only s28 and s34 moved, and s22/s29 are CORRECTLY unchanged.** In both, the only `flood_tx` of the post come from the **origin itself**: every member is a 1-hop neighbour, so the frugal `{self + hops-1}` seed marks them all covered and `flood_forward_decision` goes silent. **There is nothing to overhear** — and under the owner's "first relay only" ruling, `false` is the **truthful** answer. QA-reproduced: s22 **0 true / 1 false**, s29 **0 / 2**, with every teammate having received the post.
⇒ ★★ **`relayed` IS NOT A DELIVERY SIGNAL ON EITHER PLANE**, and for a **small co-located team — 3–10 members, the actual hiking use case — it will read `false` on every post.** ⚠⚠ **CONSEQUENCE FOR THE OLED EMERGENCY: B38 does NOT fix the distress-call display for such a team; it still shows NOT HEARD.** B38 removed a *false* negative on multi-hop teams; the 1-hop case is a **true** negative of a signal that was never measuring delivery. **This needs an owner decision, not a patch.**

★★★ **THE BRIEF'S FIX SHAPE WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A NEW BUG — a HOLDER slot reaches the confirm.** A `§F-CH-RELAY` holder is always `team`-flagged, so the blanket `if (rp.team) return;` was **silently absorbing it**. Remove the blanket without a holder guard and **every relay pushes `channel_sent{relayed:true}` for a message it never sent.** Measured, not theorised: poison-B produces **10 spurious pushes — 9 in s28, 1 in s34, all `ctr:0`**, the holder slot's giveaway. ⇒ **"delete the carve-out" was my instruction and it was incomplete; the carve-out was load-bearing for a second reason nobody had written down.**

★★ **AND `channel_reoffer_confirm` HAS TWO CALLERS, NOT ONE — the dominant one is SILENT.** `:446` (the DATA-M ingest) also emits `channel_msg_already_present`; **`:1589` in `handle_flood_rts`'s already-buffered branch emits nothing.** Corpus-wide the visible emit fires **4 times (all s17) and never at an originating node**, so **all 93 outcomes are driven by the silent RTS-M overhear.** The coder nearly mis-concluded *"the confirm is unreachable"* from the visible emit. ⇒ **an emit is not a proxy for a call site.**

★ **A THIRD FINDING: an existing native test was VACUOUS ON ITS OWN PREMISE.** *"a TEAM flood re-offers all its retries DESPITE overhearing a relay"* built `mk_flood_rts` (a **LEAF** flood) and drove it into an off-grid mobile, which `node_mac_rx.cpp:163` drops **before** `handle_flood_rts` ⇒ `channel_reoffer_confirm` was **never called** and the "DESPITE" clause tested nothing. It passed only because an unconfirmed team origin re-offers anyway. Fixed to `mk_team_flood_rts`; **found by the new push assertions**, i.e. by adding an assertion that could tell the difference.

**Coverage:** poison A (suppress the team report) → 2/36 and **all 36 byte-identical to the pre-slice baseline** ⇒ the emit-flip is the *entire* corpus delta. Poison C (differential `+7` on both ctr expressions) → **9/36 = exactly the `channel_sent`-carrying set** ⇒ the ctr site executes everywhere, while the B40 fix itself is corpus-**invisible** (every corpus ctr ≤ 3, so `rp.ctr == id & 0xff`) — an honest gap, covered by native at 255/256/257/65535→1 **plus 255 vs 511, which mint the identical on-wire msg-id** and must stay distinguishable. Native non-vacuity: the pre-slice body fails 2 cases / 7 assertions, and **255 and the wrap PASS under the old code** — itself a demonstration that the bug only bites above 255.

Their content is byte-identical to their snapshot — nothing was altered — but the code landed under the wrong message. **Do not `add -A` while a slice is in flight.**

The plane is the only variable.** ⇒ no confounder argument survives that.

★★★★ **SEVERITY AMPLIFICATION — B38 IS NOT A FUTURE-OLED PROBLEM, IT IS LIVE IN THE SHIPPED APP CONTRACT.** `ios-companion/INBOX_SYNC_CONTRACT.md:502` (QA-verified) states the app treats **`channel_sent{relayed:false}` as "stop-and-back-off (don't keep firing)"**. ⇒ **the shipped companion already backs off from EVERY SUCCESSFUL TEAM CHANNEL POST.** B38's entry rated it on the OLED emergency; **the blast radius is wider and already deployed in the app's logic.**

★ **Design notes worth keeping:** the arm mirrors `msg_recv` exactly, including its **always-emit** convention — an omit-when-false would have made *"not relayed"* and *"not a `channel_sent`"* the **same stream text**, which is the very gap being closed. The field name matches the shipped contract, so **no contract change is owed**. ⓘ A V1 fix in the same block: the header claimed *"insertion order kind,ctr,dst"*, but `nlohmann::json`'s default object is a `std::map`, so keys emit **alphabetically** — `relayed` lands last by **sort**, not by placement.

★★★★ **POISON P1 FOUND A HOLE THAT WAS INHERITED FROM CL2b, AND IT IS THE MOST TRANSFERABLE LESSON OF THE ARC: renumbering the wire codepoint `channel_inner_flag_source` 0x04 → 0x08 left the ENTIRE SUITE GREEN.** Every assertion named the flag **symbolically**, so the KAT was **self-referential about the one thing two independently-built nodes must agree on** — a node built before the renumber and one built after would have silently failed to interoperate, with 1091 tests passing on both. Fixed with numeric pins (`flag_text == 0x01`, `flag_location == 0x02`, `flag_source == 0x04`, `flags_known == 0x07`, the 6/4 widths, and a literal `inner[0] == 0x07` in the KAT). ⚠ **CL2b's flag values were equally unpinned and this closes them too.** ⇒ ★★ **RULE: a SYMBOLIC-ONLY assertion on a WIRE CONSTANT is not coverage. Pin the number.** A test that says `CHECK(x == kFlag)` proves the code is self-consistent; only `CHECK(kFlag == 0x04)` proves it matches the other end of the link.

★★ **A NOTE OF MINE CORRECTED: `lib/console` IS in the sim build.** My `§cl1` line said *"`lib/console` + `src/` are outside the sim build"* — right about `src/`, **wrong about `lib/console`**: `orchestrator/CMakeLists.txt:27` links `meshroute_console`. Measured cleanly: editing **only** `console_json.cpp` moved `lus`'s md5 while **all 36 streams stayed identical** (the sim never serialises a `Push`), whereas editing **only** `src/fw_main.cpp` left `lus` **unchanged**. ⇒ **the honest statement is per-file, not per-directory: `console_json.cpp` is compiled and linked (for `pushkind_name`) but its serialisers are never called; `console_parse.cpp` is not compiled at all; `src/` is outside entirely.**

★ **A behaviour change I am flagging because it reverses CL2b:** a **mismatching or zero** `source_hash` now **refuses the whole post** (no `channel_recv`, nothing inboxed, nothing retained — **still buffered and still relayed**), where CL2b pushed the position and declined only to *store* it. The coder's reasoning, which I accept: **both quantities are inside the AEAD envelope**, so a disagreement means the frame is **self-inconsistent** — forged or corrupt — not merely uncorroborated; and rendering the text while silently dropping the position would be **indistinguishable from a text-only post**, i.e. a silent downgrade (C2). ⇒ `peer_location_unattributed` is now **structurally unreachable and removed** (both its reasons died), with `count(...) == 0` tripwires left behind.

★★ **A DEDUP FORCED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT TIDINESS — and the number is the argument.** Adding the two naive `lat`/`lon` emits cost **+1744 B (gateway) / +1840 B (xiao_sx1262)**, attributed by ablation to `write_push` alone (6212 → 7844 B). Cause: `JsonBuf::i64` is a **hand-rolled 64-bit decimal loop with no out-of-line symbol**, so GCC inlines it at every call and on nRF52 `u64 /= 10` becomes a multiply-high sequence. Routing both callers through one `emit_lat_lon` cut it to **+416 / +480**. ⇒ **U1 was not a style preference here; duplicating two lines cost 1.3 KB of flash.**

★ **`bit2` WITHOUT `bit1`: accepted on the READ side, not originated on the SEND side — a deliberate split.** Forbidding the combination would put a **cross-bit condition into the reader's field-presence logic** (special-casing "bit2 set, bit1 clear" instead of reading each field per bit) — the enum thinking the flags byte exists to avoid — and would foreclose the obvious next feature (a text post that names its sender) behind a reader change. The send side originates `bit2` **iff** `bit1`, so a `-l`-less sealed post keeps its 173 B cap and its **exact CL2a/CL2b wire bytes**. ★ **One cross-bit rule in one place:** a `constexpr channel_inner_flags(with_text, with_location)` composer, so no send-side caller can forget it, and `channel_inner_overhead` now takes **the flags byte** rather than a bool **because with two optional fields a bool PAIR is swappable at a call site** and would silently mis-size the header. Sizes re-derived: **163 B** text with `-l` (174 − 1 − 4 − 6).

⚠ **One self-inflicted incident, caught by the coder's own guard:** their first poison runner restored from the **pre-slice** snapshot and wiped their own `protocol_constants.h` edits; a pattern-count guard on the next poison caught it immediately, and the native md5 was confirmed back to clean before continuing. ⇒ **the shared-scratchpad hazard's sibling: a snapshot taken at the wrong TIME rather than in the wrong PLACE.**

**★★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-08-01 CL2b (`§cl2b` — ★★ **`send_channel -t -l -e` WORKS: the owner's stated goal, a position inside a sealed team post**; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; committed). ⇒ THE CHANNEL-CRYPT ARC IS COMPLETE.** The sealed inner is now `[flags u8][loc 6 if bit1][text if bit0]` with `pack_loc6`; O6's refusal matrix is live; AB4's peer-book row lights up with `loc_src:"team"`. Native **1085/71490/0 → 1090/71712/0** (+5 cases, **+222 assertions**). ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 and all four `§b22` anchors unmoved; 0 movers of 36.** `sizeof(Node)` **220976 UNCHANGED**; boards **ΔRAM +0** ×3. `lus` → **`6af2929f`**. ★ **No `wire_version` bump: the inner lives entirely inside the AEAD ciphertext** — the flavor byte, the body length, the `[seal_ctr][seed8]` shape, the nonce and the AAD are all unchanged, so a non-keyholder cannot observe the difference.

★★ **THE FLAGS BYTE, and why it is not a third enumerator:** `bit0` text, `bit1` location, `flags == 0` **refused**. An enumerated space must spend a codepoint per **combination** — which is exactly how the DATA flags byte (`0xFF`) and `q_opcode` (2 bits) both reached exhaustion here. ★ **Unknown flag bits ⇒ the reader REFUSES, and that is structural rather than strict:** a future field sits *between* the flags byte and the variable-length text, so a v1 reader that does not know its width **cannot locate the text**. Guessing is not available; C2 says refuse. ★ **One `constexpr channel_inner_overhead(bool)` is shared by the size pre-flight, the assembly and the parse**, so the three cannot drift — and the inner exists **only inside the seal**, so a plaintext post's body is still the bare text and no existing post changed on the wire.

★★★ **THE BRIEF'S OPEN QUESTION RESOLVED BETTER THAN I EXPECTED — and the paranoia in the answer is the good part.** I asked which sender hash the AB4 hook could use, warning that the msg-id carries only 16 hash bits and that a wrong or truncated key would **corrupt the address book**. A full 32-bit hash **is** derivable: on a team post `origin` **is** the sender's `team_local_id`, and `_team_keys` maps exactly that to the teammate's full `key_hash32` (beacon-learned, same-team + 48 h TTL enforced — the same table `reqpubkey <id>` already trusts). ★★ **And the two are CROSS-CHECKED: the resolved hash's low 16 bits must equal the msg-id's hash bits.** That is a real test, not decoration — **a `team_local_id` is DAD'd and re-pickable, so a stale row can name the wrong peer**, while the id carries the *sender's own* bits. No resolution or no match ⇒ the position is still pushed but **never retained**, with `peer_location_unattributed{unknown_team_peer|hash_mismatch}`. ⇒ **the address book cannot be poisoned by a stale team-DAD row.**

★★★ **A FINDING THAT REACHES BACK TO CL3: "surface the position the way a located DM does" — A LOCATED DM SURFACES NOTHING.** `Push::has_location / lat_e7 / lon_e7` are set by `node_mac_rx.cpp:1196` and consumed by **nothing**: `write_push`'s `msg_recv` arm emits no coordinates, the console renderer prints none, and `record_dm` has no location field. **QA verified independently** — the single `has_location` hit in `console_json.cpp` is `write_peer_row`, i.e. **AB4's peers row, a different struct.** ⇒ **`send -l` (CL3) has been shipping a position that only becomes visible through the ADDRESS BOOK.** The coder mirrored the DM exactly rather than forking a richer channel surface — the right call — but it means **the per-message JSON carries no coordinates on EITHER plane.** ⇒ **register entry; if the app wants a position per message that is a contract addition on both planes and its own slice.**

And `-t -g -l -e` needed **no new branch** — `global_clear_copy` already owned it.

Native **1077/71355/0 → 1085/71490/0** (+8 cases, +135 assertions). ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 and all four `§b22` anchors unmoved; 0 movers in 36; `dm_delivery_breakdown` diff-identical.** `sizeof(Node)` **220968 → 220976 (+8)**; boards **ΔRAM +8 = exactly the sizeof delta** on all three; `lus` → **`63c3c20f`**. ★ **NO `wire_version` bump needed:** `channel_flavor_crypted = 0x40` is a flag bit in the **existing** M-frame flavor byte (alongside `channel_flavor_team = 0x80`), and the body mirrors `build_sealed_relay_body` byte-for-byte — both are existing wire fields.

★★★★ **MY NONCE INSTRUCTION WAS CRYPTOGRAPHICALLY VOID, AND THE CODER FIXED IT PROPERLY RATHER THAN OBEYING IT.** I wrote: *"bind the sender's hash into the AAD so two members' nonces cannot collide."* **That cannot work.** XChaCha20's keystream is `f(key, nonce)`; **AAD enters only the Poly1305 tag.** ⇒ AAD binding **cannot prevent keystream reuse** — it would have detected nothing and prevented nothing, while reading like a mitigation. ★ **What was built instead: `channel_msg_id` occupies the nonce's 32-bit slot** (`nonce = BLAKE2b(seed8 | seal_ctr LE2 | channel_msg_id LE4)[:24]`), and the msg-id is `origin<<24 | (sender key_hash32 & 0xffff)<<8 | ctr8` ⇒ **24 sender-distinguishing bits, differing per sender AND per post**, and in the clear so every reader derives it. ★★ **Proven by COUNTERFACTUAL, which is what makes it evidence:** a native test shows two members with the same key, the same forced seed and the same ctr get **different** nonces via the msg-id — **and that the settled shape (team-key hash in that slot) produces IDENTICAL nonces, i.e. textbook keystream reuse.** ⇒ **the instruction to "stop rather than ship a weaker nonce" was honoured by shipping a STRONGER one and proving the difference.** ★ **Durable: a shared-key design makes the NONCE the only place sender-distinguishing entropy can live. AAD authenticates; it does not separate keystreams.**

★★★ **MY `want_crypt` FORMULA WOULD HAVE CAUSED A REGRESSION the moment a node acquired a key.** I specified `(c.crypt == on) || (crypt_cfg && key_held)`. Measured consequence: a plain **GLOBAL** post from a keyholder → `want_crypt` true, `want_team` false → refused **`no_team`** (a keyholder could no longer post to a global channel **at all**); and `-t -g` → refused **`global_clear_copy`** (a working invocation starts failing **because a key arrived**). Both are rows the spec itself marks *"unchanged"*. Built correctly as `(c.crypt == on) || (want_team && !want_global && _cfg.team_channel_crypt && key_held)`. ⇒ **and my follow-on claim that "the refusal strings' scope widens" is therefore also false** — correctly scoped, both permanent refusals still fire for **explicit `-e` only**, and CL1's wording stands unchanged.

★★★ **THE CORPUS IS STRUCTURALLY BLIND TO THIS ENTIRE SLICE — measured, not assumed.** I briefed *"s28/s29 hold team keys ⇒ team-channel scenarios WILL move."* **False: no simulator node can hold a team content key at all.** Every establish path (`team_channel_key_mint`/`_adopt`/`_adopt_priv`/`_load`, and the sealed TYPE-19 grant) is reachable only from `src/` or the `team grantkey` console verb — **none of which the sim builds** — and the wrapper maps no team-key config key. ⇒ `team_channel_key_present()` is false in all 36, nothing seals, and **0 movers is the correct answer.** ★★ **Poison A is what turns that from a claim into a measurement: forcing `key_held` to succeed moved 4/36 — s22, s28, s29, s34 — which is EXACTLY CL1's poison-arm D prediction.** ⇒ the seal site **is** reached by the corpus; the only thing keeping the slice inert is the absent key. ⓘ **QA re-verified the blindness independently and had to inspect the hits to do it:** a grep for `tkpriv|tkpub|team_ch` returns 5 hits, **all false positives** — two `"emit_type": "team_channel_purged"` assertions, s28's own `_name`, and two comments. **Fourth instance this week of a substring grep matching prose; caught only by reading the hits.**

★★ **THE KEYING MODEL IS SYMMETRIC, AND THE DEPARTURE FROM T-K2's WORDING IS RIGHT.** `key = BLAKE2b-512("MR-TEAM-CH-v1" | team_ch_priv32)[:32]` — one key per team keypair, derived identically by every holder. **Not** "seal to `team_ch_pub`" as T-K2 §2.2 words it, because **every member already holds `team_ch_priv`** (T-K3 grants the private half; T-K4's QR carries it) ⇒ an ECDH adds **zero** confidentiality while making a post **unreadable to any keyholder lacking the SENDER's pubkey**, contradicting T-K2's own requirement in the same paragraph (*"Everyone holding team_ch_priv opens"*), costing an X25519 per cached peer per received post, and making the no-key signal ambiguous. ★ **The accepted bound is already ruled in-source:** `node.h:127-144` (AB4's `PeerLocSrc`) records that *"the team content key is SHARED, so sealing proves MEMBERSHIP, NOT IDENTITY"* — this is that ruling's implementation, not a unilateral deviation.

★ **Placement won the byte-identity for free:** the seal happens in `do_send_channel` **in place** into `ChannelEntry::payload`, so the buffer, the flood DATA-M, the cached re-flood body, the pull response and the digest all carry ciphertext verbatim — **not one line of flood/pull/digest code changed, and the relay plane stays content-blind BY CONSTRUCTION.** Open happens on delivery-to-self only, straight into `Push::body`; **the buffered entry stays sealed so a puller gets identical bytes** (pinned by a test: an un-keyed node still serves a `CHANNEL_PULL` byte-identically).
★ **A +174 B RAM regression was caught and removed in-slice:** the first pass used a function-`static` scratch for the opened plaintext (the `e2e_open_relay` idiom); `nm` attributed **ΔRAM +184/+176**, and opening straight into `Push::body` (241 B already on that frame) took it to **+8 = exactly `sizeof(Node)`**, and deleted a non-reentrancy caveat with it. Four warnings introduced in that pass were also found and fixed.

⚠ **TWO ITEMS FOR THE OWNER.** (1) **`team_channel_crypt` is LIVE-ONLY — no NV, no `kVersion` bump** (precedent: `team_hop_cap`, `nav_enabled`, `intra_layer_relay`). The coder's argument: the design value **is** the privacy-safe one, so a forgotten opt-out **heals itself on reboot**, and it keeps an unrequested reprovision out of the slice. **Overrulable — that would be device_nv v24 + a boot restore, its own slice.** (2) **No sealed `source_hash`** in the inner: unverifiable under a shared key (it would look like authentication and be none), +4 B/post, and the msg-id already carries 24 sender bits in the clear.

**★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-07-31 O3 (`§o3-key-lifetime` — the team channel key now lives exactly as long as its `team_id`; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; committed):** `set_team_id` cleared routes, the peer set, liveness, the **peer** key cache and the DAD id — but **not** the channel keypair, so once CL2a seals, a member that ran `team <other>` would **seal for its new team under the old team's key**. ★★★ **THE DISPROOF — MY INSTRUCTION WOULD HAVE BROKEN `team new` AND QR ONBOARDING, AND THE SEMANTIC SURVIVED IT.** I briefed *"clear unconditionally inside `set_team_id`"*. But **`handle_team` mints/adopts the key BEFORE calling `set_team_id`** — deliberately, per its own comment at `firmware_config.cpp:1011`: *"Placed BEFORE set_team_id so a refusal leaves the team UNJOINED rather than keyless."* ⇒ an unconditional clear **mints a key and destroys it three lines later**, persisting `present=0`, silently breaking the 2026-07-29 ruling that *"the creator always ends up holding a keypair"* — and the same for `team <id> tkpub=/tkpriv=`, the **T-K4 QR path**. Not hypothetical: `mint_form` draws a fresh random id, so `set_team_id` always returns true there. ★ **Fixed by stashing the private half in the already-present `tk_priv` frame buffer and re-adopting after the switch** (byte-exact — clamping is idempotent), chosen over moving the mint below the switch because a refusal after the switch would leave the node in the new team **live but unpersisted and un-DAD'd**. ⇒ ★★ **the OWNER'S RULING IS INTACT — "the key lives as long as its team_id" still holds, because `team new` mints the key FOR the team it is creating. What was wrong was my IMPLEMENTATION instruction, not the semantic** — and spec §3.1's "~3-line slice" understated it.

★ **NV mirroring turned out FREE BY CONSTRUCTION — no `kVersion` bump, and the reasoning is worth keeping.** `blob_take_team_channel_key` reads through the **accessors**, which return `nullptr` while keyless, so the call **already present** in `handle_team` after the switch persists `present=0` + zeros automatically. No new save, no field, no version change. ⇒ **an accessor-based serialiser makes "clear the live state" and "persist the cleared state" the same act.** ⓘ Correction to my brief: `kVersion` is **23**, not 22 — §loc-per-send bumped it after T-K1.

★ **The clear is NOT in `clear_team_routing_state()`, and that placement is load-bearing:** that helper is shared with `clear_routing_state()` (create/join/leave), which must **preserve** the key ⇒ putting it there would have destroyed the key on every reprovision. It sits in `set_team_id` between the routing clear and `purge_tx_carriers`; the same-id no-op is preserved by construction (`set_team_id` returns `false` at its first line, before any clear).

★★ **POISON DISCIPLINE WORTH COPYING: the coder DISCARDED two invalid instruments rather than reporting them as evidence.** A clear→mint poison gave 0/36 — but `rand_bytes` has **no downstream consumer** in these scenarios, so the probe could not have moved anything. ★★★★ **MY BRIEF CONTRADICTED THE SPEC ON A SECURITY-RELEVANT POINT, AND THE CODER WAS RIGHT TO OVERRIDE ME. This is the most important entry in this note.** I wrote *"No behaviour change: store it, and emit so it is observable."* Spec §2.7.2 says the opposite, and QA re-verified it at source: *"a plaintext `DATA_FLAG_LOCATION` is parsed and pushed **exactly as today** but **NOT retained** … **a spoofed position in an address book is worse than an absent one**, because the UI presents it as fact."* ⇒ **O6 settled "drop silently" vs "drop AND emit" — never "drop vs store"; its *"no behaviour change"* qualifies THE EMIT.** **Had the brief been followed literally, the address book would retain SPOOFABLE positions.** The coder read the spec, found the contradiction, built authenticated-only retention (`if (crypted_ok && sender_hash) peer_loc_set(...) else MR_EMIT("peer_location_unauth", …)`), and flagged it with the observation that *"a QA read of the brief alone would gate the wrong behaviour."* ★★ **Lessons, both durable: (1) when a brief and the spec disagree, the SPEC wins and the disagreement is the report's headline — a coder who silently followed the brief here would have shipped a security regression through a green gate. (2) A one-sentence paraphrase of a ruling is where rulings go to die: O6's ruling text and its RATIONALE sat two lines apart in the spec, and compressing them inverted the meaning.** The spec now pins the ruling explicitly so it cannot be re-briefed wrongly.

★★ **AND MY 256 B FIGURE WAS INVALIDATED BY MY OWN RESCOPE.** §2.7.1 specifies a 4-field record — measured, genuinely **16 B, no padding**. But the 2026-07-31 rescope (mine) **added `loc_src`**, and I kept quoting the pre-rescope size: `+ src` measures **20 B**, `offsetof(src) == 16`, ⇒ the ring is **320 B, not 256**. Both spec sites corrected. ⇒ ★ **when you add a field to a design in a rescope, the size claims elsewhere in that design become YOURS to re-derive** — nobody else will notice they were computed before your field existed.
★ **The 3 tail bytes are a NAMED `reserved[3]`** — AB1's lesson applied unprompted, because implicit padding is **indeterminate** after `PeerLoc{}`. Pinned by a `static_assert` on `sizeof` **and** `alignof` **and** `offsetof(src)` **in `node.h`** rather than a native CHECK, so it fires on **every board toolchain**, not just the host.

★★ **THE SIX-ENV LINK GRID WAS REPLACED BY A SIX-CELL COMPILE-ONLY PROBE — and calibrated, which is what makes it legitimate.** `+320` **uniformly on all six flag-sets** (`xiao_sx1262`/`xiao_esp32s3` 116728→117048 · `gateway`/`gateway_esp32s3` 147344→147664 · both `*_mobile` 116696→117016), **and its PRE column reproduces the ledger's existing `§loc-per-space` figures exactly** — that agreement is the calibration. ⇒ **the grid exists to find a per-target padding difference, and the probe MEASURED that there is none** (T-K1's precedent). The coder's call, taken after measuring, not granted in advance. ★ **And `_peer_loc_n` costs ZERO** — `_parked_sends_n` already had **7** bytes of pad before the 8-aligned `_l2c_redirect`; the hole shrinks to **6**. **Ninth application of the padding-placement rule**, ledger entry appended in the existing style.

★ **Three more of my premises corrected.** (1) **"`peer_book_join_ids` is the ONE place" holds only for the APP-FACING surface** — `peer_book_walk`'s passes (2)/(3) resolve ids straight off the row and never call it, so `peers all` would have shown positions on **keyed rows only**, and ★ **those unkeyed rows are precisely the shape CL2's channel source lands on** (O5). One helper, **three** call sites. (2) **`recent_ring.h` was refused as a forced fit (§C)** — its entry contract mandates `uint64_t t_ms` ⇒ **32 B/record vs 20 = +192 B of nRF52840 RAM to share ~10 lines of template**, and the *meaning* differs (a TTL suppression window with an age-out sweep, vs a retained value whose stamp is a **displayed age** that must never expire behind the app's back). (3) The ring is **Node-global, not per-`LayerRuntime`** — a deliberate divergence from its three hash-keyed neighbours, documented at the site: **a `key_hash32` is layer-independent**, so per-leaf copies would cost 2× to hold two answers to a one-answer question. Not `MR_FEAT_TEAM`-gated — the live source is a sealed DM, which a gateway receives like anyone.

★ **A detail worth copying: a BACKWARDS clock reports `0xFFFFFFFF` (maximally stale), never 0** — because **0 renders an unknown-vintage fix as CURRENT**, the exact failure the mandatory-age rule exists to prevent. `t_s = _hal.now()/1000`; uint32 seconds spans ~136 years of uptime ⇒ **wrap unreachable on a monotonic since-boot clock**, stated in-source rather than guarded.

⚠⚠ **THIS PATH IS PERMANENTLY CORPUS-DARK, AND THE REASON IS A SIM GAP, NOT A FIRMWARE ONE.** QA measured it before dispatch and the coder re-measured (V1): **`peer_location` / `has_location` / `lat_e7` = ZERO hits in all 36 NDJSONs** — the corpus never puts a location on the wire, because **the sim's `send` verb has no `-l`** (`dm_plane_from_tail` handles only `-t`, and the sim has no loc-intent field at all). ⇒ **the `if (loc_present)` block is never entered, every corpus poison probe on this code is VACUOUSLY 0/36, and native is the WHOLE detector.** ★ Unlike `§ab3`, **there is no site where the precondition holds by construction to inject at** — so the honest report is that the corpus contributes only an inertness proof. **Five NATIVE poison probes instead:** remove retention → **8** failed assertions · drop the `crypted_ok` gate → **4** · evict-newest → **2** · backwards-clock→0 → **1** · remove the view join → **12** across 3 cases.

**ΔFlash +2672 / +3136 / +516, attributed per symbol and NOT noise:** `node_hashlocate.o` +3508 of the ARM net, and it is **inlining duplication** — the three new bodies (`peer_loc_set` +636, `peer_loc_find` +366, `peer_book_join_loc` +368) plus GCC inlining the join into `peer_book_join_ids` (+370), `..._fill_from_peer_key` (+376), `peer_book_walk` (+830, two extra call sites) and `peer_book_by_id` (+696). ★ **A `noinline` on the join would reclaim ~2 KB — reported as an available lever, deliberately NOT taken (C1: a size micro-optimisation is its own concern).** Warning set **byte-identical** to baseline on all three envs; gateway `MR_FEAT_TEAM 0` clean.

★★ **WHAT CL2 MUST DO TO LIGHT UP THE CHANNEL ARM — and this is the payoff of building `loc_src` up front:** call the **same** `peer_loc_set(source_hash, lat, lon, PeerLocSrc::team)` from the sealed-channel-post receive path. **No schema change, no new field, no `PushKind`, no wire change.** The view join already covers **keyless** hashes (O5's shape) at `peer_book_walk` passes (2)/(3), and `write_peer_row`'s `"team"` golden **already exists** in `test_console_json.cpp`. CL2's own decision is the **authentication predicate** (T-K2's team-key-cache verification) — the retention side is done. ⇒ **the deferred half costs one call, because the discriminator was designed in before the second source existed.**

**★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-07-31 B29 (`§idbind-loop` — ★★★★ **A SHIPPED DEVICE HANG, FIXED IN ONE LINE**; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; uncommitted):** `Node::key_hash_for_id` looped `for (uint8_t i = 0; i < protocol::cap_id_bind; ++i)` with `cap_id_bind = 256`, so **`i < 256` was ALWAYS TRUE**, `++i` wrapped 255→0, and the trailing `return 0` was **unreachable**: on a miss it never returned. Fixed to `for (uint16_t i = 0; i < _active->_id_bind_n; ++i)` — the **verbatim idiom of its correct sibling `key_hash_of_id`** (U1). Native **1057/71046/0 → 1058/71061/0**. ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 unmoved in BOTH arms; 36/36 `cmp`-identical**; **`lus` md5 `15ec7b85…` IDENTICAL in both arms after 30 TUs recompiled** ⇒ **object-code proof the function is never emitted in the sim.** Boards 3/3 **ΔRAM 0**.

★★★★ **THE MOST INSTRUCTIVE RESULT IN THIS ARC: THE FIX COSTS FLASH (+16 / +32 / +16), AND THAT IS THE UB MADE VISIBLE.** My brief predicted *"≈0 or slightly negative — a bounded loop is not larger."* **Disproven, and the disproof is the point:** the loop was `const` and side-effect-free, so an infinite loop there is **undefined behaviour**, and **GCC was entitled to drop the termination test entirely** — the broken version compiled **smaller** because part of it had been optimised away. **The fix costs flash precisely because it restores a check the optimiser had licence to delete.**
★ **And the coder killed the noise hypothesis with an object-level control rather than arguing:** `firmware_remote.cpp.o` **11680 → 11688** (that TU carries **no** date stamp), `fw_main.cpp.o` 107136 → 107168, while `firmware_commands.cpp.o` — which **does** carry `__DATE__`/`__TIME__` — is **55028 → 55028**. ⇒ the delta is real codegen, not the ±32 B noise floor. ★★ **Generalisable: a UB loop can be CHEAPER than the correct one. "The fix should not add flash" is not a safe premise when the bug was undefined behaviour — and a ΔFlash that DISAGREES with intuition is evidence about the compiler, not noise to dismiss.**

★★ **A 3-ARM PROBE PROVED NEITHER HALF OF THE ONE-LINE FIX IS REDUNDANT** — exactly the discrimination the brief asked for, measured rather than asserted:
| arm | miss test | stale-tail test |
|---|---|---|
| `uint8_t i < cap_id_bind` (**as shipped**) | **HANGS** (killed at 45 s, exit 124) | FAILS — returns `0xBBBB2222` |
| `uint16_t i < cap_id_bind` | passes | **FAILS — same value** |
| **the fix:** `uint16_t i < _id_bind_n` | passes | passes |
⇒ **`uint16_t` alone pins TERMINATION; `_id_bind_n` alone pins the STALE-TAIL read.** Two defects, one line, two tests.

★★ **THE PUNCHLINE, and it is the durable lesson: A DEAD ERROR BRANCH IS EVIDENCE ABOUT ITS CALLEE.** `src/firmware_remote.cpp:196` reads `if (!th) { … "unknown id (no beacon heard from it yet)" … }` — **unreachable, because the callee could not report a miss.** Someone wrote a miss-handler for a function that could not miss. ⇒ **when a guard cannot fire, ask whether the CALLEE can produce the condition before concluding the guard is redundant.** The branch is now live and **linked** (the string is present in both the `xiao_sx1262` and `gateway` ELFs) — verified as a **board-compile + reasoning claim, stated as such**, since `src/` is compiled by neither detector.

★ **The restored assertion is the real proof.** `§ab3` had to write `⚠⚠ ADDING CHECK(key_hash_for_id(<unbound>) == 0) HANGS THE ENTIRE NATIVE SUITE` — because it did. **That assertion is now in, in two places, and the suite does not hang.** All five stale `✖✖`/`⚠⚠` markers were rewritten (V1), with one 16-minute reference kept deliberately in the **past tense**. The stale-tail test drives the **real rehome self-heal** path through the public API only — `id_bind_evict_other_hash_holders`'s update branch is the one eviction that `return`s **without refilling the freed slot**, so the evicted row stays byte-intact past the prefix.

⚠⚠ **DOC-LAG FOUND IN THIS FILE, AND QA CONFIRMED IT — the per-scenario md5 rows in HISTORICAL notes have drifted.** Verified live at this HEAD: **`s09_two_layer_gateway` = `f171652c`/2266**, while the 25j-era row documents **`92765cdb`**/2266 — **same event count, different md5** ⇒ a value-only change that was never written back. Likewise `s35a` reads **`585b9cc8`/2388** live vs the T7 note's `4508ec38`/2267, and the T7 note still cites `sizeof(Node)` **220592**, superseded by `§loc-per-send`'s **220648**. ★★ **RULE, so nobody chases a phantom mover: the AUTHORITATIVE anchor is the NEWEST note that names a scenario, and when in doubt a FRESH baseline run — never a per-scenario md5 quoted from a historical note.** The historical rows are an audit trail by design; they are not the gate.

★ **New register entry from this slice (found, not fixed — C1):** `key_hash_for_id` is still **neither authoritative-gated nor TTL-gated**, unlike the sibling whose loop idiom it now shares, so it can answer from a `claimed` or lapsed row. Residual is narrow (the hash only feeds `peer_key_find`, which ages independently, and `id_bind_set` maintains the id↔hash bijection) — recorded in-source as a **deliberate, scoped-out divergence** rather than left silent. ⇒ **B31.**

**★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-07-31 AB3 (`§ab3` — the GENERATED address-book view + `hashof`/`nameof` rewired; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; uncommitted):** the two-namespace defect the owner hit on metal is closed — `hashof <id>` read only `_id_bind` while `reqpubkey <id>` read `_team_keys`, so a just-cached team hash still answered `unknown`. Both verbs now resolve through **`peer_book_by_id`**, which calls **`key_hash_of_id` and `team_key_of_id` — literally the functions the send path uses** — so they cannot answer differently again. Native **1046/70918/0 → 1057/71046/0** (+11 cases, +128 assertions). ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 unmoved; 36/36 `cmp`-identical** (QA-reproduced). **ΔRAM 0 on all three boards**; `sizeof(Node)` unmoved, proven **three** ways incl. a **compile-only template reveal on the real ARM target (116728 → 116728)**. `lus` `c82f9c67…` → **`15ec7b85460e4ce5328b763325d7ecc1`**.

★★★★ **THE SLICE'S REAL YIELD IS A SHIPPED DEVICE HANG — see register B29.** `Node::key_hash_for_id` (`node.h:672`) loops `for (uint8_t i = 0; i < protocol::cap_id_bind; ++i)` where `cap_id_bind` is **256** ⇒ **`i < 256` is always true, `++i` wraps 255→0, and the trailing `return 0` is UNREACHABLE: on a miss it never returns.** Reproduced — a miss-case assertion spun the native suite **16 minutes** with no output. ⚠⚠ **And it is UB, not merely a hang:** the function is `const` and side-effect-free, so the compiler may hang, elide or return garbage, **differently per optimisation level and target** — untestable rather than broken. ★★ **The proof it was unintended is the NEXT LINE of its first caller** (`firmware_remote.cpp:196`): `if (!th) { … "unknown id (no beacon heard from it yet)" … }` — **dead code. A miss-handler for a function that cannot report a miss.** ⇒ **generalisable: a dead error-handler is EVIDENCE ABOUT ITS CALLEE. When a guard cannot fire, ask whether the callee can produce the condition — not whether the guard is redundant.**

★★ **MY BRIEFED "TRAP 1" NAMED THE WRONG TABLE, and being wrong found a second bug.** I warned that the hash→id reverse lookup could alias in **`_id_bind`**. Measured: it **cannot** — `id_bind_set` calls `id_bind_evict_other_hash_holders` on **both** accept paths **unconditionally**, so **one-hash-one-id is an enforced invariant** (all 7 callers plus the only direct removers go through it), and the coder pinned that non-aliasing as a test. ★ **The ambiguity is real on `_team_keys`:** `team_key_set` upserts **by id only** and never dedups by hash, so a teammate that re-runs team-DAD leaves its old `(id,hash)` row live for the full **48 h** TTL. ⇒ freshest-wins was built there — **and the same aliasing already bites the LIVE plaintext send-by-hash path at `team_id_of_key` (`node_routing.cpp:855`), which silently first-matches. ⇒ B30.**

★ **A third premise corrected, and it is a V1-on-comments case:** spec §1.3's *"`_id_bind` hash-0 id-only row"* is **not a live table state** — every `id_bind_set` caller passes a beacon/join/self hash, and because eviction keys on the hash, all hash-0 rows would collapse to **one**. The spec's evidence was `key_hash_for_id`'s doc comment, which describes its **return on a miss, not a row**. The genuinely network-reachable id-only flavour is the **`_team_peer`-bit-without-a-`_team_keys`-row** one. Supported via the test seam and marked as such in-source.

★ **Design worth keeping.** **Generated, zero-RAM, STREAMING** — one row on the stack at a time through a fn-ptr visitor, never an array, because `_id_bind`'s 256 rows × 48 B is **~12 KB** on the loop-task stack (the `do_post_ack` lesson, now applied pre-emptively). **Dedup on `hash` needs no "seen" array** — each later pass probes the earlier pass's *table* directly. ★ **No new `.cpp`**: the sim lists `lib/core` sources **explicitly**, so a new TU would have been **silently uncompiled by the simulator** — a trap worth remembering for every future core file. And **no `CmdKind`, no `Push` field, no `command.h` touch was needed at all** (`peers` is a local read-only dump like `routes`) ⇒ **both hard constraints I briefed were dodged by construction**, so `-Wswitch`'s `default:`-arm blindness never became a risk.

★★ **`peers all` is REFUSED over BLE** — `{"ev":"peers_err","reason":"console_only"}`, **intercepted before fw_main's text fallback**, which would otherwise have handed the full up-to-256-row dump to `dispatch()` and streamed it over **the transport that has wedged this node before** (the `mrcon` drop-never-block lesson). §2.6(a)'s bound is therefore enforced at the transport, not just documented.

★ **One judgement call flagged for review, and it is the right one:** an **aged** non-pinned `_peer_keys` row still contributes its name and ids (matching `peer_name_find`, which deliberately does not age-gate) but reports **`has_key=false` and `conf` downgraded to `overheard`**, because `peer_key_find` would refuse it and a seal would fail. ⇒ **reporting its stored `authoritative` would recreate spec §0.1 — the offer-then-fail UX this whole arc exists to remove.** JSON carries `"aged":true`.

**COVERAGE — the reachability injection is a NEW probe shape and it is the model for "0/36" from here on.** A 7-site combined poison moved **0 of 36** (⚠ with `lus` md5 changing, so the poison *was* built — nothing in `lib/core` calls the view; its callers are `src/` + `lib/console`). Then **three** controls turned that shrug into a measurement: a **same-site** poison of the one pre-existing function edited (`id_bind_find_by_hash`, const-widened) moved **3 of 36** ⇒ the edit stayed on a live path; a reachability injection at `id_bind_set` fired **1229 times**; and ★ **CONTROL A3b at `push_peer_key_cached` — where `_peer_keys` is non-empty BY CONSTRUCTION — fired 14× across 3 scenarios with the join working on REAL corpus data**: `team_id` **224/241** (s22) and **200** (s30) — the §2.5 hash→team-id join firing for real — `has_key` agreeing with `peer_key_find`, `alias_dropped=0`, and s27 correctly `team_id=0` on a teamless scenario. ⇒ **when a 0/N is genuine, INJECT AT A SITE WHERE THE PRECONDITION HOLDS BY CONSTRUCTION rather than arguing about reachability.**

**The §2.5 regression is the bench transcript verbatim** (team node 114, id 228), and ★ **its negative half is what makes it a test of the RIGHT fix**: after the answer caches, `key_hash_of_id(228)` is **still false**, `id_bind_find_by_hash(hash) == -1`, and the static arm is empty ⇒ **the forbidden `_id_bind` write (register B2) was not taken.** A test checking only that `hashof` answers would have passed **with the bug reinstated**.

⚠ **A MEASUREMENT ARTEFACT WORTH THE GATE METHOD: `Print.cpp.o` appeared to grow 660 B and had not changed.** **Two** `Print.cpp.o` exist (Arduino core + RadioLib), and `find | head -1` picked a different one per tree — because PlatformIO's `libXXX` directories are **CONTENT-hashed**, so **equal path LENGTH does not make paths correspond across trees.** ⇒ **key on the path below `libXXX`, never on find-order.** ΔFlash otherwise attributed on flash-bearing sections: `node_hashlocate` **+3104** ARM vs **+1944** gateway — ★ **the 1160 B gap IS the `#if MR_FEAT_TEAM` arms compiling out, measured rather than asserted.**
**DEVIATIONS accepted:** `id_bind_find_by_hash` gained `const` (one word, pure widening, proven still on its live path by the same-site control); `write_peer_name` gained two **additive omit-when-0** fields with the pre-AB3 shapes pinned byte-identical; `hashof` now answers for a **claimed** static binding, labelled `(claimed)` and never as a vouch — a deliberate widening in the direction of the slice's point (*"the node knows and will not say"*). **B29 and B30 left unfixed per C1**, both marked in-source.
