Native **1036/70785/0 → 1046/70918/0** (+10 cases, +133 assertions). ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 unmoved; 36/36 `cmp`-identical** (QA-reproduced). `sizeof(Node)` **220648** and `sizeof(Push)` **292** both unmoved. Boards 3/3 **ΔRAM 0**; ΔFlash +2240/+2256/+1008, attributed to **only the 6 TUs the coder touched — all console/verb surface, zero protocol code.** ★ **NEW `lus` ANCHOR: `c82f9c67608efbb14dc4c52f9a4308f8`** (was `3ee45562…`).

★★★ **MY EVENT COUNT WAS A 2× DOUBLE-COUNT, AND THE IRONY IS THE LESSON.** I briefed *"28 `peer_key_cached` MR_EMIT events — s22(8)/s27(12)/s30(8)"* to warn the coder that **two surfaces** share that name. Measured (QA-reproduced): **`"emit_type":"peer_key_cached"` = 14** (s22 4 / s27 6 / s30 4) and **`"kind":"peer_key_cached"` = 14** — the push lines. My `grep -c peer_key_cached` matched **both**, giving exactly 8/12/8 = 28. ⇒ ★ **When the whole point of a brief is that two surfaces must be DISTINGUISHED, the measurement must distinguish them too — count the DISCRIMINATING FIELD, never the shared substring.** The coder took the fork correctly anyway (JSON only, no MR_EMIT change ⇒ byte-identical, no attributed mover).

★★ **Two corrections that sharpen the corpus-visibility model — worth reusing:** (1) **`console_parse.cpp` is NOT compiled by the sim at all** — only `console_json.cpp` is (`CMakeLists.txt:137-138`). So the **parse** half of any console change is corpus-dark **by construction**, not merely unexecuted; my brief said "you cannot assume `lib/console` is corpus-dark", which is true of the file but not of the half. (2) ★ **The peer NAME reaches NO corpus-visible surface** — its readers are the push body (which the bridge strips) and `src/` (uncompiled) ⇒ **byte-identity does not verify the name path at all**, and the coder said so and made native its only detector rather than letting 36/36 imply coverage.

★★ **A GATE BLIND SPOT FOUND: `on_command`'s switch has a `default:` arm, so `-Wswitch` is STRUCTURALLY BLIND to a missing `CmdKind` case there.** Adding `peername` needed an explicit arm and **nothing would have warned** if it were omitted. ⇒ this is the [[meshroute-warnings-gate-blocking]] lesson one layer deeper: the earlier finding was *"an enum is protected only where it reaches a switch AS THE ENUM TYPE"*; here the switch **is** on the enum type and the guard is still defeated — **by a `default:` arm.** ★ **Recorded for AB3/AB4: a new `CmdKind` will NOT be caught by the compiler.**

★ **`Push` gained `peer_conf` at offset 11 — inside the alignment pad already sitting between `relayed`(10) and `ctr`(12)** ⇒ `sizeof(Push)` stays **292**, so `_push_ring[32]` and `sizeof(Node)` are untouched. **Eighth application of the padding-placement rule**, and now pinned by **per-ABI `static_assert`s on `sizeof(Push)` AND `offsetof`** (`command.h:222/225`) mirroring `device_nv.h`'s discipline — so the free-placement claim is proven **per target by the ordinary build**, not by a host-only probe. ⚠⚠ **THE PAD IS NOW SPENT: any further `Push` field WILL move `sizeof(Node)`** — a hard handoff for AB3/AB4.

★★ **A SECOND INSTANCE OF THE SAME DEFECT, FOUND BY THE CODER — the sweep-scope pattern, self-caught this time.** `fw_main.cpp`'s USB line printed **`"(on-air, unpinned)"` unconditionally** — the identical lie to the JSON hardcoded literal, on the **sibling** surface, reachable when a QR-**pinned** peer's key re-arrives on air. Fixed to print `conf=<level>`. ⇒ **the defect was a PAIR and the register entry described one half**; a slice that fixed only the JSON would have left the console still lying.

★ **DESIGN CALL WORTH KEEPING — `Node::peer_name_set` is deliberately NOT routed through `peer_key_set`**, against the spec's implementation hint, for three reasons documented in `node.h`: (a) `peer_key_set` needs a hash-verifying `ed_pub`, so a rename would read it back through the **aging** `peer_key_find` — **a TTL-lapsed peer would refuse a rename while `nameof` still answered**, which is spec §2.5's defect class exactly; (b) it refreshes `last_seen_ms`, and **a rename is not a sighting**; (c) its pinned early-return would have to be defeated by passing `pinned` back in — **the console asserting a confidence it never verified**, which is precisely what AB1's never-silently-promoted ruling forbids. ★ And `peer_key_set`'s two name-copy sites now **delegate to** the new single writer, so there is one writer rather than a third clamp-and-copy — proven equivalent by a poison that fired **3 pre-existing** cases (`§1.3` name refresh, `§S2` INTRO receive, `§name` WANT_PUBKEY).

**COVERAGE — the corpus-poison pair is again the model shape.** Combined poison (conf string + `sender_hash+7` + `peer_conf=7` + name→`Q`) = **0 of 36**, with **two positive controls proving invisibility rather than deadness**: poisoning `pushkind_name("peer_key_cached")` moved **3 of 36 / 14 lines** (⇒ the push really is drained 14× and handed to the bridge), and instrumenting `peer_name_set` showed it **executes 14×** (⇒ the delegated name write IS exercised, just unobservable). A third control (insert-confidence→0) moved **2 of 36** (s22 −55, s27 −1697 events) ⇒ the same function has corpus-visible fields, so the probe was strong enough. **8 native poison probes**, each caught by its expected test.

★ **A CHEAPER NOISE/SIGNAL SEPARATOR FOR ΔFLASH, new:** three unrelated `src/` objects grew ~280 B in **file** size with **0 flash-bearing bytes** — pure **DWARF line-table** growth from the enlarged `node.h`/`command.h`. ⇒ **diff the flash-bearing SECTIONS, not the object file size**; it separates real codegen from debug-info churn without guessing, and it is cheaper than the `__DATE__`/`__TIME__` hunt.

**DEVIATIONS accepted:** `peerkey <hex64> "<name>"` **bare-quoted, not the spec's `name="…"`** — the kv scanner lives in `src/firmware_config_parse.h`, a **device-layer** header `lib/console` must not include, so honouring that spelling means **forking it** (the U1 rot that header's own note complains about); bare-quoted reuses `parse_send_tail` and matches `send`/`send_channel`/`peername`. An **empty name is REFUSED on both verbs**, not treated as "clear" — no clear operation is agreed and a UI passing `""` by accident must not wipe a label. Two dedups bundled (the delegation above; two magic `32`s → `protocol::peer_name_max` pinned to `sizeof(PeerKey::name)`) — **QA accepts both as *not creating* a duplicate rather than as C1 refactors.**

**★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-07-31 AB1 (`§ab1` — the address book BEGINS: `/mrpeers` now persists NAMES and CONFIDENCE; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; uncommitted):** `PeerRec` gains `confidence` + the name, `kPeersVersion` **1 → 2**, the boot restore honours both, eviction is pinned-over-authoritative. Owner rulings: *persist the name* **and** *persist authoritative keys too* ⇒ **`/mrpeers` stops being a pinned-only store.** Native **1026/70620/0 → 1036/70785/0** (+10 cases, **+165 assertions**). ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 unmoved; 36/36 `cmp`-identical** (QA-reproduced from a **rebuilt** `lus` — 34 objects recompiled and the streams still matched, which is stronger than the no-recompile shortcut). `sizeof(Node)` **220648**, proven positively by forcing the assert to 220649 and watching the build fail with no binary left behind.

★★★ **THE `setup()` STACK IS 4 KB, AND THIS TREE HAS ALREADY HARDFAULTED THERE — so "static vs stack" was FORCED, not a style call.** `LOOP_STACK_SZ = 256*4` = **4096 B** (`cores/nRF5/main.cpp:42`, QA-verified in the vendored framework), and `fw_main.cpp` documents a prior hard fault at that site with `stackhw` down to **72 B**. A 1160 B local in `setup()` is **28% of the entire stack**. ⇒ one **shared static** in `.bss`, safe because the two users provably cannot overlap (`setup()` returns before `loop()` lazily creates `g_mesh_task`). ★★ **And the slice REMOVED stack pressure while adding `.bss`:** BEFORE there was **no** `PeerBlob` static at all — **two 584 B stack locals, one of them in `setup()`** — and both frames drop to zero. `nm` confirms the single 1160 B `.bss` object, and **ΔRAM equals `sizeof(PeerBlob)` EXACTLY on all three envs**, so nothing else moved.

★★ **THE RECORD IS 72 B, NOT 70 — AND THE PADDING HAD TO BE *NAMED*, WHICH IS A NEW VARIANT OF THE PADDING DISCIPLINE.** `alignof(PeerRec)` is 4, so 4+32+1+1+32 = 70 carries **2 bytes of tail padding** ⇒ the blob is **1160 B, 1.986×**, not the 1128 I briefed. ★ **The padding is declared as a named `_pad[2]` deliberately:** without a named member, `PeerRec{}` leaves those bytes **indeterminate**, and the new wear guard compares whole records with `memcmp` — so unnamed padding would have made the guard unsound (spurious writes, or worse, spurious *skips*). ⇒ **the six prior applications of this rule were about WHERE padding sits; this one is about padding that must be NAMED because a byte-comparison depends on it.**

★★★★ **PROBE P2 IS THE MOST VALUABLE RESULT: allowing a confidence DOWNGRADE moved NOTHING.** The store's security story depends on upgrade-never-downgrade — yet poisoning that rule left the suite green. Cause: `peer_rec_put`'s **rule-1 early return** (pinned is immutable to an on-air set) intercepts the *only reachable* downgrade, and with just two persistable levels every other combination **is** an upgrade. ⇒ **the rule was defence-in-depth that an earlier rule SHADOWS, and it was completely untested.** The coder did **not** delete it (a third level, or any direct `peer_rec_merge` caller, would then silently downgrade); they added a case driving `peer_rec_merge` **directly**, recorded the measurement in-source, and **re-ran P2 → RED**. ★ **Generalisable, and it applies well beyond this store: a rule that an earlier rule shadows is INVISIBLE to end-to-end tests. Test it at its own level, or it rots silently and the next refactor deletes it as dead.**

★ **The boot restore no longer LIES about provenance.** It used to hard-code `pinned` for every restored key; it now maps the stored value through `peer_conf_restorable`: `pinned`→pinned, `authoritative`→authoritative, and **anything else is SKIPPED and counted** — never widened to "not pinned means authoritative". ★ **Why that matters: `authoritative` is precisely what permits a DM to be SEALED**, so guessing it would manufacture a sealing capability out of flash corruption; and restoring a TOFU key as `pinned` would claim a human verified it by QR. A boot banner `peers = N restored (P pinned, A authoritative)` makes the one-time v2 loss **observable instead of silent**.

★ **`/mrpeers` is now HAMMERABLE, and the wear guard is per-record.** The second write trigger is the `peer_key_cached` push drain (precedent: `config_adopted` one arm below it), so an on-air key-learn now writes NV. `peer_rec_put` therefore returns an **outcome** and the caller writes only on `updated`/`inserted`/`evicted` — cheaper than `/mrcfg`'s whole-blob `memcmp` H3 guard (no second buffer, no extra read); the asymmetry is recorded beside that guard. ⚠ **And the store is deliberately RE-READ on every call rather than cached** — `factory_erase()` wipes `/mrpeers` behind this function's back, and a warm cache would write the wiped records straight back.

**Record-size caps CONFIRMED on both backends (they were an open question in the brief):** ESP32 NVS partition `0x5000` = 20 KB with a blob cap of **15,988 B** ⇒ 1160 B is comfortable, and `Preferences::getBytes` returns **0** (not a truncated length) on overflow, so `slot_size_ok` always catches a mismatch. nRF52 `InternalFS` is **28,672 B** total with **128 B** LFS blocks ⇒ `/mrpeers` grows by ~5 blocks (~640 B). **The nRF52 side is the tighter one and should be tracked, not rediscovered.**

**GATE:** boards 3/3, **ΔRAM +1160 on every env** (`gateway` **80.8% → 81.3%**), ΔFlash +1408/+1440/+780 attributed at symbol level (`peer_store_sync` 1436 B + `peer_store_restore` 452 B − the retired `persist_pinned_peer` 552 B + inlined policy and banner strings). Four detector probes **all exact** (474/8 · 1892/20 · 851/12 · 917/16). `src/` warning **sets identical** on all three envs. Six-env escalation **not** taken (no `Node` member, no board `#if`, no linker change) — the three-env floor is right.
⚠ **MY BASELINE WAS STALE AND THE CODER CAUGHT IT:** I carried the `lus` md5 `483d97a1…` forward from the B27 brief, but **B28 changed `lib/core`**, so the binary at this HEAD is **`3ee45562bddca06839df71bcb1d6216f`**. QA-confirmed. ⇒ **a "bit-identical `lus`" anchor is only valid until the next `lib/core` slice — re-measure it per brief, never carry it forward** (V2 applied to my own numbers, which is exactly the hazard I keep warning coders about).
⚠ **NEW TRAP, worth the gate method: `touch` does NOT force a PlatformIO recompile** — pio uses a **content signature**, so the coder's first BEFORE warning pass silently measured nothing (0 warnings, no `Compiling` line). **Delete the `.o` instead.** This is the mtime family in a new guise, and it defeats the standard `touch`-after-restore fix that the `cp -a` trap taught us.

**TWO BENCH WATCH-ITEMS FOR THE OWNER (metal-only, nothing here can test them):** (1) **ESP32 NVS churn** — NVS is copy-on-write, the blob doubled, and the write trigger is now every on-air key-learn; the `unchanged` guard is what bounds it, so confirm a busy mesh does not visibly cycle NVS pages. (2) **Post-boot trial-decryption cost** — restoring up to 16 `authoritative` keys means `e2e_open_trial` may try up to 16 candidates per sealed frame right after boot instead of ~0–2. Not a new worst case (it is the steady-state cost paid earlier), but it is a real change in post-reboot RX CPU.

**★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-07-31 ROLE-MODEL (`§role-model` — register **B28** CLOSED: `team_id != 0` ⇒ `is_mobile` is now ENFORCED; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; uncommitted):** the owner ruled the invariant after the demotion question exposed that the role's transitions were five mechanisms that disagreed; spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-31-node-role-model-design.md`. Enforced at **three** points (the core switch `Node::set_team_id`, the **boot** normalisation, and a **refusal** at `cfg set mobile 0`) plus two role-surface refusals (**O2** hosting, **R4** gateway). Native **1023/70582/0 → 1026/70620/0**. ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 unmoved; 36/36 `cmp`-identical** (QA-reproduced). `sizeof(Node)` **220648**. Boards 3/3, **RAM FLAT on all three**; ΔFlash +672/+1472/+1636 attributed per-TU and **dominated by the five fail-loud refusal strings that name the way out** — the price of C2, paid deliberately.

★★★ **THE A/B PROBE PAIR IS THE MODEL ANSWER TO A 0/N RESULT — copy this shape.** Poisoning the **already-consistent** arm (`cfg.team_id += 1`) moved **1 of 36** (`s34_team_switch_clears_plane`) ⇒ **the inserted call IS executed by the corpus**. Poisoning only the **R2-forcing** arm (`cfg.team_id ^= 0xFFFFFFFF`) moved **0 of 36** ⇒ the forcing arm is never *reached*, because no counterexample exists. ⇒ **"the call runs, the decision never fires" is now MEASURED rather than assumed** — which is exactly the distinction §0's rule demands ("a 0/N means the corpus cannot reach it, NEVER that it is inert"), demonstrated in one pair.

★★★★ **THE INVARIANT ALREADY EXISTED — AS A BUILD DEPENDENCY, FOR MONTHS.** `lib/core/mr_features.h:47-49` `#error`s on `MR_FEAT_TEAM && !MR_FEAT_MOBILE` with the words *"a team member is is_mobile; the team plane reuses the mobile link-layer"*. ⇒ **R2 is that same statement at RUNTIME.** The design intent was **compile-time-enforced on the BUILD axis and entirely unenforced on the CONFIG axis.** ★ **Generalisable: when a `#error` asserts an invariant about a build, check whether the same invariant is asserted about the CONFIG — the two axes are independent and only one of them was guarded here.**

★★ **THREE OF MY PREMISES DISPROVED, TWO OF WHICH WERE IN MY OWN SPEC (§1.2 is corrected):**
1. **`cfg set mobile` is NOT reboot-to-apply — it is LIVE and self-announcing.** My spec row 1, the register entry and the brief all said otherwise. Measured: `lc` **IS** `g_node.mutable_config()`, `live` keeps its `true` default (unlike `duty`/`ble_*`/`n_layers`/`l1_*`, which clear it **explicitly**), the reply is `ok (live + saved)`, and `is_mobile` is packed off `_cfg` at frame-build time so the next beacon re-announces. ⇒ **spec inconsistency I-d ("two latencies") DISSOLVES.** What is genuinely missing is the **state** cleanup (`_my_mobile_reg` on demotion, the FSM kick on promotion) — marked `✖ MISSING`, its own slice.
2. **`leave` is NOT a live role demotion.** `provision_apply_live` never writes `lc.is_mobile`/`lc.team_id` (only leaf_id/routing_sf/sf_list/duty/lineage/channel tunables/leaf_name), so it zeroes the **persisted** role and leaves the **live** one until reboot. ★ **The conclusion I drew survived; the mechanism I attributed to it did not** — `leave` still cannot create the outlawed config, but because it changes **neither** live field, not because it changes both. **State the mechanism, not just the conclusion, or a wrong mechanism cannot be caught.**
3. **A stale non-zero `team_id` on a no-team build is NOT inert** — `node_beacon.cpp:550`'s pre-parse foreign-nibble beacon drop reads `_cfg.team_id == 0` with **no `MR_FEAT_TEAM` guard and no `is_mobile` conjunct**, so such a build would ingest beacons from foreign leaf nibbles. ⇒ the "treat as 0" arm is a **real fix**, not defensive decoration.

★ **A sharper link-level instrument than the `handle_team` one: `Node::set_team_id` is ITSELF absent from the gateway ELF** (`nm`: 0 symbols vs 1 on `xiao_sx1262`) — gc-sections drops it because `handle_team` is its only caller. ⇒ for team-switch work, **`gateway` inertness is provable at the symbol level.** The coder also built a **per-profile ELF string matrix** (which refusal strings are live vs folded per profile) — a reusable way to prove a build-gated arm is present *exactly* where it should be.

★ **Design worth keeping:** `lib/core/node_role.h` holds two **pure** functions (`role_enforce`, `role_set_refusal`) — the one place `lib/core` and `src/` can both include, which **converted two corpus-dark console decisions into natively testable logic**, and keyed the "impossible" arm on **`MR_FEAT_MOBILE`** (the axis that actually decides it) rather than `MR_FEAT_TEAM`. ⚠ **Two edits beyond the five were load-bearing:** `handle_team` persists the promoted role (else every reboot leans on the boot backstop), and the O2/R4 refusals also guard the **team-implied** promotion — otherwise `team <id>` is a back door around `cfg set mobile 1`. ★ And that refusal **must** be `t != 0`-guarded, or O1 fires on `team 0` and **refuses every leave** (pinned by a test). **Named residual, in-source: `Node::on_init` is deliberately NOT a fourth enforcement point** — which is precisely why the corpus can still construct the outlawed config and byte-identity remained a real prediction; a fix there must **refuse**, not silently normalise.

⚠ **HONEST COVERAGE GAP, stated not papered over:** the three console refusals' **wiring** is reachable by no test — `firmware_config.cpp` is compiled by neither native (`test_build_src = no`) nor the sim (its CMake lists `lib/core` + `lib/console` only) — and **no native binary can reach the `dropped_team` arm** (native is `MR_FEAT_MOBILE 1`). Their *decisions* are 100% native-covered as pure functions; their *wiring* rests on the board compile plus the ELF string matrix. **The test asserts `kRoleHasMobilePlane` instead of pretending to cover the other arm.**

**★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-07-31 NV1 (`§nv1` — register **B26** CLOSED: the NV backend's duplicated blob validation is factored, and it is **AB1's forcing function**; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; uncommitted):** owner-queued *before* AB1 because the validator sat inside `#if defined(ARDUINO)`, which is why **nothing in `test/` included `device_nv.h`** — and AB1's gate mandates a v1-blob rejection test. Now three layers: a per-arm `read_slot`/`write_slot` primitive, a shared validator **above** the `#if`, and **one** copy of the eight typed wrappers for all arms. Native **1014/70507/0 → 1023/70582/0** (+9 cases, **+75 assertions — the first native coverage of this file**). ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 unmoved; 36/36 `cmp`-identical**, with the strong proof reproduced by QA: rebuilding `lus` emitted **0 recompiles** and a **bit-identical** binary (`483d97a1…`). `sizeof(Node)` **220648** by template-reveal. Boards 3/3 **ΔRAM 0**, **ΔFlash NEGATIVE** (−16 / −96 / −560).

★★ **MY COUNT WAS LOW FOUR WAYS, and one correction is a genuine discovery.** (1) The **size** check was written **16** times — all 8 loads **and** all 8 saves — not the 6 I counted (6 was right only for the magic/version predicate). (2) Each arm had **10** definitions and there were **three** arms ⇒ **30 → 23**, not "16 wrappers". (3) ΔFlash is **negative**, not ≈0 — the correct signature for removing duplicated **inline** code, and object-attributed to **exactly the 4 TUs that include the header**. (4) ★★ **THE `#else` STUB ARM WAS DEAD CODE:** `#if defined(ARDUINO)` wrapped the entire backend section, so the host build had **no `mrnv::` functions at all**, and no board env is "Arduino but neither nRF52 nor ESP32". NV1 makes it the **host** arm ⇒ all three arms are now compiled by the gate (nRF52 by `gateway`+`xiao_sx1262`, ESP32 by `xiao_esp32s3`, no-backend by native), and a mutant proves the host arm is **executed**, not merely compiled.

★★★ **MUTANT F VINDICATES THE SCOPE LINE I DREW — and it is the most useful number in the slice: deleting `save()`'s H3 change-detection leaves native 1023/1023 GREEN.** Nothing automated can see that guard — **native cannot observe a write and the corpus cannot compile `src/`** — so **the owner's bench is its ONLY detector.** That is exactly why the brief forbade folding it in: a "unified save" would have silently deleted flash-wear + corruption-window protection in a tree that has been **NV-bricked once**, and **no gate in this project would have caught it.** ⇒ **generalisable: before unifying two similar functions, ask which of them has a guard NO test can see.** The asymmetry is now marked in-source (`✖ NOT REPLICATED … ON PURPOSE`) naming each record and why none is slider-driven, so it reads as deliberate rather than as missed dedup.

★★ **BUILT AS AB1's FORCING FUNCTION (unasked, and the best thing in the report):** `test_device_nv.cpp`'s `/mrpeers` case asserts version±1 rejected **relative to** `kPeersVersion`, so AB1's bare bump to 2 **stays green and IS the mandated v1-rejection test**, while switching to `blob_valid_range` turns it **RED** ⇒ **the range-vs-exact decision cannot be made silently.** Three constant tripwires (`kPeersVersion == 1`, `sizeof(PeerRec) == 36`, `sizeof(PeerBlob)`) must be updated deliberately. `kPeersVersion` stays **1** and the `/mrpeers` header comment is byte-unchanged — **both are AB1's.**

**Design details worth keeping:** `slot_size_ok(int n, size_t want)` — **`n` is signed and that is load-bearing:** nRF52 `File::read()` returns **negative** on a corrupt CTZ block, the exact signal `mount_or_repair()` self-heals on, so it must never be widened to `size_t` (a mutant pins it). The **range-vs-equality asymmetry is now NAMED at the call site** (`blob_valid_range(…,2,kVersion)` for `/mrcfg` vs `blob_valid_exact` for id/peers) rather than hand-copied — deliberately **not** a bare `[v_min,v_max]` for all three, because a numeric `(kPeersVersion,kPeersVersion)` would let a later edit widen peers invisibly. `Slot{path,ns,key}` makes `/mrfault`'s separate NVS namespace **data** instead of a forked code path, and a test now enforces `kSlotFault.ns != kSlotCfg.ns` — folding it into `"mr"` would make `factory_erase`'s `p.clear()` wipe the fault history. **Self-heal confirmed five ways**, none of them "it compiled": `mount_or_repair` and `factory_erase` are **textually unchanged**; their new callees were traced to the same primitives in the same order; `InternalFS.begin()` idempotency was **verified in the vendored source** (short-circuits on `_mounted`), so dropping one redundant `begin()` cannot change mount behaviour.

⚠⚠ **PROCESS HAZARD, NEW: the session scratchpad is SHARED BETWEEN CONCURRENT SESSIONS.** Another agent's `before/`/`after/`/`pristine/` directories were already in it, and files appeared **while this slice ran**. The coder proved its 36 streams were its own by mtime and moved to `nv1-`-prefixed paths. **Same family as the `cp -a` mtime incident** ⇒ **the dispatch contract now requires a slice-tag prefix on every scratchpad path.** ★ And a second, cheaper instrument fell out: the ±32 B flash-noise note names only 2 TUs, but object-diffing proved **at least three more** bake `__DATE__`/`__TIME__` (RadioLib `Module.cpp` on both toolchains; ESP32's `chip-debug-report.cpp` and `firmware_msc_fat.c`) — they show as **changed-but-size-identical** objects whose only delta is the timestamp, which separates noise from signal without guessing.

**★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-07-31 B17 (`§team-target-range` — register **B17** CLOSED **on the `team` verb only**; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; swept into the owner's commit `2858eba` mid-flight):** a fully-consumed but out-of-range team target now refuses. Two arms, because **the two ABIs fail differently**: `errno == ERANGE` (the 32-bit board saturation) and `ul > UINT32_MAX` (the 64-bit truncation). Native **1013/70440/0 → 1014/70507/0** (+1 case, **+67 assertions**). ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 unmoved; all 36 `cmp`-identical** (QA-reproduced). `sizeof(Node)` unchanged — native **220648**, gateway **147344**, xiao_sx1262/esp32s3 **116728**. Boards 3/3, **ΔRAM 0**.

★★ **VERIFICATION STANDARD WORTH COPYING: the coder disassembled the SHIPPED newlib rather than trusting its docs.** `strtoul`'s saturation-plus-ERANGE was confirmed in the binary on both cross-compilers — ARM `mov.w r5,#0xffffffff` / `movs r3,#34` / `str r3,[r7]`, Xtensa `movi.n a3,34` / `s32i.n a3,a2,0`, and `ERANGE == 34` on both — plus `static_assert`s that `sizeof(unsigned long) == 4` there. ⇒ **V1 applied to a TOOLCHAIN, not just to our own comments.** The 32-bit runtime could not be reproduced (no gcc-multilib; `-m32` fails on `bits/c++config.h`), and that limit was **stated rather than skipped**.

★★★ **MY OWN CORRECTION WAS ITSELF HALF-WRONG, AND THE MUTANT PROVED IT.** I had corrected the register from *"a native test can NEVER catch this class"* to *"the `>2^64` token gives the ERANGE arm real coverage on native"*. Measured: it gives that arm **exercise, not necessity** — on a 64-bit ABI `ERANGE ⇒ ul == ULONG_MAX`, which is itself `> UINT32_MAX`, so **the width arm subsumes the ERANGE arm on the host.** **Mutant A (delete the ERANGE arm entirely) leaves the whole native suite GREEN — 1014/70504/0, zero failures.** Its necessity is **32-bit-only**, where `UINT32_MAX == ULONG_MAX` makes the width comparison a compile-time false. ⇒ **the honest framing is ONE ARM PER ABI, each provably inert on the other** — and the coder asserted that split **in-test** so a future "simplification" trips over it instead of silently deleting the arm that only matters on hardware. ★ **Generalisable: "a test exercises this line" and "a test would FAIL without this line" are different claims, and only the second is coverage. A mutant separates them; a passing suite does not.**

★★ **AND NATIVE WAS WORSE THAN I DESCRIBED — the destructive outcome is reachable on EVERY ABI.** I framed the host case as a harmless truncation to `0` (a LEAVE). Measured: **`team 99999999999999999999999` JOINED GARBAGE TEAM `0xFFFFFFFF` on the host too** (ERANGE ⇒ `ULONG_MAX` ⇒ truncates to `0xFFFFFFFF`), while `team 0x100000000` was the silent LEAVE. ⇒ **the "boards only" framing in my register entry understated the bug: only the TOKEN that triggers it is ABI-specific, not the damage.**

★★★★ **THE REAL YIELD — A NINTH SWEEP-SCOPE INSTANCE, AND IT MEANS B1 *AND* B17 ARE NOT CLOSED ON THE DEVICE.** `src/firmware_config.cpp:238` — **`cfg set team_id`** — does `set_team_id((uint32_t)strtoul(val, nullptr, 0))` with the **endptr DISCARDED**, so it has **none of the three guards** `parse_team_target` now carries: no leading-digit rule, no whole-token consumption, no range clause. All three defects are live on it, it is a **live team switch**, and `cfg set` is dispatched from **every transport** (the USB reader *and* the shared sink that BLE/companion use). Spellings verified: `cfg set team_id exportky` → **LEAVE THE TEAM** (the 07-30 `§team-target` bug) · `88A672BA` → **team 88** (B1) · `4294967296` → **LEAVE on host / garbage `0xFFFFFFFF` on boards** (B17). ⇒ **B1 and B17 closed the `team` verb; the family is still live on a second, console-typed path.** Left unfixed per C1 and marked `✖ MISSING` in-source with all three spellings and the U1 fix direction (route it through `mrfw::parse_team_target`). ⇒ **B27.** ⓘ **QA-checked the blast radius: the companion app does NOT send `cfg set team_id` today** (`grep` over `ios-companion/**/*.swift`), so the exposure is **operator-typed, not app-driven** — but it is a documented cfg key, so that is a fact about today, not a guarantee.

★ **A LINK-LEVEL INERTNESS PROOF, new and worth reusing: `handle_team` is ABSENT from the `gateway` ELF.** `MR_FEAT_TEAM 0` garbage-collects the whole function, which is why that env's ΔFlash is **exactly 0** while the other two moved. ⇒ for any team-console change, **`gateway` ΔFlash 0 is a linker-level proof of inertness, not a noise-floor reading** — a sharper instrument than the ±32 B rule. The other two deltas (+240, +228) exceeded 64 B and were **fully attributed at object level**: `.rodata handle_team` **3669 → 3849 (+180)** = the new refusal text, `.text handle_team` **4248 → 4276 (+28)** = the two checks plus one `println`, **every other section byte-identical**.

**DESIGN notes worth keeping.** `errno` is **unavoidable**, not a convenience: on a 32-bit target `team 0xFFFFFFFF` (legal) and `team 4294967296` (illegal) **both return `ULONG_MAX`, and only `errno` separates them** — and U1 forbids forking a second integer parser. **Explicit `0xFFFFFFFF` stays ACCEPTED**; `team 0` still leaves; `4294967295`, `0xffffffff`, octal `037777777777` and the PHY-tail forms are all pinned. **The refusal message was rewritten to state the RULE SET rather than a diagnosis**, because `parse_team_target` returns `bool` and the branch therefore *cannot* know which clause fired — the old text named only the two syntax clauses and was **false for `4294967296`** (which does lead with a digit and does parse wholly: B1's exact trap, one layer down). An enum return for per-clause attribution was **considered and rejected** as a second concern (~30 lines of test churn for a message nicety); available as its own slice if pinpoint text is ever wanted.

**COVERAGE — the poison probe belongs in NATIVE and the coder said so instead of reporting a vacuous 0/36:** the corpus provably cannot reach `src/` at all (verified independently — after the `src/` edits, rebuilding `lus` recompiled **nothing** and produced a **bit-identical** binary, and no sim TU includes the header). Mutants: **A** ERANGE arm deleted → **green** (the finding above) · **B** width arm deleted → **19 assertions red** · **C** both deleted (= pre-fix) → **35 red** (19+16, consistent). New cases cover both overflow shapes with and without a PHY tail, fail-closed asserts (the live `team_id` survives, the tail is not half-written), the negative controls incl. B1's `0x88A672BA freq=868 sf=7` regression, an **`errno`-hygiene** case (a pre-set `ERANGE` must not refuse a good id), and the ABI-split assertion.

Four detector probes **all exact** (474/8 · 1892/20 · 851/12 · 917/16), reverted with `lus` rebuilding to the same md5 as pre-probe. Warning-text sets **byte-identical** before/after on all four targets; `-Wswitch` 0.

★★★ **A THIRD DEFECTIVE READER THE REGISTER ENTRY DID NOT NAME — and taking it was right.** `sync_response_fire` (`node_query.cpp:387`) emitted `sync_response_tx{rt_total}` from the same plane-blind `_rt_count`, **~11 s later on the SAME pull** — **288 corpus events across 26 scenarios.** Leaving it would have made one mechanism **half** plane-aware (`scheduled` team-correct, `tx` team-wrong for one and the same `team_sync`) — **which is exactly the failure mode already filed as B9** (`rt_update.slot` mislabelled on the team path). ⇒ **QA accepts the scope extension: one concern (the responder's route count must name the plane it answered on), three readers.** It cost `SyncPending.team_plane`, measured at **0 bytes** — it lands in the pad the struct's three existing bools already share. **This is the sixth application of the padding-placement rule.** Revert path if ever wanted: 2 lines + the member.

★★ **MY MEASURED "FLOOR" WAS RIGHT TO BE CALLED A FLOOR — AND THE METHOD THAT PRODUCED IT UNDER-COUNTS THIS BUG CLASS 4:1.** I briefed *"exactly ONE corpus event carries the signature `rt_total:0` — s38, 1 of its 2"*, and warned it was a floor because a responder with **some** static routes reports a **nonzero-but-wrong** count. Measured: the true set is **s35a AND s38, two lines each = 4 field values**, and **s35a is precisely the invisible case** — `rt_total` **2 → 3**, a value my `grep '"rt_total":0'` could never have found. ⇒ ★ **The generalisable lesson: a signature grep that keys on a SENTINEL VALUE (0, empty, absent) finds only the subset where the wrong answer happens to be degenerate. For a "reads the wrong variable" bug, the honest detector is a DISCRIMINATOR at the site, not a value grep on the stream** — which is what the coder used (a temporary `zzpl`/`zzrtt` tag predicted the mover set exactly, then the real fix reproduced it line-for-line).
⚠ **And my predicted node class was wrong while the prediction held:** I guessed the invisible case would be a **homed** member. **No homed member answers a team pull anywhere in the corpus.** s35a's mover is an **off-grid member that hears static beacons** — so its `_rt` is populated (2) while `_rt_team` differs (3). ★★ **THE DERIVATION TRAP WAS REAL AND IS NOW MEASURED, NOT ARGUED.** I forbade deriving the plane inside the function from `team_active()`/`is_mobile`/`_cfg.team_id` because a homed member is team-active *while answering a static pull*. The coder quantified it: **12 corpus events across 5 scenarios (s22 3 · s28 4 · s29 2 · s35a 2 · s38 1) are STATIC-plane sync responses emitted by a node that HOLDS team routes**, 9 of them with a differing team count ⇒ **a derived plane would have mis-answered every one**, converting one plane breach into two. **★★★★★★★★★★★ 2026-07-31 LOC-PER-SEND (`§loc-per-send` — register **B0** CLOSED: ★★ **THE LAST LIVE LEAK — COORDINATES WERE AIRED IN THE CLEAR**; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; uncommitted):** `loc_in_dm` attached `DATA_FLAG_LOCATION` with **no crypt check**, and `want_crypt` was computed *below* the gate — so a plaintext DM from a node with the toggle on aired a 6-byte position readable by anyone in range. The toggle is **GONE** (field, `cfg set`, NV, and the app-facing binary TLV); location is now the per-send **`-l`** flag on `send`, and a `-l` send **REFUSES** unless it will be sealed. `kVersion` **22 → 23**. Native **1006/70360/0 → 1012/70417/0** (+6 cases, +57 assertions). ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 UNMOVED; all 36 `cmp`-identical.** `sizeof(Node)` **220656 → 220648 (−8)**.

★★ **THE BEFORE-ARM WAS ALREADY IN THE TREE, GREEN — THE NATIVE SUITE ASSERTED THE LEAK.** `test_node_r3.cpp:5225` built a node with `e2e_dm` off and no identity, sent, and **CHECKed that the 6 location bytes round-tripped off the wire** — i.e. it pinned the leak as correct behaviour. Instrumented on the pristine tree it prints `B0 crypted=0 aired=1 flag=1 has_loc_on_UNSEALED_wire=1`, with the packed position **`a9a2839a3a00`** sitting verbatim between the source hash and the body. ⇒ **spec §5's demanded before-arm cost nothing to produce, and the test is now inverted to a refusal.** ★ **The generalisable lesson: a green test can be the bug's strongest defender.** When a leak has lived a while, look for the assertion that *locks it in* before writing a probe — B0's had been green since 2026-06-14.

★★★ **MY PREMISE 4 WAS WRONG, AND IT CUT THE SLICE'S SCOPE.** I briefed *"the sealed cross-layer path already carries location — `node_hashlocate.cpp:414`"*. That line is inside **`e2e_seal_inner`**, whose third statement is `if (flags & DATA_FLAG_CROSS_LAYER) return 0;` (`:382`) — it is the **same-layer** seal. The real sealed-XL path is `build_sealed_relay_body` (`:500`), which **hard-codes** `flags = DST_HASH|SOURCE_HASH` and `lat=0, lon=0`, and the SEALED_RELAY body (`[seal_ctr 2][seed8 8][ct‖tag]`) **carries no flags word at all** — so setting LOCATION on the seal side alone would make the peer parse 6 position bytes **as message text**. ⇒ **`send_layer -l` REFUSES** (`err_unsupported` + `send_failed{unsealable}`); carrying a position cross-layer needs a SEALED_RELAY **body-format change**, which is a wire change and its own slice (C1/C4). ★ **And there is a THIRD cross-layer builder my brief did not name** — `delegate_send_layer` (`node_mac.cpp:742`) builds its own `TxItem` and masks the flags word down, exactly as `enqueue_cross_layer` (`:483`) does. QA verified all three claims in source.

★★ **THE THIRD REFUSAL WAS MEASURED UNREACHABLE AND DELIBERATELY NOT BUILT — accepted.** Spec §2.3 wanted a distinct refusal for *"the +6 B does not fit"*. The coder implemented it, then swept `body_len` 200…239 through `enqueue_data`: a `-l` send airs to **208** and is refused `too_large` **by the seal** from **211** up, whereas an unsealed-layout gate (`after_origin + 4 + 6 + body ≤ 241`) could only fire above **226** — 16 bytes past. QA re-derived the arithmetic against `max_payload_bytes_hard_cap = 241`: the gate is **dead code**. The §2.3 guarantee still holds structurally — a `-l` DM is sealed by construction, so the 6 bytes live inside the seal's plaintext and an overflow refuses the **whole send** loudly. **The silent best-effort drop is gone either way**, which was the point of the flag.

★★ **TWO PRE-EXISTING SILENT BANDS FELL OUT OF THAT SWEEP — new register entries, not fixed here (C1):** **(a)** `max_payload_bytes_hard_cap` subtracts `data_inner_overhead = 6` (a 4-byte MAC), but a **CRYPTED** frame's trailer is **8** ⇒ for `body_len` 215–216 (209–210 with `-l`) the seal succeeds, `pack_data` then refuses at TX time, and **no `send_failed` is pushed at all**. **(b)** for `body_len ≥ 237` the DST_HASH fit-check drops the flag, so the `!DST_HASH` branch emits `e2e_no_pubkey` **without** `push_send_failed` — also silent. ⇒ **B20/B21.** Both marked at the site.

★★★★ **AND MY RECOMMENDED DESTINATION WAS WRONG: `team_key_set` was REFUSED, with three source-level reasons. This is the most valuable thing in the slice.** I said *"route the team-scoped binding into `team_key_set` — the team map is where it belongs."* Refused because:
1. ★ **`_team_keys` has NO CONFIDENCE DIMENSION, and this path ingests CLAIMED bindings** (a cache-relayed `DATA_TYPE_H_ANSWER`; every snoop is second-hand **by construction**). `_id_bind` keeps claimed-vs-authoritative and `send_by_hash` **refuses a claimed row**; `team_key_of_id`/`team_id_of_key` have **no such test** ⇒ re-homing would **UPGRADE an unauthenticated observation to trusted**, and one consumer is **DST_HASH derivation for a sealed team DM** (`node_mac.cpp:137`).
2. ★ **It feeds team-DAD L2a mediation** — `node_beacon.cpp:821` compares `team_key_of_id(b.src)` against the beacon's key and fires a **mediated DENY** on mismatch. Seeding that comparator from *transit* traffic **manufactures spurious DENYs against legitimate teammates** — the hazard `node.cpp:567` already warns about.
3. **`_team_keys` is a 16-slot evict-OLDEST LRU whose documented feed is the BEACON**, and its `last_seen_ms` means *"heard now"* ⇒ cache-on-pass would **fake liveness AND evict genuine beacon rows**.
⇒ **The fix is to NOT bind at all on the team plane.** ★ This does **not** contradict address-book §2.5 — that spec forbids the `_id_bind` write and fixes `hashof` with a **VIEW**, *"never by a write"* — and **nothing is lost operationally**: `drain_parked_sends` takes `hb->node_id` **directly**, so the parked team send still flies on the same answer. ⇒ **LESSON: "the obvious other table" is not automatically the right destination — a map without a confidence dimension cannot receive claimed data.**

★★ **AND THE +78/+78/+56 ON s24/s25/s26 IS A FINDING, NOT NOISE:** removing the write also removed the only corpus-reachable use of the **READ-side twin**. A **relay** was answering a repeat team-scoped H out of its **static** `_id_bind` (`h_resolved` on the relay); now it **forwards** and the **owner** answers. **Delivery preserved** (same `msg_recv`, ~1.5–2 s later). Marked ✖ MISSING at `handle_h` — **register D2 / spec §12, the read-path plane audit.**

★ **B1 was WIDER than the "one line" I briefed, declared rather than smuggled:** dropping the `v == 0` gate also flips **`team 12abc`** (and `1x`, `7abc`) from *accepted as team 12/1/7* to **refused** — and the 2026-07-30 comment documented that leniency as **deliberate**. Removed on purpose (a suffix the id parser did not consume is a typo, not a tail). ⚠ **And the existing refusal string was FALSE for this case** — it said *"a team id must BEGIN WITH A DIGIT"*, which `88A672BA` does; rewritten in the same edit rather than left to drift (V1). ★ **The PHY tail is safe, re-measured over 33 cases and pinned** (`7 freq=869.0 sf=7 bw=125` and `0x88A672BA freq=868 sf=7` both fully consume their token).
★★ **NEW FINDING — THE SIM WAS ALREADY RIGHT.** `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp:739`'s `team` verb **already required whole-token consumption** (`got && p == cmd.size()`). ⇒ **B1 moves the FIRMWARE TOWARD THE SIM, shrinking a divergence instead of creating one** — the opposite of the four `Plane::AUTO`-class divergences. Residual on two axes: **octal** (`team 010` → sim refuses, metal joins 8) and **range**.
★★ **NEW ✖ MISSING, marked in-source with a before-arm — AND IT DIVERGES BY TARGET WIDTH:** a **fully consumed but OUT-OF-RANGE** token still lands. `team 4294967296` truncates to **0 = LEAVE** on 64-bit native, but on the **32-bit boards** `strtoul` saturates (ERANGE) so the same command **JOINS GARBAGE TEAM `0xFFFFFFFF`**. A **range** clause, not a syntax one ⇒ its own slice (C1). ⇒ **a native test can never catch this class; only a board build or a width-aware test can.**

**MECHANISM:** `PostAck` gains `bool team_plane`, set from **`for_team_data`** — the codebase's single team-plane discriminator, already the T6/B dedup key. **Carried, not derived**, because `do_post_ack` runs *after* `_pending_rx` is reset and `PostAck` holds neither `next` nor `addr_len`. Both ingest functions take it as a **required** parameter (C2 — a forgetful caller must fail to compile). `sizeof(PostAck)` 262 → 264, **absorbed by padding `LayerRuntime` already had** ⇒ `sizeof(Node)` unchanged. ★ Compiled **unconditionally** (deliberately not `#if MR_FEAT_TEAM`) to keep the layout profile-independent and avoid a board-conditional `#if` — trading ~64 B on a board at 54.8% flash for **not triggering the D2 grid**. Flash >64 B was investigated on `gateway`: exactly **2 of 283 objects** changed.

★★★ **MY RULE FOR SUB-SLICE A WAS INSUFFICIENT, AND IT WAS CAUGHT BY TESTING THE RULE RATHER THAN READING IT.** I specified *"the target token must begin with a digit"*. **`team 0x` · `0X` · `0xZZ` · `08` · `09` · `0abc` all begin with a digit**, yet `strtoul` consumes only the leading `0` ⇒ **0 ⇒ LEAVE**. ★ **`team 0x` is the commonest hex typo there is** — you start typing a hex team id and hit enter. ⇒ the shipped rule has **TWO clauses**: (1) lead with a digit; (2) **if the value is 0, the digits consumed must span the whole token**. Clause (2) is gated on `v == 0`, so it can only ever change the outcome of a command that would have **left the team** — `team 12abc` still joins 12, unchanged. ★ **BONUS VARIANT MEASURED: `strtoul("-1")` → `0xFFFFFFFF`, so `team -1` silently JOINED A GARBAGE TEAM.** Both now refuse loud, before any NV load/save, `set_team_id`, key mint/adopt or PHY retune.

★★★ **THE SPEC'S TRANSPORT CLAIM IS FALSE, AND ACTING ON IT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED A PRIVATE KEY AS INBOX TEXT.** §2.3 states the grant "rides the existing sealed machinery (same-layer sealed, **SEALED_RELAY for XL/delegated**)". **SEALED_RELAY CANNOT CARRY AN APP TYPE.** The `MOBILE_SEND` wrapper has exactly **one** enclosed-type byte and the sealed path already spends it on `DATA_TYPE_SEALED_RELAY` (`node_hashlocate.cpp:1051`, `node.cpp:1304`) ⇒ a delegated grant would arrive **sealed but TYPE-STRIPPED**, and the recipient would file **37 raw private-key bytes as inbox TEXT**. Separately `e2e_seal_inner` refuses `DATA_FLAG_CROSS_LAYER` outright (`:382`), so an XL type-19 could only ever be **cleartext**. ⇒ **delegated and cross-layer grants are REFUSED, not implemented** (C1: giving SEALED_RELAY an inner type byte changes a landed frame's body format — its own slice). **v1 transports: same-layer CRYPTED · team-plane CRYPTED (leaf-exempt, so off-grid members on different nibbles ARE reachable) · a home's CRYPTED last-mile to a hosted mobile.** ★ Sealed-only is **STRUCTURAL, not conventional** — three guards with distinct loud refusals at `enqueue_data`, `enqueue_cross_layer` and `send_by_hash`'s delegate branch, so **no code path can emit a type-19 unsealed or type-stripped**; receipt mirrors it and the grant is **consumed on every outcome** (never inbox'd, never a `msg_recv`).

**FORMAT (my recommendation, verified before adoption):** `[team_id u32 LE][name_len u8][team_name ≤32][tkpriv 32]` = **37..69 B** — **`tkpub` dropped**, since `team_channel_key_derive` clamps at store and is idempotent so re-deriving is exact, and a pub/priv mismatch becomes structurally impossible rather than something the receiver validates. U2 kept via a **fourth entry point on the same derivation path** (`team_channel_key_adopt_priv`), not a fork — calling `adopt(derived, priv)` would have paid a second X25519 to re-check a cross-check that cannot fail. Receiver refuses a `team_id` mismatch and an **unsealed** type-19; re-grant is idempotent; the name is **not persisted**.
**COVERAGE:** corpus-dark and **proven dark** — 7 sites, and **4 of the 7 controls sit on the SAME SOURCE LINE** as the probe (so a 0 means "this branch never took", not "this function never ran"), with same-TU tracers at 7619 / 772 / 1125 / 146 invocations. ⚠ **Honest weak spot: `send_by_hash`'s delegate-branch control fires only 4 times in 1 of 36** — the delegated-sealed shape is barely exercised corpus-wide and that guard rests on its native test. Console verbs remain algebra+compile only (`src/` is outside both builds) — mitigated by putting `parse_grant_args` in the pure native-tested header.
⚠ **ALSO STILL OPEN:** `handle_team`'s mistyped-subcommand fallthrough (**`team exportky` → `team 0` = LEAVE**, T-K1b's finding — `grantkey` is matched before the numeric parse for the same reason, but the underlying one-line fix still needs a ruling); and a U1 near-duplicate (`parse_grant_args`'s 0x-hex scanner restates `console_parse.cpp:69`'s grammar because that function's `Tok` type is TU-private — noted in-source, C1).

**★★★★★ 2026-07-29 T-K1b (`§team-ch-key T-K1b` — `team exportkey` + lock-state JSON; **36/36 BYTE-IDENTICAL** — QA GO; committed):** closes the export half the contract never defined (T-K1 flagged it as the T-K4 blocker). `team exportkey` → `{"ev":"team_key_export","team_id":…,"tkpub":"<64 hex>","tkpriv":"<64 hex>"}`, **on every transport per the owner ruling**, plus `team_ch_key` on the JSON `ready`/`cfg` surface (T-K1 had added it to the *text* dump only). ★ **All 36 byte-identical, s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 UNMOVED, `lib/core/` has ZERO changed files, `sizeof(Node)` 220656 unchanged, boards 3/3 ΔRAM 0 on all three.** Native **967/69766/0 → 970/69791/0** (+3 cases, +25 assertions). All four detector probes reproduce exactly. QA-reproduced. ★ **First brief in this arc whose premises I could not have disproven — all three held.**

★ **THE KEYLESS ANSWER, RULED BY THE CODER AND ENDORSED: a distinct event, never a null-filled success object.** `{"ev":"team_key_err","reason":"no_key"|"no_team"}`. The reasoning is better than my "null or a refusal" hand-wave: the surface emits **zero** JSON `null` literals (measured), every optional field being omit-when-absent, and **the consumer is a QR encoder** — a null-blind encoder writes the literal `null` or 32 zero bytes into a team QR, and an all-zero scalar is **exactly what `team_channel_key_derive` refuses as a non-key**. A distinct `ev` cannot be mistaken for a payload; a null inside a success object can. Matches the existing `mobile_err`/`peerkey_err` idiom (U3). ★ **`no_team` is a real case it added deliberately:** a node can hold a key while teamless (`team new` then `team 0` — T-K1's flagged non-clearing switch), and exporting then gives `team_id: 0`, i.e. **a QR that provisions "leave"**. It refuses to export and does **not** touch the key, so **T-K2 still owns the clear-on-switch ruling.**

It is masked on a mobile node (`parse_phy_tail` refuses the unknown key first) but **live on a node with `is_mobile == 0` and a non-zero `team_id`**, which is reachable from the console. The coder placed `exportkey` **before** the numeric parse so the intended verb can never land there, and rejects a non-empty tail — but deliberately did **not** touch the join path (C1: that is a behaviour change to `team <id>`, its own slice). **Marked in-source. The fix is one "tail must begin with a digit" check** — and it should probably be taken before anyone types at a live team on metal.

★★★ **THE CLAMPING FINDING — exactly the trap the brief warned about, and the coder read the vendored source instead of assuming.** `crypto_x25519` (`monocypher.c:1546`) trims a **LOCAL COPY** via `crypto_eddsa_trim_scalar` (`:1469` — `out[0] &= 248; out[31] &= 127; out[31] |= 64`) and **never writes back**; `crypto_x25519_public_key` (`:1557`) *is* `crypto_x25519(pk, sk, {9})`, so it clamps too. ⇒ ★★ **monocypher clamps at USE, NEVER at STORE.** An unclamped stored scalar therefore **round-trips perfectly inside monocypher while being non-interoperable outside it** — which is precisely where T-K3 (the sealed key grant) and T-K4 (the QR) will take it. Fixed by **storing the CLAMPED scalar**, so the persisted and distributed bytes are canonical RFC 7748; clamping is idempotent (bit 6 of byte 31 survives `& 127`), so `adopt` reproduces the identical pair.
⇒ ★★ **THIS IS WHY THE BRIEF DEMANDED A KAT AND NOT A ROUND-TRIP: a round-trip test passes even when both halves are wrong together.** The shipped KAT is against **RFC 7748 §6.1**, feeds the RFC's deliberately **UNCLAMPED** Alice scalar, and **spells out the expected clamped bytes rather than borrowing monocypher's own constants** — so it cannot be satisfied by the implementation agreeing with itself. A second KAT runs both RFC parties through our function and reproduces the RFC's shared secret **K**.

★★ **MY NV PREMISE WAS WRONG, AND THE CORRECTION IS A REAL HAZARD I MISSED.** I briefed that `device_nv.h`'s `version >= 2 && version <= kVersion` range means *"a v21 blob still loads after the bump — verify the new fields default safely."* **Disproven: both backends ALSO require `n == sizeof(out)`**, and `sizeof(mrnv::Blob)` goes **208 → 272**, so a v21 record fails the **SIZE** check and is rejected wholesale — the version-range check never sees it. The real safe-default guarantees are elsewhere, and both were verified: (a) `mrnv::Blob nv{}` is **zero-initialised** at `fw_main.cpp:590`; (b) ★ **appending at the struct TAIL is LOAD-BEARING, not stylistic — the ESP32 `Preferences::getBytes` path DOES write the 208 stored bytes into the buffer before returning a mismatched length, so a MID-STRUCT insert would have splattered v21 bytes into the key fields.** A keyless node would have booted holding garbage that passes an all-zero check.

★ **THE ZERO-DRAW PROPERTY IS STRUCTURAL, NOT LUCK — and it was proven POSITIVELY, not inferred from byte-identity.** The sim's `team` verb **refuses `team new` outright** (`NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp:778`: *"`team new` is deliberately unsupported: its random nonce would break scenario byte-reproducibility — name the id instead"*), so **no scenario CAN call it.** Execution-trace probe over all 36: `team_channel_key_derive` **0** · `team_channel_key_mint` **0** · `_adopt` **0** · `_load` **0**, with **two independent live controls**: same-TU `set_team_id` = **1** (s34) and ★ **same-seam `_hal.rand_bytes` (`node_hashlocate.cpp:399`) = 10** (s22 ×2, s27 ×8). **The same-seam control is the decisive one** — the corpus *does* draw `rand_bytes` ten times and team keygen contributes zero of them, which byte-identity alone could never distinguish from *"generation is broken and skipped."* The two claims are established separately: draws = 0 by probe, generation works by KAT + 19 native cases.
⚠ **RNG MODEL REFINED (mine was coarser than reality):** the sim's `rand_bytes` is a **separate per-node `mt19937`** (`_rng_bytes`, `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp:117`) from `rand_range`. A stray keygen draw would **not** shift protocol jitter — it would shift that node's later **E2E seal nonces**. **QA therefore re-verified s22 and s27 specifically byte-identical**, since they are the only scenarios that draw at all.

★ **PADDING, fifth application of the rule: +64, not +65 and not +72.** The largest top-level hole in `Node` is only **7 B** (80 B total, all fragmented — from a clang record-layout dump), so the two 32-B arrays genuinely cost 64. But **the has-key flag costs exactly 0** in the 1-byte hole at native offset **19** (between `_team_dad_pending` and the 4-aligned `_key_hash32`) — the same hole `node.h:1242` already exploits. **Six-env ABI × member-set grid, compile-only on the real toolchains:** `xiao_sx1262` / `xiao_esp32s3` **+64** · `xiao_mobile` / `xiao_esp32s3_mobile` **+64** · ★ **`gateway` / `gateway_esp32s3` +0** (`MR_FEAT_TEAM 0` — the inertness proof).
★★ **AND AN `offsetof` ASSERT WAS REFUSED WITH PROOF — my brief asked for something impossible.** I asked for T5's unguarded `static_assert(sizeof) + offsetof` shape. On `Node` **both are impossible**, and the coder tested it rather than arguing: `offsetof(Node, _key_hash32)` is an **access error** (private) *and* emits **`-Winvalid-offsetof`** (non-standard-layout) — i.e. **a new warning, which is gate-blocking**; and an unguarded `sizeof(Node)` assert cannot exist because the value differs per ABI. ⇒ **T5's shape works for a standard-layout POD (`PeerLiveness`), NOT for `Node`.** Substituted the six-env compile-only measurement, which is **strictly stronger than an assert** — it measures the real toolchains instead of asserting one.

⚠⚠ **MARKED IN CODE, NOT DEFERRED SILENTLY — T-K2 MUST RULE ON THIS BEFORE IT STARTS SEALING:** `set_team_id()` **deliberately does NOT clear the key**, so a `team <other>` switch leaves the **previous team's key** in place. **Inert today** (nothing reads it) — but the moment T-K2 seals, **a switched member would seal for its new team under the old team's key.** The `⚠ MARK OF WHAT IS NOT DONE` block at `node.h:_team_ch_pub` states it and a native test **pins the current behaviour** so any later change is deliberate. ★ Note the interaction with **s34** (`team_switch_clears_plane`): the switch clears the routing planes but **not** the content key.

★★ **TASK ZERO — the gate hole I owned is CLOSED, and T3 disarmed nothing.** All four detector probes re-measured at HEAD before any T5 work: **P-T7 / s38 = 451 ev, 8 of 16** · **P-T1 / s35a = 1836 ev, 20 FAIL** (bench string present) · **P-T6A / s37 = 807 ev, 12 of 28** · **P-T6A+P-T7 = 872 ev, 16 of 28**. **Event counts moved (T3 re-anchored all three streams); FAILURE COUNTS ARE IDENTICAL IN ALL FOUR.** Re-run at the end of T5: 474/8 · 1892/20 · 851/12 · 917/16 — counts rose (T5 adds telemetry), **discrimination unchanged**. ⇒ the lapse was real but caused **no damage**, and the numbers above are the new recorded baseline.

★★★ **`sizeof(Node)` DOES NOT MOVE — MY "★★ THIS IS THE D2 SLICE" WAS WRONG, AND THE ALTERNATIVE IS BETTER THAN THE SPEC'S.** §3/T5 and my brief both called for a dedicated `_team_bidi[16]`; §10 sized it at **128 B** (u32-seconds) and that arithmetic is **correct** — the u64-ms form really is 256 B and alignment does not collapse them. But `PeerLiveness` (`node.h:1350`) is **72 B with byte 1 and bytes 4–7 as pure padding** (the u16 needs 2-align, `first_timeout_ms` needs 8-align). Placing `team_bidi_state` at byte 1 and `team_bidi_confirmed_s` at bytes 4–7 keeps `sizeof(PeerLiveness) == 72`, so **neither `_peer_liveness[64]` nor `_team_liveness[16]` grows a byte: 220592 → 220592, ΔRAM 0 on ALL SIX envs.** ★ **Fourth application of the padding-placement rule** (after `radio_freq_mhz`, `team_hop_cap`, `HashQuerySeen.team_scoped`). It is also the **U1** answer: a separate `_team_bidi[16]` would have been a *second* 16-entry `team_local_id`-keyed table beside `_team_liveness` with identical cap, LRU and lifetime — the field-drop fork the rule exists to prevent.
★★ **A METHOD IMPROVEMENT WORTH COPYING: the layout claim is proven on EVERY ABI, not just native.** `static_assert(sizeof(PeerLiveness) == 72)` plus two `offsetof` asserts are **deliberately NOT `MESHROUTE_NATIVE`-gated** — because a 0-byte claim is an **alignment** claim, and the usual `#ifdef MESHROUTE_NATIVE` guard would let a board ABI break it silently. Every board build now re-proves it. ⇒ **use this shape for any future "it fits in the padding" claim.**

★★ **TWO MORE OF MY PREMISES DISPROVEN — both by refusing work rather than doing it, which is the harder call.**
**(1) The feed set is TWO sites, not three: the ACK-site team confirm was NEVER a parity item, and was REFUSED.** `note_link_confirmed` has exactly two callers in the engine — the CTS site and the beacon heard-set scan — and **the static plane performs no bidi confirm on an ACK either.** Adding one for team would be a **team-ONLY mechanism, the inverse of this arc's mandate**, and pure decoration: `awaiting_ack` is set only inside `do_data_tx` and its stash-retry/re-issue twins, all downstream of `handle_cts`'s gap timer, so **a team ACK is structurally unreachable without a team CTS on the same flight** — the confirm has already fired milliseconds earlier. Refused as a forced fit (§C); **T2's ✖ note at `node_mac_rx.cpp:1397` and spec §3/T2 row 5 are both corrected in-source.**
**(2) `node_routing.cpp:192-193` is NOT a bidi consumer.** It reads `_dest_seen_ms[256]` — **last-heard**, which is a *different fact* from bidi-confirmed (**does-it-hear-us**). Driving it from a confirm timestamp would mark **every never-CTS'd team route non-viable**: a delivery risk with no basis. `mark_dest_seen` is static-only and the team plane genuinely has no last-heard fact. Left alone (C1/C2).
★ **And a FOURTH zeroed bypass my brief omitted: `node_routing.cpp:159` `budget_penalty_q4`** (reads `_neighbor_budget_tier`, an R4.2 node_id-keyed map with **no team mirror**) — named in-source so the residual asymmetry is visible. Also found: **`node_cascade.cpp:172`** reads `_link_bidi[from_next]` for the slow-reprobe, **static-only by construction** (the `pt.plane == TEAM` branch returns above); a team slow-reprobe would need `_link_reprobe_last_ms`, another 2048 B array — out of scope.

★★★ **TWO METHODOLOGICAL FINDINGS, EACH WORTH MORE THAN THE CODE.**
**(a) ★★ A UNIFORM-OFFSET POISON IS NOT A VALID CONTROL FOR A COMPARISON-ONLY SCORE.** `effective_score` is consumed **only relatively** (`route_strictly_better` compares `av` vs `bv`), so subtracting a constant from *every* team candidate **cancels** — it can bite only at the `_routing_snr_floor_q4` viability boundary, which no team candidate approaches. So **CTL-PEN's 0/36 meant "my probe was too weak", NOT "dead code"** — and the coder distinguished the two instead of banking the zero, then built **CTL-PEN2 (differential: penalize `confirmed` instead)** which proves the site live at **4/36**. ⇒ **for any relative/comparative metric, a control must be DIFFERENTIAL, not an offset.** This belongs beside the "0/N means unreachable, not inert" rule in §E.
**(b) ★★ PZ-SELF WAS LOAD-BEARING WITH NO ASSERT — an honest gap found and closed.** A plane-blind self id makes a **HOMED** member (`node_id` ≠ `team_local_id`) never find itself in a teammate's page, so **22 healthy confirms become a false `one_way` plus 13 `degraded_advertise` beacons** — a healthy team link falsely demoted on every homed member. ⚠ **`s35a`/`s35b` cannot express this at all** (every member is off-grid). Closed with **8 new asserts in `s37`**, the only homed-member scenario; PZ-SELF now fails asserts 30 and 31. ⇒ the same off-grid blind spot that made `s37` necessary in the first place.

**THE C3 HUNK (the constraint that was one slice old):** `!b.is_mobile` is **UNTOUCHED**; the team destination was added as `else if (same_team_beacon)`. A plain mobile and a foreign-team member satisfy **neither** branch and are still dropped exactly as T3 left them ⇒ **no team id can reach `_link_bidi` by any path through that block, decidable from one wire bit.** The self-id is plane-selected (`team_plane ? team_local_id() : _node_id`); not-yet-DAD'd ⇒ **refuse** (C2). `decay_link_bidi`'s team arm is threaded but **NOT wired**, for the identical reason as the static arm (nothing treats `confirmed` ≠ `unknown`), stated ✖ in-source.

**THE HONEST-OUTCOME CLAUSE, ANSWERED — and the trade I feared does not exist.** `dm_delivery_breakdown` **`diff`-identical on all 12 movers + s18 + s17**; **airtime IDENTICAL** (same tx count, same total ms, **+0.0%** on every mover); **ΔRAM 0 on all six envs**. What it buys, measured: ★ **mutual one-way recognition on the team plane — the literal §3/T5 thesis** (s22: TeamB(143) establishes its direct link to TeamA(241) is one-way; TeamA independently reaches the same verdict about 143); ★ **one real route commitment** — TeamB promotes the 2-hop path to 241 via TeamC(224) from `alt` to `primary` while the direct hop is one-way, **then recovers** = *"commit to the 234 path deliberately rather than by accident"*, happening; T3's `heard_set_complete`-on-team-beacons gains its declared-missing consumer; 920 events of operator visibility. Cost: **+2688 B flash on ARM-full**, 0 RAM, 0 airtime, 0 delivery change. **Coder's recommendation, which I endorse: LAND IT** — with zero RAM and zero airtime the decision rests on correctness and the mechanism demonstrably works. ⚠ **But be clear-eyed: delivery value is NIL in this corpus** (consumed by T2/T7, as with T3), and ★ **`degraded` is read by NOTHING that makes a routing decision on either plane** (only the wire bit and `degraded_advertise`) ⇒ **stage ③ is gossip, not routing; stage ② is the ONLY path-selection change T5 makes anywhere.**

★★★★ **PART C — IMPLEMENTED, SWEPT, AND BACKED OUT. THE HOP-CAP ASYMMETRY IS A STRUCTURAL FINDING, NOT A TUNING PROBLEM.** Flipping `node_beacon.cpp:861` to `hop_cap_for(same_team_beacon)` is **inert on 34 of 36**; its *only* effect is to disarm `s35a`/`s38`. **No value of `team_hop_cap` restores them** — swept on the real files: **1 → 5 failures** (discovery can no longer reach 3 hops; three DMs stop delivering) · **2 → 3 failures** with `rreq_forward` → **0** (the 2-hop relay answers from its own cache at 1 hop) · **3 / 4 / 8 → 9 failures** (DV pre-teaches the target). **The window is empty.**
★★ **WHY, and it generalises:** after the flip a node's **DV reach and its RREQ reach are the same number**. For a relay to *forward* an RREQ it must satisfy `dX < cap` **and** `dXT > cap`; with a single cap and `dX + dXT` = the true distance, that is **unsatisfiable**. ⇒ a team destination becomes either **already-DV-known** or **discovery-unreachable**, and **radius-clipping dies as a test method.** ⚠ **This is ALREADY true of the static plane** — `dv_hop_cap` is both the DV cap **and** the F RREQ TTL (`node_carriers.h:138`). ⇒ ★ **the flip is CORRECT static parity, and `s35a`'s radius clip was exploiting the very asymmetry T3 was sent to close.** Real discovery fills **table/page** holes, which is also what the §0 bench actually showed.
⇒ **UNBLOCK PATH (recorded in-source at `node_beacon.cpp:859-891`, with the DONE/MISSING notes in `node_carriers.h` and `node.h:473` and the sim dispatch row all flipped to ✖ MISSING + evidence):** land the flip only after **`s35a`/`s38` are re-authored so the far teammate is unknown for a KNOWLEDGE reason** — a late join, or an engineered dirty-rotation page hole — while preserving their team-plane `rreq_forward` coverage. **Part B makes the flip a one-token change the day that is settled.** ⇒ **QA RULING (mine, recorded here): DO NOT re-author yet.** The flip is measured **inert on 34/36** and R4's 8-hop ceiling is **practically unreachable** in a 3–10 member team (R4 itself says 1–3 typical, stragglers to 8; the corpus tops out at 3). Trading a risk to the arc's two headline detectors for a change with **no measurable effect** is the wrong trade today. ★ **TRIGGER TO REVISIT: the first time any team scenario produces a team DV path of >8 combined hops, the asymmetry becomes live and the flip must be re-taken.** Until then it is a documented, measured, bounded deviation from R4 — owner may overrule.

★★★★ **AN I2 BREACH THAT PART A UNBLOCKED — FOUND BY MEASUREMENT, CONTAINED IN THE SAME SLICE, AND IT IS §12's READ-PATH CLASS AGAIN (third instance).** `node_beacon.cpp:897`'s bidi-census guard read **`!same_team_beacon`**, and its own comment then equated *"not same-team"* with *"static"*. **False** for a **foreign-team** member's beacon and for **any plain mobile**: both are `is_mobile`, both air `b.src` = a **local** id, and `update_link_bidi_from_beacon` writes the **STATIC** `_link_bidi[advertiser]`. **Latent only because a mobile beacon never set `heard_set_complete`, so absence was never authoritative — and the T3 census makes it authoritative, at which point the `one_way` write fires.** Measured on the unfixed arm: **s30's static `S` wrote `_link_bidi[100]/[200]/[98]`** (three team local ids) · **s37's statics H1/S2 wrote `[210]/[220]`** · s24's statics `[34]/[44]/[91]` · s25/s34 members wrote **each other's foreign team ids**. **Invariant I2 breached verbatim, via a READ-PATH gate rather than a learn site.** Tightened to **`!b.is_mobile`**, **provably strictly stronger** (`same_team_beacon ⇒ b.is_mobile`), s18-inert; every leaked write is gone and `link_one_way` returns to its HEAD value on all five scenarios. ⇒ **Part A could not have landed without it** — it would have activated a documented invariant breach. Precedent: T1 *"…and close the ledger wedge it opened"*.

It buys operator visibility for silent teammates, T5's prerequisite, and 4 convergence gains for a few percent of beacon airtime; and it flushed out a real I2 breach no clean-run gate would have shown, which is arguably its largest yield.**

★★ **DURABLE METHOD FACT, and it disproves a premise of mine: ADDING ASSERTS IS FREE — it does NOT re-anchor.** `ExpectRunner` evaluates **post-hoc** over the finished stream, so assertions are not events. I briefed "s37 will re-anchor if you add asserts"; it does not. ⇒ **strengthening a detector costs nothing**, which removes the last excuse for leaving a weak one in place.

★★★ **THE TEST-DESIGN LESSON — why `s37`'s asserts 13/14/20 lapsed, stated as a rule.** They assert an **absence** (`no static-plane r_tx`). T7 satisfied that absence **by making the frame route somewhere else** — it gave `TO` a route, so no RREQ was needed and the symptom vanished while the breach remained. **They could not be repaired in place** (under the T7-live shape no `r_tx` occurs at all, so no `r_tx`-keyed form can fire); they are kept, and the *property* is re-armed by four new asserts **25–28**:
| # | assert | fenced arm / T7-live |
|---|---|---|
| **25** ★★ | `event_count H1 e2e_ack_rx == 0` — the home **consumes** its mobile's teammate's ack (`push{kind:e2e_acked}` for a DM it never sent) | 2 / 1 — **fails in BOTH shapes** |
| 26 | `event_count H1 data_rx == 0` — the frame-level twin | 2 / 1 |
| 27 | `event_count S2 data_rx == 0` — static **relay** of a team ack | 2 / 0 — complements by shape |
| 28 | `not_contains TO rt_update "dest":101` — the direct I2/A2 signature | 1 / 2 |
⇒ ★★ **THE RULE: AN ABSENCE ASSERTION NEEDS A PAIRED PRESENCE ASSERTION, or it is satisfiable by the thing simply not happening.** Masking 25/26 requires the forbidden event to stop occurring, and the only two routes to that are (a) the ack travels **team-internally** — correct — or (b) **no ack at all**, which asserts **11 and 19** fail on. So *(11+19: "the ack must reach TH")* ∧ *(25+26: "H1 must never see it")* can both pass **only** if the ack stayed inside the team plane. **That pairing is exactly what 13/14/20 lacked.** ⚠ Assert 28 is **honestly weaker and labelled so in-file**: matching on `dest` alone it cannot separate `_rt_team` from `_rt`, so it also forbids a legitimate static route to 101 — **over-strict by design**, because over-strict fails loud while under-strict passes silently. ★ It is deliberately **NOT keyed on `slot`**, and the coder quantified why: **~120 team installs corpus-wide read `primary`/`alt` against 9 reading `team`** (the `node_beacon.cpp:876/879` mislabel).

★★★ **PART B — THE COUPLING IS REACHABLE, BUT NOT BY MY HYPOTHESIS.** ⚠ **My ack-on-AUTO route is DISPROVEN as stated:** a not-yet-DAD'd member's AUTO send to a team peer **is** accepted and aired with a **static** inner origin (`origin=17`, `addr_len=1`, `mobile_src=1`) — but its RTS airs **`src = 0`**, so `learn_route_via` refuses `via == 0`. **T7's blocker holds for that producer.** ⚠ Its **multi-hop extension remains UNMEASURED and is claimed by nobody**: the relay accepts and CTSes the `src=0` frame, but **neither the src=0 arm nor the valid-src control relayed** in the harness — the control was **vacuous**, so *"does a relay forward a `src=0` frame?"* stays **OPEN**, recorded in-source.
★★ **The REAL producer needs no DAD-pending at all, and it rides the DEFAULT METAL VERB.** Two ordinary beacons suffice — a plain **non-mobile** beacon giving `_rt[55].next = 238`, then a **team** beacon setting `_team_peer[238]` (the §18/I9 numeric collision, or a live `is_mobile`/`team_id` config change) — after which a **`Plane::GLOBAL` `send 55`** aired `RTS src=93 next=238 dst=55 addr_len=1` with inner **origin 17**. ★ **`GLOBAL` is what `console_parse.cpp:259` emits for EVERY plain `send`**, so this is the default verb on metal, reached with no test seam. Receive side is **unconditionally open**: the same frame with any valid non-zero `from` installs `_rt_team[17]` + `_team_peer[17]` at one hop. **Corpus: 0 occurrences** of the direct A2 signature across all 13 team scenarios — **the hole was not live in the corpus**, which is why nothing re-anchors.

★★ **PART C — THE FIX IS AT THE SENDER, AND THE RECEIVER-SIDE CHECK I EXPECTED IS PROVABLY IMPOSSIBLE.** Team-DAD draws from **17..254** (`node_mobile.cpp:191`) and static `node_id`s are **1..254**, while neither RTS nor plain DATA carries a `team_id` (**I9**) ⇒ *"is this origin a team-namespace id?"* is **undecidable at the receiver**. Only the originator knows which plane it meant. **Root cause: two decisions disagreed.** `node_mac.cpp:867`'s `team_next` chose the **WIRE** plane from `is_team_peer(pt.next)` — the *next hop* — while `stamp_origin` chose the **origin namespace** from `flight_is_team_plane(plane, dst)` — the *plane*. Fix = **two symmetric conjuncts**, `&& flight_is_team_plane(pt.plane, pt.dst)` at `:867` and its twin at `:1395` (**both needed** — the RTS and DATA `addr_len` must agree or the receiver rejects a DATA it already CTS'd). `PendingTx` already carries `plane`, so **no new state and no layout move**, and `node_mac.cpp:767` already computes the identical expression to pick the table ⇒ **stamp / route / wire now agree BY CONSTRUCTION — T6's thesis one level down.** ★ Closing origination closes the whole class: **a relay never calls `stamp_origin`** (`node_mac_rx.cpp:1198` copies `pa.origin`), so it can only ever propagate a mis-stamp, never create one.
⇒ ⚠ **MY T7 NOTE'S CLAIM IS NOW CORRECTED BY MEASUREMENT, in-source and here.** "The fallback arm provably cannot install (`via == 0`)" was over-broad; the **actual** bound is `via == 0`, **one hop, one producer** — and a different producer (above) reaches the same breach by another route entirely.

Native non-vacuity: with the fix reverted, **exactly the 3 payoff assertions fail**, residual + control both hold.

★ **FOUR MORE OF MY PREMISES DISPROVEN (the count for this arc is now ~15, and the coders have been right every time).** (1) **THREE sim DM sites, not two** — `CmdKind::send` is built at `send_hash` (795), **`send_hashx` (825)** and `send`/`send_e2e` (904); `send_hashx` had **no `-t` at all**, so *a sealed team DM could only ever ride AUTO*. (2) **SEVEN `AUTO`≠`GLOBAL` branches in `lib/core`, not two** — my `node_routing.cpp:22` + `node_hashlocate.cpp:1021`, plus `node.h:191`, `node_cascade.cpp:277`/`:313`, `node_mac.cpp:748`, `node_hashlocate.cpp:1168`. (3) **s22 is a fifth mover and a MANDATORY team scenario I never named.** (4) "plain-DM-to-a-static is unaffected" was right but for a **stronger** reason: what matters is the `_team_peer` bit for `dst` *plus* the **sender's** `team_id != 0` — s35a's plain `send S4` lines are issued by static S1, so doubly unaffected.

★★ **ONE MECHANISM EXPLAINS ALL FOUR ORIGINAL MOVERS, and it is a genuine defect the divergence was hiding.** The *first* plain `send_hash` to a teammate takes the **plane-independent** off-grid team park (`node_hashlocate.cpp:1098` — **no plane test**), whose H answer installs the teammate's hash → **team local id** into **`_id_bind`, the STATIC-plane map**. Every *subsequent* plain send then hits `id_bind_find_by_hash` (`:1011`), which **precedes** the `:1021` AUTO cascade, and calls `do_send(team_local_id, …, plane)`: under AUTO ⇒ `_rt_team` ⇒ delivered; under GLOBAL ⇒ `_rt` ⇒ no route ⇒ **4 static RREQs** ⇒ `send_deferred_giveup` + `send_failed`. ★★★ **And in s24 the STATIC nodes re-flood it: `rreq_forward{dst:34, origin:91}` on n1/n2/n3 — BOTH values are team local ids.** Static infrastructure flooding RREQs for team ids: the **same I2/R3 class as s38's assert 12, arriving on the SEND side**, and on metal it is the **default** path. ⇒ the AUTO default was masking it corpus-wide.
⚠ **PRE-EXISTING DEFECT SURFACED (present in the BASE arm, NOT introduced): the hash-bind ingest is PLANE-BLIND** — a **team-scoped** H answer writes `_id_bind` (`id_bind_set{node:34, source:"h_relay"/"h_query"}`). s24 asserts a static *bystander* never does this, but **team members do it to each other**. **Own slice owed.** Non-movers explained rather than assumed: **s28** — off-grid sender + `_id_bind` miss ⇒ `:1098` forces TEAM either way; **s34** — its post-switch plain sends *already* storm and fail in the AUTO baseline (`is_team_peer` cleared by the switch).

Proven **not** this slice's doing: a pristine `git archive HEAD` (no A, no B, pre-slice sim) gives the **identical 729/9**. **T7 narrowed it 12 → 9 and nobody measured it.** The three that lapsed are asserts **#13, #14, #20 — the R3 static-isolation trio, including the one `s37` itself labels *"★★ THE ACCEPTANCE CRITERION"*.** Mechanism, measured: under P-T6A the origin is static **101**, and **T7's now-unfenced DATA-origin learn installs `rt_update{dest:101, hops:1, next:210}` on TO — a STATIC node id inside `_rt_team`** — so TO holds a route, emits no static RREQ, and the symptom the asserts key on disappears.

That covers only the **1-hop** case, where the not-DAD'd sender's own RTS airs `src = 0`. It does **not** cover `via = 210` (a valid relay's team id) with `origin = 101` (static) — precisely the shape above. **T6 and T7 are therefore MUTUALLY LOAD-BEARING: T7's DATA-origin learn has NO team-namespace check on `origin` and is protected only by T6's stamp.** The coder assessed this as counterfactual-only (it needs T6 reverted). ⚠ **I am NOT accepting that as settled** — a *relayed* frame from a not-yet-DAD'd member would present exactly `origin=static, via=valid-teammate`, and whether a relay forwards a frame whose `src` is 0 is **unmeasured**. **OWED: measure it, and if reachable, add the namespace check + an assert.** Restoring s37's asserts 13/14/20 is owed regardless.
