★★★★ **2026-07-28 FINDING — `Plane::AUTO` IS UNREACHABLE ON REAL FIRMWARE, AND THREE MANDATORY TEAM SCENARIOS
DEPEND ON IT.** Found by QA while scoping the `node_mac.cpp:89` slice; **verified, not inferred.**

**`lib/console/console_parse.cpp:259` is the ONLY site in `lib/` or `src/` that assigns a DM's plane:**
`out.u.send.plane = team ? 1 /*TEAM*/ : 2 /*GLOBAL*/;   // §6.4 HARD SPLIT: -t => team-only; plain send =>
global/home (fails loud if no home)`. It **never emits 0 (AUTO)**. The companion/BLE transports share the same
`dispatch()` and therefore the same parser, so GLOBAL is their default too. ⇒ **on metal a user-originated DM is
always TEAM or GLOBAL; `Plane::AUTO` is never carried in a `Command` **plane field**.** ★★ **CORRECTED 2026-07-31 (B3): the older wording here — *"`Plane::AUTO` is simulator-only"* — is FALSE on metal.** The console verb `resolve <0xhash> [hard]` assigns **no plane**, and `request_resolve` calls the 2-arg `emit_hash_query`, so `node.h:809`'s `Plane::AUTO` default applies — on hardware, via `fw_main.cpp:472`/`:799`. Three more AUTO-default call sites are metal-live (`node_join.cpp:468`, `node_mac_rx.cpp:1361`, `node.cpp:1495`). ⇒ **B23**; the claim above is the narrow, defensible one.

**The sim never emits GLOBAL for a DM.** `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp` assigns `u.send.plane` at exactly two sites
(`:802` `send_hash`, `:913` the id-addressed verb), both **TEAM on a trailing ` -t`**, otherwise the field stays
**0 = AUTO**. Nothing documents that as a deliberate choice — it reads as a **stale wrapper**: the firmware gained
the §6.4 hard split (2026-07-13/14 team-plane bench fixes) and the sim was never updated to match.

★ **CONSEQUENCE 1 — s24, s25 and s26 validate a path metal cannot take.** All three reach teammates with a
**plain** `send_hash` and no `-t` (`send_hash cccc0003` / `aaaa0003` / `dddd0004`). In sim: AUTO ⇒ `rt_find` takes
`is_team_peer` ⇒ routes `_rt_team` ⇒ delivers. On metal: GLOBAL ⇒ §18 forces `_rt`, **and**
`node_hashlocate.cpp:1021` (`plane != Plane::GLOBAL && …`) **skips the team hash-locate branch entirely** ⇒ not a
team DM at all, and for an off-grid member with an empty `_rt` it fails. **The sim is MORE PERMISSIVE than metal
here** — the inverse of the usual concern, and it means these three scenarios' team delivery is not evidence about
firmware behaviour.

★ **CONSEQUENCE 2 — T6's AUTO arm is dead on metal, and my BASELINE record of it was wrong.** The T6 note above
records the coder's justification for widening the predicate: *"the corpus's team DMs are AUTO — s24/s25/s26 use
`send_hash` without `-t`; that is also the companion default."* The first half is true **of the sim**; the second
half is **false** — the companion shares the console parser and so sends GLOBAL. ⚠ **T6's EXCLUSION of GLOBAL
remains CORRECT** (§6.4's hard split is deliberate: a plain send is global/home and must fail loud without a home),
and `flight_is_team_plane` still matches `rt_find` verbatim, which is the property that matters. What is wrong is
only the *justification*: **the AUTO arm is scaffolding for the DM verbs, not a reflection of real usage** (⚠ **not** "simulator-only" — see the B3 correction above: metal's `resolve` verb and three internal call sites do reach AUTO). Corrected
here rather than silently.

★ **CONSEQUENCE 3 — the `node_mac.cpp:89` gate is a DEFAULT-PATH bug on metal, not a corner case.** The gate reads
`!(plane == Plane::TEAM || is_team_peer(dst))`. Since metal's `plane` is only ever TEAM or GLOBAL, the separate
`is_team_peer(dst)` arm can fire **only for a GLOBAL flight** ⇒ a plain `send <teammate> -a` from a mobile with no
routable home is **admitted as "team"** when §6.4 says it must fail loud. Raises that slice's priority. ⚠ **And its
scenario control cannot be written until the sim can emit GLOBAL** — so either the divergence is fixed first, or
that slice is native-only and must say so.

⚠ **OWNER DECISION PENDING.** Fixing the sim to match firmware would make s24/s25/s26's plain team sends **fail**
(correctly), so those scenarios need rewriting to `-t` — a multi-scenario re-anchor of the mandatory team set. That
is the honest fix and it is not small. Do not let this note rot: it is the only record that three green mandatory
scenarios are green for a reason metal does not share.

**★★★★★★★ 2026-07-28 T6 (`§team-parity T6` — one origin namespace per plane + plane-keyed ledgers; corpus 34 → **35** — QA GO; committed):** the owner ruling of §11. **Part A** — `stamp_origin` gains `(Plane, dst)` and a team flight stamps `team_local_id()`. **Part B** — plane-key the ledgers: `_per_origin_channel` and `_hash_query_seen` keyed, `_seen_origins` corrected, **`_mediated_recent` REFUSED with a proof**. **RE-ANCHORS: s28 `27f486fa` → `4ca592d3` · s29 `7c6d4aa1` → `71947232` — both VALUE-ONLY** (counts unchanged at 3552/1622; delta is one inner byte, `origin 101 → 233/196`, plus pkt hashes; `dm_delivery_breakdown` **diff-identical**). **NEW: `s37_team_homed_origin` `7b3bd8d6`/654, 24 asserts.** ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 UNMOVED; no static scenario moved.** Native **921/69286/0 → 930/69355/0** (+9 cases, +69 assertions). All QA-reproduced independently.

★ **ACCEPTED DEVIATION — the coder WIDENED my predicate, and was right.** My brief said `plane == Plane::TEAM`. It shipped `flight_is_team_plane(plane, dst)` = `plane == TEAM || (plane == AUTO && is_team_peer(dst))`, which is **`rt_find`'s dispatch expression VERBATIM** (`node_routing.cpp:22`) — QA diffed the two. ⇒ **"what identity a flight claims" and "where it routes" cannot diverge, and that divergence IS the defect §11 ruled on.** The literal form would have left every **AUTO** team DM still stamping the home id — and the corpus's team DMs *are* AUTO (s24/s25/s26 use `send_hash` without `-t`, which is also the companion default), so my version would have fixed almost nothing while appearing to pass. `GLOBAL` is deliberately excluded (§18: a global send to an id colliding a teammate's team id routes on `_rt` and must keep the accountable home id). `rt_find` was deliberately **not** refactored onto the new helper — C1, a cleanup slice owns it.

★★ **`_mediated_recent` REFUSED — my four-ledger list was wrong, and the refusal came with a proof rather than a judgement.** Its key's `loser_hash` is a 32-bit **key** hash — a global node identity, not a per-plane id — so an alias would need one physical node to be key-loser for the same numeric id on *both* planes. The two writers' loser sets are **disjoint on the single wire field `b.is_mobile`**: the team writer requires `same_team_beacon` (`b.is_mobile && same_team(...)`, `node_beacon.cpp:532`); the only static writer passing `source==bcn && authoritative` is `node_beacon.cpp:627`, guarded by `!b.is_mobile` at `:626`. ⇒ **saves +256 B of nRF52840 RAM** (`MediatedRecent` 16 → 24 B × 32) **and a `sizeof(Node)` move.** This is the third of my premises a coder has disproven in this arc, and the third time the coder was right.

★ **`_seen_origins` ALREADY had a plane-ish bit — and BOTH my brief and `node.h`'s own §P2-7 audit note were drifted about it.** `node_mac_rx.cpp` ORs **bit 62** when `mobile_from` is set; but that is **not the plane** — a registered mobile's ordinary *static-plane* DM sets it too, so the residual alias was team-vs-mobile-static, never team-vs-static as the note claimed. Fixed properly with **bit 61 from `for_team_data`**; four disjoint ranges now. `_hash_query_seen` cost **0 bytes** (`team_scoped` shares the existing tail slot with two bools; `sizeof` 24 → 24, template-revealed). ⚠ Its documented safety was worse than "fragile": the role-exclusion invariant is **defeatable by live config** — `handle_h` returns pre-mark for a static H iff `is_mobile` (`:584`) and for a team H iff `!same_team` (`:603`), so any `team_id != 0 && !is_mobile` node marks **both**, and `set_team_id` (`node.cpp:413`) never touches `is_mobile` ⇒ `cfg set team_id` produces exactly that node. ⚠⚠ **CORRECTED 2026-07-31 (B27): that is TRUE BUT NOT EXCLUSIVE, and the difference matters** — `handle_team` has **no `is_mobile` guard either** (it calls `set_team_id(t)` unconditionally; only `team_dad_fire` is `c.is_mobile`-gated, `firmware_config.cpp:958`), and **NV persists `team_id` and `is_mobile` independently** (`fw_main.cpp:603-604`), so a reboot reproduces the config with no console involved. ⇒ **removing `cfg set team_id` (B27) does NOT close this invariant hole** — do not sell it as doing so. The hole needs its own decision: either a guard where the team is adopted, or an explicit ruling that `team_id != 0 && !is_mobile` is legal and the H-plane arms must handle it.

★★ **`sizeof(Node)` is UNCHANGED at 220592 — MY ESCALATION'S PREMISE DID NOT MATERIALISE.** I escalated this slice to all ten board envs on the D2 grounds that Part B moves the layout. It does not: `_hash_query_seen`'s new field fits existing tail padding and `_mediated_recent` was refused. Proven **positively** (template-reveal + a tripwire `static_assert(== 220593)` that fires); the assert comment carries the four-part arithmetic. **Boards 10/10, ΔRAM +0 on every env**, warning histogram byte-identical per env, `-Wswitch` 0/0. ★ ΔFlash investigated per the >64 B rule and it yields a real result: **`gateway` is +0 for Part A** and +112 for Part B — i.e. **Part A costs exactly zero bytes on a `MR_FEAT_TEAM 0` build, the inertness proof by construction.** (The `__DATE__` noise floor was also reconfirmed live: an identical BEFORE tree gave `xiao_sx1262` 468884 then 468916.)

★ **AND MY CORRECTION TO THE ESCALATION WAS ALSO WRONG.** I cut it to four envs as the "complete ABI × feature grid". There is a **third member-set axis I missed**: `node.h:1229-1233` gates three `Node` members (`_admin_pubkey[32]`, `_admin_counter_floor`, `_admin_provisioned`) on **`MR_FEAT_REMOTE_MGMT`**, which only the three `*_mobile` envs clear (via `-DMR_PROFILE_MOBILE`; `production` and the gateways deliberately KEEP it — verified, the comment that looks like production's is the mobile-role section header). ⇒ the padding grid is **3 × 2 = 6**, not 2 × 2: `{full, gateway, mobile} × {ARM, Xtensa}`. Moot for T6 (ΔRAM +0 everywhere), corrected in the gate doc. ⇒ **the routine board set stays THREE per the owner's ruling; the D2 escalation set is SIX, and was never ten.**

**s37 — the R3 violation captured as a countable assertion.** 4 nodes, one PHY, one leaf: static host `H1`(101) + `S2`(102), **homed** member `TH` (team id 210, `_node_id` 254 host-assigned), **off-grid** control `TO` (220). ★ **`H1↔TO` is deliberately ABSENT** so `TO` can never hold a static route to 101 — that converts the pre-T6 plane crossing from a *silent success* into a loud countable failure; `S2↔TO` is present on purpose so `TO`'s static plane is not degenerate. **P-T6A (revert the team arm) → 801 events, 12/24 fail**, reproducing the §11 signature exactly: `tx_enqueue{origin:101}` · `r_tx{dst:101,reason:no_route,ttl:1}` ×3 on TO · ★★ **`rreq_forward{dst:101,origin:220,ttl:15}` ON THE STATIC NODE S2 — a team message's ack re-flooded by static infrastructure, which is the R3 breach in one event** · `e2e_ack_tx{dst:101}` · `send_failed{e2e_ack_timeout}` on TH · `e2e_ack_rx` 0 where 2 are required. All four control-arm asserts hold under the probe. Assert 12 keys on the `send_failed` **emit**, not the push — push emits drop `reason`, a §E blind spot, measured both ways. **No assertion reads `slot`** (the SCEN note's trap #1 respected).

**COVERAGE:** Part A `enqueue_data` **2/34** (s28/s29, the only homed-team scenarios) + s37; Part A gateway-envelope and delegated/XL **algebra, and stronger than "unreachable" — value-identical to pre-T6 on every reachable path** (`send_by_hash`'s TEAM block returns in all 3 arms; `send_layer` forks on `is_mobile` and returns in every arm; the one mobile that reaches it is off-grid, where `mob==false` ⇒ pre-T6 already stamped `_node_id == team_local_id`); channel-M ×2 **0/34 but PROVEN inert not unreachable** — a destructive `origin = 0xAB` poison at both sites moved 0/34 while a tracer showed ~1900 flood + ~500 pull executions incl. 8 with `channel_flavor_team` whose value genuinely changed, and the same-site control at the DM site moves 2/34; Part B ledgers **native-only** (P-ALL reverting all keys → 6 fail / 12 assertions, with 3 deliberate controls holding: static reduction · not-DAD'd fallback · off-grid identity).

★★★ **A PREMISE IN MY OWN HANDOVER WAS WRONG, AND ACTING ON IT WOULD HAVE MADE s23 WORSE.** I wrote that T1's sim-side `-t` patch "survived and is committed" at `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp:801`. **It did not.** That line is inside the **`send_hash`** branch (`:788`–`:813`), it predates T1 by a week (sim `a54baf7`, 2026-07-21, `§F-TR-1`), and the **id-addressed `send` verb (`:896`) had NO `-t` handling at all** — `c.u.send.plane` was assigned at exactly ONE site in the file. I mistook a pre-existing parse for the lost one. ⇒ converting s23 to `"send 254 hop_test -t"` on the tree as it stood would have aired `hop_test -t` as the body **still on plane AUTO**. The coder found this, wrote the missing 10-line patch (mirroring the `send_hash` site verbatim — U1, one suffix convention, no second parser), and flagged it. **QA independently verified the enclosing branch and the 2026-07-21 date.** ⇒ **the lesson stands and is now doubly earned: T1's sim patch was lost WITH s35, and the surviving `.patch` file I found in a scratchpad was a red herring** — see [[meshroute-agent-scratchpad-is-volatile]].

**A — s23 corrected** (lines 25/31/37/43, prefix `-t` → trailing ` -t`). **RE-ANCHOR `d019abfc` → `96f6ffe3`, event count UNCHANGED at 795 — a VALUE-ONLY re-anchor.** ⚠ I predicted the count would move; **it does not**. Whole delta attributed: the DATA hex loses `2d7420` (`-t `), airtime **150 → 139 ms**, push payloads `-t hop_test` → `hop_test`, and every downstream timestamp/pkt-hash shifts. **s28 audit: NO defect** — its only `-t ` match is prose in `_desc` and its team send is already correct suffix-form on the hash path. Corpus sweep: **11 other ` -t` uses, ALL `send_hash`, all correct since 2026-07-21 — s23's four lines were the only defect in the corpus.**

**B — `s35a_cochannel_isolation` `f95bf6ce`/2336 (74 asserts) + `s35b_..._control` `4f49e969`/1063 (5 asserts)**, both 0 failures, 3-run stable, **both now MANDATORY** (spec §7.3). 8 nodes on ONE PHY (870 MHz / BW62.5 / SF6 / `sf_list=[6]`, no per-node override), chains S1–S2–S3–S4 and 213–234–235–174 interleaved so every static sits between two teammates and hears both; one member on leaf 4 (the bench's mixed nibble); all off-grid. ★ **`dv_hop_cap: 1` on the members is the load-bearing knob and the obvious alternative is a trap:** raising `team_beacon_period_ms` does NOT stop team DV, because **a member's ordinary beacon IS a same-team beacon** — the team converged by DV in 13 s and the scenario silently stopped testing discovery. Recorded in `_desc`. A7 is a within-run A/B (statics idle vs loaded); **A6 is the pair, and measured IDENTICAL** — `S1→S4 4 sent / 4 arr / 100% / 0 giveup / mean_hops 3.0` in both, so co-channel team traffic costs the static plane nothing.

★ **PROBE MATRIX — the discrimination proof, replacing the no-longer-executable "must fail before T1":** P-T1 (revert `node.cpp:1138` to `!is_team_peer`) → **20 FAIL incl. `actual_reply="OK error ctr=0 depth=0"`, the literal bench string from spec §0** · P-T4 → **3 FAIL** · P-ISO-F (I1 team-F drop) → **31 FAIL** (8×A1 with a static holding `{dest:213,hops:1}`, 9×A3, both storm ceilings: `r_tx` 6→20, `rreq_forward` 1→13) · P-ISO-Q (I7 gate) → **3 FAIL** (statics emit `q_rx opcode 0`). ★★ **s35b held 0 failures under EVERY probe — that is what makes it a control rather than a second copy.** Also: assert 10 (`ttl:8` = `team_hop_cap`) is the **first corpus coverage of a site T0 recorded as 0/32 gate-blind**, attributable to T1∧T4 jointly; and s35a is the **first corpus exercise of `rreq_forward` on the team plane**.

★★★ **THE §11 / T6 MEASUREMENT — my premise CONFIRMED, my prediction right about the outcome and WRONG about the mechanism, and the difference is what matters.** A homed member (`team_local_id 210`, home 101) stamps **`origin=101`, the home's STATIC node id**, on a team-plane DM; the off-grid control stamps its team id. But **the reverse ack is not routed on the team plane at all** — the acking teammate addresses `dst=101` on the **STATIC** plane. ⇒ (i) **with** a static route to the home the ack RETURNS (1.77 s, via the home last-miling to its hosted mobile) — so a `-a` team DM from a homed member **requires static infrastructure for its ack, a plane crossing that violates R3** ("team-internal routing only"); (ii) **without** one — and that is the bench's own leaf-4-vs-leaf-7 shape — it **FAILS**: `r_tx {dst:101, reason:"no_route"}` at ttl 1 then ttl 16 ×3, i.e. **four STATIC-plane RREQ floods for the home's id, generated by a TEAM message's ack**, then `send_deferred_giveup`, and at +60 s `send_failed{e2e_ack_timeout}` on the originator. ⇒ **the defect is real but configuration-dependent, the failing configuration is the bench's, and the R3 violation is a stronger argument for T6 than the one §11 was ruled on.** `stamp_origin` and the `node_mac.cpp:70` comment deliberately untouched — T6 owns them.

★ **TWO NEW FINDINGS (reported, not fixed):** (1) **`rt_update`'s `slot` field is WRONG on the team DV path** — `node_beacon.cpp:876/879` label a beacon-DV merge `"primary"`/`"alt"` regardless of which table was merged, so a same-team beacon's merge into `_rt_team` emits `slot:"primary"`. Proven from the stream: a member emitted `{dest:174,slot:"primary"}` then routed a `Plane::TEAM` send through it, and `rt_find(dst,TEAM)` hard-forces `_rt_team` (`node_routing.cpp:22`) with no fallback. ⇒ **the corpus-wide `slot:"team"` count (9) understates team installs by a large factor and ANY analysis keyed on `slot` is unsound.** Sim-only telemetry, so not a firmware bug. (2) ★ **the board ΔFlash instrument has a ±32 B noise floor on nRF52**: `src/fw_main.cpp:420` and `src/firmware_commands.cpp:261` bake `__DATE__ " " __TIME__`, and when the two TUs compile in different *seconds* the literals stop merging. A control build of the **identical unchanged tree** gave `xiao_sx1262` a third flash value (468900 → 468916 → 468932, +16 each) with `nm` showing no symbol size change. ⇒ the gate doc's "phantom ±16 B" is at least partly this, not only path-hash length, and it is **reproducible on demand**.

Boards 3/3 per the new owner ruling, **ΔRAM 0**; ΔFlash +32/+16/0 = the build-clock noise proven above. `-Wswitch` 0, warning set unchanged by construction. Sim 27/27 from scratch. ⚠ **The stale-artifact hazard hit the coder THREE more times** — twice a restored source with a stale binary silently replayed a probe (2131 ev / 31 fail), caught by cross-checking event counts. **Fifth and sixth instances in this arc; `rm`-before-build is now in its harness.**

★ **THREE OF THE QA BRIEF'S PREMISES WERE DISPROVEN BY THE CODER, AND IT WAS RIGHT EACH TIME:** (1) `q_opcode` is a **2-BIT** field (`(b3 >> 6) & 0x03`, `frame_codec.cpp:556`) — 1/2/3 were taken, so `team_sync` had to take **0, the last free codepoint; the opcode space is now FULL** and a fifth Q kind needs a wire change. The brief's implied `= 4` was *probed*: it truncates to 0 on the wire while comparing as 4 ⇒ the feature never dispatches **and** the isolation gate stops firing (s28's static S3 gains a `q_rx`) — a dead feature plus a leak, with no build error and plausible telemetry. (2) **`-Werror=switch` could not have caught it** — there is no switch over `q_opcode` anywhere; `frame_trace.h:120` switches a **`uint8_t`**, so `-Wswitch` is structurally blind. (3) the **`_node_id == 0`** guard blocked off-grid members too, not just the `is_mobile` line. ⇒ ★ the warnings gate does not cover enums that reach a switch as integers — the manual audit is still load-bearing.

**★★★★ 2026-07-28 T2 (`§team-learn-parity` — RE-ANCHOR of 5 team scenarios — QA GO; committed):** the team plane gains the static plane's RX-event coverage: six sites activated (overheard RTS, CTS, DATA prev-hop, ACK, NACK, Q sender) plus **one new — DATA-origin learning**. **RE-ANCHORS: s23 `d019abfc`/795 · s24 `6fe66b63`/1357 · s25 `cc42279b`/559 · s26 `c87bee65`/714 · s34 `1396f1e7`/771.** Native **904/69024/0 → 913/69103/0**. ★ **s18 keystone `1cd21235`/271629 UNMOVED; NO static scenario moved.** `sizeof(Node)` **220592**, boards 10/10 ΔRAM 0 — ★ **ΔFlash exactly 0 on all three `gateway_*`, the `MR_FEAT_TEAM 0` proof.**
★★★ **THE OWNER'S DATA-ORIGIN RULING IS ONLY PARTLY IMPLEMENTABLE, AND THE BENCH CASE IS NOT YET FULLY FIXED.** I pushed for unconditional `_rt_team[origin] = {next, hops}` on the strength of my §10.1 review. **Measured: it would be an isolation breach.** `stamp_origin` (`node.h:821`) returns `_my_mobile_reg.home_id` whenever the registration is active — **no team-plane exception** — so a **homed** teammate stamps its **home's STATIC node id** on a team-plane DM. Observed live: **s28** node 3 (`team_local_id=233, home=101`) and **s29** node 3 both send with **`origin=101`**. Unconditional learning would put static id 101 into `_rt_team` (s35's A2 breach) and `rt_find(101, AUTO)` would then **shadow that node's own static route to its home**. ⇒ the learn carries a soundness gate **`is_team_peer(origin)`**, which fires on exactly that case (4 rejections corpus-wide, all `origin=101`). **CONSEQUENCE, stated plainly: 213 still learns nothing about a never-heard 174** — "is this origin a team id?" is undecidable while a homed member stamps a static one.
★★ **AND T1 LEFT A FALSE INVARIANT IN A COMMENT — QA verified both halves at source.** `node_mac.cpp:70`, written by T1, justifies its E2E-ACK gate change with *"the origin it stamps is the team_local_id, which every teammate CAN route."* **False for a homed member.** ⇒ T1 turned a loud `mobile_no_home` refusal into an accepted send whose **ack cannot return** on the team plane — a silent failure replacing a loud one (the C2 anti-pattern). Same class as the `do_data_tx` "we only EMIT our own team's messages" claim closed in 27zc. ⚠ **OPEN FOR THE OWNER, and it is the top item: should a `Plane::TEAM` origination stamp `origin = team_local_id()` instead of the home id?** That is what finally closes the bench case AND makes a homed member's team DM replyable — but it moves **anti-spam accountability** from the home to the member, and `origin` also keys `_seen_origins` dedup. A design ruling, not a bug fix. Deliberately NOT taken unattended.
★ **TWO MORE PREMISES OF MINE DISPROVEN.** (1) I warned `committed_hops` saturates at 7 while R4's ceiling is 8, so the deepest path understates. **It does not** — saturation applies to what a *forwarder* airs, so a receiver 8 hops out reads exactly 7 → `hops = 8`, **exact**; understatement begins at 9, which `team_hop_cap` forbids. And no overflow guard is needed: it is a **3-bit wire field**, so `+1` can neither wrap nor exceed 8 (unlike the F path's 8-bit `f.hops`, which did need one). (2) I said row 7 (Q sender) "pairs with T4, which is what makes a team Q exist" — **a team Q exists today**: `node_channel.cpp:524/1181` send `channel_pull` with `mobile = is_mobile`, and **s28 carries 17 such receptions**.
**WHAT T2 ACTUALLY DELIVERS** (all measured): refresh/shortening of multi-hop team routes from live traffic — which today only a beacon or an F can do — and ★ **the RATCHET FIX**: `rt_merge`'s metadata path refreshes `last_seen_ms`, so `age_out_stale_routes` no longer strips a busy teammate's route and its `_team_peer` bit. That closes spec §0's *"one-way ratchet toward permanently-unsendable"*.
⚠ **THREE DELIVERATE NARROWINGS, all marked in-source.** (a) The overheard-RTS arm is gated `is_team_peer(r.src)`, **not** the spec's "src is a same-team local id" — **the RTS carries no team id** (`frame_codec.h:289`), so admitting an unknown `mobile_src` would set `_team_peer` for a foreign team's member or a plain mobile. ⇒ **all six arms refresh KNOWN teammates and never admit a new id.** (b) Rows 3/5's "+ team `note_link_confirmed`" is **NOT DONE** — it writes `_link_bidi[256]`, the exact static alias the guard prevents; **T5 must carry it**. (c) A `learn_direct_neighbor` U1 hoist was built and **reverted** (C1, and provably inert: `had=1` in 66/66 team merges).
**ISOLATION — MEASURED, NOT ARGUED.** `rt_merge` instrumented with its plane on matched trees: **0 plane/table mismatches** in every probed scenario; the **static merge call sequence is byte-identical in s18 (293 148 calls)**, s23, s26, s28, s29. ⚠ s24/s25/s34 each gain exactly **one extra static-plane merge ATTEMPT** — each **refused** before any write (`rt_skip_mobile_transit` +1). **Zero static writes.** Delivery: `dm_delivery_breakdown` **`diff`-identical on all five movers and on s18**; zero change in any dedup/suppression class. ★ s23's `rts_tx` **22→21**: the reverse DM now takes the *verified* reverse path instead of the beacon-learned one — same hop count, one fewer transmission.
**COVERAGE — six of seven arms are corpus-INERT, each with a moving same-site control** (so the zeros mean inert, not unreachable): RTS 141 execs 0/32 (control 20/32) · CTS 69, 0/32 (9/32) · DATA prev-hop 64, 0/32 (1/32) · ACK 61, 0/32 (7/32) · Q 12, 0/32 (23/32) · ★ **NACK: 0 corpus executions — native test is its ONLY detector**. Only **4b (DATA-origin)** is corpus-covered — 64 gate entries → 20 learns, and it is the **sole** source of all five stream deltas (poison `p41t` moves exactly those 5). Non-vacuity: pre-T2 core fails **6 cases / 9 assertions**; an over-broad mutant (gates dropped) also fails 6/9 **including a pre-existing `§S7 T-A` test**.
⚠ **PRE-EXISTING EXPOSURE SURFACED (reported, C1-deferred to T3):** a team member's beacon carries `_rt_team` entries in its route page and a **static receiver evaluates them against `_rt`**. Closed today only by construction — `_mobile_peer[b.src]` is set before the DV loop and every team beacon is `is_mobile`, so `route_uses_mobile_as_transit` always refuses. **Residual:** a *different* team's member whose `_team_peer` bit for that id happens to be set (§18 collision) makes the predicate return false and the team id installs.

**★★★★ 2026-07-28 T1 + T1b (`§team-discovery` — the BENCH FAILURE IS FIXED, and the regression it opened is closed — QA GO; committed):** T1 removes the `!is_team_peer(dst)` precondition so `send -t <unknown-teammate>` triggers on-demand discovery instead of `err ctr=0 depth=0`; T1b closes the permanent-failure state T1 opened. **★ ALL 32 SCENARIOS BYTE-IDENTICAL** through both. Native **895/68744/0 → 900/68825/0 → 904/69024/0**. Boards **10/10, ΔRAM 0 on all ten** both slices. `sizeof(Node)` **220592**.
★★★ **MY PREMISE THAT T1 WOULD MOVE ANCHORS WAS WRONG — and the reason is a lesson worth keeping.** I briefed, on T0's evidence, that adopting `team_hop_cap` at team-live sites must re-anchor. T0 measured **site executions** and I inferred observability from them. T1's author measured **values**: `node_route_discovery.cpp:224` runs **4361** times, exactly **3** on the team plane (all s28, at `hops` 0 and 1) — orders of magnitude below both the old bounds (16/32) and the new (8/16), so the guards decide **identically**. ⇒ **EXECUTED ≠ OBSERVABLE.** (Two corrections to T0's own numbers: `:283` is 2 team executions not 1, and s28 is the *only* scenario with any team-plane F traffic.) Arithmetic verified: a flood at ttl=N reaches k hops out carrying `hops == k-1`, so `hops >= cap` first bites at k = cap+1 ⇒ the deepest teammate a team flood resolves is exactly **8** = R4's ceiling.
★★ **A SCOPE ERROR OF MINE, CAUGHT BEFORE IT SHIPPED.** I specified the send guard as `team_id == 0 || team_local_id() == 0`. The coder added **`_cfg.is_mobile`** — because `team <id>` on the console sets `team_id` **without** `is_mobile`, while `handle_f_team` requires `is_mobile` **on the receiver**: such a node would flood team RREQs that teammates answer and then **drop its own replies** — a non-convergent storm. It is also the identical predicate the sibling `send_channel -t` already uses (`node.cpp:1130`), so U1. **Scope split accepted as briefed:** `node_route_discovery.cpp:233`/`:283` IN (the RREQ guard's rationale — *"a legitimate RREQ's hops can't reach the cap, it IS the TTL bound"* — only holds if the cap tracks the TTL), `node_beacon.cpp:846` OUT to T3.
★★★ **T1 OPENED A PERMANENT-FAILURE STATE; T1b CLOSES IT. QA held the commit until it was fixed.** `_rreq_last_team[16]` had **no age-out** — QA verified at source: the `kAgingTimerId` sweep had six `age_out_*` calls and none for it, cleared **only** inside `set_team_id`. Pre-T1 the ledger was unreachable (a `-t` send required a known peer); T1 made it reachable. Measured: one member addressing 20 distinct ghost ids floods for **ids 1–16 only**, then emits **120 `table_cap_hit{refuse, cap:16, plane:"team"}`** — ⇒ **after 16 distinct team destinations, team discovery is DEAD on that node until a team switch or reboot**, and the app sees only `send_failed{no_route}`, indistinguishable from a genuine miss.
★ **T1b overruled BOTH of my instructions, correctly.** (1) **TTL = `route_request_seen_ttl_ms` (10 s), not my `send_defer_ttl_ms` (30 s)**: at `node_route_discovery.cpp:66` the read predicate is `(now - t_ms) >= route_request_seen_ttl_ms`, and once open it *always* returns true — the stored `ttl` is never consulted again. An entry is **spent at 10 s**; holding it 20 s longer burns the very budget the fix frees. Matching the read predicate's boundary also makes the sweep **provably outcome-identical to no sweep except at cap saturation** — the minimal possible blast radius — and `protocol_constants.h:348` already argues for decoupling these two constants. (2) **The static `_rreq_last[128]` is swept too, against my team-only lean**: measured max occupancy **5 of 128**, `table_cap_hit` **zero corpus-wide**, so the static sweep is inert *by theorem*; and the two ledgers are the two arms of **the same ternary in the same function** — sweeping one is the U1 field-drop rot. The `_rreq_seen` siblings are deliberately NOT swept (they `recent_ring_mark`, which evicts-oldest rather than refusing, so they degrade but cannot wedge) — stated at the site.
★ **AND THE SITE IS CORPUS-DISCRIMINATING, so 0/32 means inert not unreachable:** a tracer shows the sweep reached in **32/32**, 31153 invocations, **115 entries actually reclaimed** (114 static + 1 team) — ⇒ my "the team ledger is corpus-invisible" premise was wrong; s28 node 7 marks at t=660475 and the sweep reclaims at t=720976. A **destructive** sweep (dropping live entries) moves **4/32**; disabling the rate limit entirely moves **7/32**. Non-vacuity: removing the sweep call fails **4 cases / 9 assertions**; an empty-everything mutant fails **2 / 6**, caught by the selectivity test.

⚠ **ALSO HIT AND CAUGHT: the 27zd `cp -a` mtime hazard, in T1's own probe harness.** `rsync -a` left a content-restored probe tree older than its build objects, so make skipped the rebuild and the first probe run replayed the *previous* probe's stale objects — reporting a false 13/32. Fixed with a forced `touch` sweep plus a sanity probe, and every probe re-run from scratch. **The hazard recorded in 27zd is real and recurring; treat the rule as standing.**
★★ **s35 IS WRITTEN AND PROVEN TO DISCRIMINATE — BUT BLOCKED ON A 1-LINE SIM CHANGE.** 5 nodes, 33 assertions, labelled by what they pin (1–3 transport, 4–13 mechanism, 14–33 isolation controls). BEFORE: **860 events, 10 failures** including the literal bench string `"OK error ctr=0 depth=0"`; AFTER: **952 events, 0 failures**, 3-run stable. ★ **The blocker: the simulator's id-addressed `send <id> <text>` verb has no `-t`** — `c.u.send.plane` is set only on the `send_hash` path, and `Plane::TEAM` reaches `node.cpp:1110` **0 times in 1.86 M events**. A suffix-form patch is written and verified byte-identical on all 32. ⚠ **AND FOUR LIVE SCENARIO LINES ARE SILENTLY WRONG TODAY:** `s23_mobile_team_multihop:25/31/37/43` write `"send 254 -t hop_test"`, where the parser takes `-t hop_test` as the **body** on the **AUTO** plane — it only works because AUTO dispatches on `is_team_peer`, while s23's `_desc` calls `send <id> -t` "the PRIMARY routing mechanism". A *prefix*-form patch would re-anchor s23; the suffix form does not.
**STORM BOUND MEASURED (§9/Q2) — no rate window warranted.** R4 worst case (10 members, full mesh, SF6, all addressing a departed teammate at once): **40 originated RREQs = exactly 4 per node** (1 probe + 3 escalations, uniform, no runaway), 88 relayed forwards, **31 F frames aired = 868 ms over 30 s = 2.9 % occupancy**, then all 10 fail loud at exactly +30 s. Zero `table_cap_hit`. My §10.4 estimate of ~30 was close; the true figure is 40.
⚠ **REPORTED, NOT FIXED:** `enqueue_data`'s DST_HASH selection is **plane-blind** — `key_hash_of_id(dst) || team_key_of_id(dst)` consults the **static** `_id_bind` first, so a team DM to team-local-id N can be stamped with a *static* node N's key hash and fail verify-on-delivery. Pre-existing and identical in shape pre-T1 (the `||` order was always static-first); T1 only widens the reachable `dst` set. This is §10.3's plane-blind-ledger class again.

**★★★ 2026-07-28 T0 (`§team-parity` groundwork — PURE REFACTOR, gated ALONE on byte-identity — QA GO; committed):** first slice of the team-plane static-parity arc (spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-team-plane-static-parity-routing-design.md`, owner ruled **full plane-parameterization**). Adds `hop_cap_for(bool team_plane)`, `protocol::team_hop_cap = 8`, `NodeConfig::team_hop_cap`, and threads an explicit plane through the six learn sites, the beacon packer entry-selection and the query path — **every team branch INERT**. **★ ALL 32 SCENARIOS BYTE-IDENTICAL**, native **895/68744/0 EXACT** (no test added or removed), boards **10/10 ΔRAM 0**.

★ **FIX 1 — a new `SendFailReason::reprovisioned`, and the argument for it is right.** `no_route` was rejected because it is *transient and invites a retry to the same `dst`* — but a reprovision changes the **network**, so the 8-bit dst now names a different node or nobody: retrying is a **mis-address, not a retry**. This is the only reason whose correct app action is *re-address, then resend*. Appended at the end per the `queue_full` contract rule (the numeric value is app-persistable). ★★ **`-Werror=switch` EARNED ITS KEEP THE DAY AFTER ADOPTION — it FAILED THE BUILD** until `console_json.cpp`, `fw_main.cpp` and the `ord()` guard in `test_console_json.cpp` were all handled. That is exactly the class the 2026-07-27 ruling was made for. The sim needs no change (verified: `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp` never switches on `SendFailReason` and its push emit drops `reason` entirely).
**Per-carrier push audit — pushes go ONLY where a local app future exists:** `_tx_queue` and `_pending_tx` push **iff the frame is this node's own DM**; the `_channel_buffer` row, `_flood[]` relay state and **transit legs** push nothing. ★ Answering the brief's explicit question: a queued **channel M the app originated via `send_channel` owes nothing** — its future is the re-offer slot, not the frame. s36 proves it live: `queued:3, flight:true` yields exactly **3** pushes (the flight is the `chan-content` broadcast). ⚠ Noted in-source, not bugs: a `-a` DM now gets **two** pushes (immediate `reprovisioned`, later `e2e_ack_timeout`), and `_push_ring` is 32 with drop-oldest so a worst-case gateway purge can evict earlier pushes.
⚠ **STILL OPEN (reported, C1-blocked):** a **re-offer slot** dropped by the purge strands its `channel_sent` future — but `channel_reoffer_fire` already drops it silently on *any* buffer eviction (`node_channel.cpp:676`), so the fix belongs there for all callers, not here.

So `on_init`'s `if (_node_id != 0)` guard at `node.cpp:341` — written for precisely the unprovisioned case — **was defeated by ordering**: the id-0 row had already been written. QA verified the line numbers. Also found: `src/firmware_commands.cpp:344` (`regen` while unprovisioned). ⇒ **two of the three sites of that identical call already had the guard; `set_identity` was the outlier.** (`activate_layer` writes `_node_id` directly at `node.cpp:608`, bypassing `set_identity`, and its own `id_bind_set` at `:643` already guards `!= 0`.) **No consumer depends on the row** — every reader either scans ids ≥17, rejects on `!= _node_id` (which IS 0), rejects on `> 0`, or short-circuits own-hash before the table.
⚠ **ORTHOGONAL, STILL OPEN:** `handle_h_query` (`:607`) and `request_resolve` (`:1398`) read `_node_id` directly, so an unprovisioned node still **answers an own-hash locate with 0** — the `node_join.cpp:324` "absurd responder_node_id=0 OFFER" family. Not subsumed by this fix.

**Coverage:** non-vacuity proven four ways — Fix-1 pre-fix 2 cases/4 assertions, predicate inverted 2/3, **over-broad (both guards removed) 3/4 including the s34 team guard**, Fix-2 pre-fix 4/4; all 8 new cases covered. ★★ **ARC RETROSPECTIVE — four distinct leaks, and every audit was larger than the brief.** (1) the team plane kept stale routes on a switch; (2) the team **channel** payload leaked via **four** carriers, not the one I named — a flood re-broadcasts from its own `fs.body` and never reads the buffer; (3) the **leaf axis** was the same disease on five carriers, and `src` mattered more than `leaf_id` (a survivor airs claiming `src=0`); (4) **`_team_keys` was cleared by nothing at all** — not even `join`/`create`/`leave` — a mis-addressing hazard, not a stale route. Plus `_channel_reoffer_pending`, cleared by nothing. **Every one was found by a coder's audit or a poison probe, never by re-reading.** Both axes now carry corpus coverage with first-hand-verified discrimination, and no relayed test-efficacy claim remains anywhere in the arc.

**★★★ 2026-07-27zg (QA — s34 CARRIER COVERAGE 1 → 3 sweeps, and the last relayed non-vacuity claim closed; uncommitted):** two loose ends from the carrier-purge arc, both QA's own work.
★ **s34 re-anchors `8741145b`/663 → `ce9a154f`/769**, 0 assertion failures, 3-run stable, +1 assert. It previously exercised **1 of the 5** team-axis sweeps (`rows:1` only) while s36 exercised all five on the reprovision axis. The switch is now timed at **348300 on purpose** — inside the window where X1's own 348000 team channel post is still IN FLIGHT (it does not air until ~358099) — giving **`rows:3, flight:true, reoffers:1`**. `flight` is the most consequential carrier: a frame one hop from the air.
⚠ **`floods` and `queued` stay NATIVE-TEST-ONLY on the team axis, and the reason is MEASURED, not assumed:** `floods` needs the switch AFTER a teammate's post is ingested (~359600) while `flight` needs it BEFORE X1's own post airs (~348300) — **mutually exclusive windows**. A two-switch-with-rejoin variant was tried and rejected: the rejoin shifts `_team_local_id` so the second window closes. `queued` needs a team channel M queued behind another, which `channel_min_interval_ms = 10000` prevents. Recorded in s34's `_desc` so nobody re-derives it.
★★ **THE LAST RELAYED "does the test work" CLAIM IS NOW FIRST-HAND.** 27ze's native non-vacuity was the coder's measurement. Restored by **content write, never `cp -a`**; `git diff` empty and native back to **887/68692/0**. ⇒ no relayed test-efficacy claims remain anywhere in this arc.
⚠ **A DSL trap worth the same billing as the `cmd_reply_contains` prefix one:** `script_emit_contains` matches the **raw** NDJSON substring, which has **no spaces** (`"flight":true`). **★★★★ 2026-07-27zf (`§sim-leave-verb` + `s36` — the reprovision blind spot CLOSED; corpus 31 → 32 — QA GO; uncommitted):** the sim gains a **`leave`** verb (SIM REPO, one file, +67 lines) and QA authored **`s36_reprovision_purges_carriers`** — **anchor `870926b0`/470**, 0 assertion failures, 3-run stable. **All 31 pre-existing scenarios BYTE-IDENTICAL** (QA re-ran from a from-scratch `lus`), so the verb is inert until used.
★★ **MY MEASUREMENT WAS RIGHT BUT UNDERSTATED — the blind spot was the WHOLE function.** I reported `purge_tx_carriers(reprovision)` at 0/31. Independently reconfirmed with a tracer: **0 entries in 1 857 425 events** — and **`clear_routing_state()` ITSELF also had 0**. So routes, `_id_bind`, `_deferred`, `gw_schedules`, `bridged_layers`, `_mobile_reg` and `clear_team_routing_state` were *all* unreachable from the corpus, not merely the carrier sweep. One verb closes all of it. (`purge_tx_carriers(team_switch)` = 1 entry, via s34's `team` verb — that axis has exactly one detector.)
★★★ **THE LEAK IS NOW OBSERVED, NOT INFERRED.** A poisoned build (the single `purge_tx_carriers(reprovision)` call removed) re-airs the channel payload **after** the reprovision: `TX DATA a0070001fd31016368616e2d636f6e74656e74` at 600185 — `6368616e2d636f6e74656e74` = **`chan-content`** — now stamped with the new `leaf_id` and `src=0`, while the three surviving queue items churn `no_route` → `send_deferred` → `send_deferred_giveup` ×3 (exactly the `queued:3`). **Fixed = 0 failures, poisoned = 5 failures.**
**All five carriers demonstrated:** one `leave` drops four at once — `reprovision_tx_purged{flight:true, queued:3, reoffers:1, rows:1}` (QA-reproduced verbatim) — and the fifth (`floods:1`) was driven separately by leaving a node mid-rebroadcast-backoff. The **gateway** case confirms the every-leaf span: `{flight:true, queued:1}` on `gw_4`.
★ **`leave` is the RIGHT and ONLY verb — a `provision`/`reprovision` sibling was proposed by me and correctly REFUSED.** `join`/`create` end in the *same* `clear_routing_state()`, so a sibling would fork a second path to one mechanism (U1) purely to add the NV/PHY surface deliberately excluded. The join-shaped half is available by **composition**, verified: `leave` → `join` → `join_claim_sent{proposed_node_id:85}` (a **fresh** id — the old one was denied) → `join_adopted` → a DM delivers. Deliberately not mirrored (stated in-source): NV (the sim has no `mrnv`), the PHY re-apply (a faithful copy would set `allowed_sf_bitmap = 0` and mute the node for the rest of the run), and DAD (`leave` *is* the `do_dad=false` call site — its absence is a mirror, and it keeps the verb RNG-free and byte-reproducible, same reason `team new` is refused).
⚠ **A CORRECTION TO MY OWN WORDING:** I called the sweep's `MR_EMIT` unconditional. **It is gated** on `rows || floods || queued || flight || reoffers`, so a *reached but empty* sweep emits nothing. ⇒ `event_count_min reprovision_tx_purged >= 1` proves "reached **with something to drop**", not merely "reached". s36 stages carriers precisely so the two coincide.
⚠ **AN ASSERT-ORDERING TRAP, found the hard way and now recorded in s36's `_desc`:** `cmd_reply_contains` matches `command` as a **PREFIX** and takes the **most-recent** match — so `"leave"` also matches `"leave 3"`. The malformed C2 probe **must fire before** the real `leave`, or assert 1 silently reads the ERROR reply and the scenario passes vacuously. (Same family as the `team 0` / `team 0x…` trap recorded for s34.)
**Honest assert labelling in s36:** 1–2 pin the *transport* and pass in a poisoned build; **3–4 are the durable mechanism pins**; 5–6 are behavioural (measured poisoned: 658 ms / 6 frames aired, `data_tx` 2, `send_deferred_giveup` 3) and carry the usual exact-count re-anchor fragility.
★★ **SIDE FINDING — pre-existing firmware inconsistency, reported NOT fixed, QA-verified at source.** `Node::set_identity()` (`node.cpp:61`) calls `id_bind_set(_node_id, _key_hash32, self, authoritative)` **unconditionally**, while `on_init` guards **the same call**: `if (_node_id != 0)` (`node.cpp:341`, *"node_id 0 is unprovisioned (no identity yet)"*). ⇒ every `set_identity(protocol::unjoined_node_id, …)` writes an **authoritative self-binding for the reserved id 0** — visible in s36 as `id_bind_set{node:0, source:"self", confidence:"authoritative"}` at 600002. On the reprovision path it is harmless **only because of the order** (`clear_routing_state()` wipes `_id_bind_n` on the very next line), but two other `set_identity(0)` sites have **no following wipe**: `forced_rejoin()` (`node_join.cpp:410/420` — and `node.h:272` says the heal *deliberately keeps* its routes) and `mobile_reset_registration()` (`node_mobile.cpp:250`). Those leave a live authoritative `{node_id:0, our key_hash32}` row in `_id_bind`. **No functional failure proven** — plausibly the same family as the already-noted *"kills the absurd responder_node_id=0 OFFER"* (`node_join.cpp:324`). **OPEN for the owner.**

**★★★★ 2026-07-27ze (`§clean-join-carriers` — ONE sweep, TWO axes: the leaf-axis twin of the team leak — QA GO; uncommitted):** the owner spotted that the team fix and the `clear_routing_state()` gap *"use the same mechanism"* and asked for both at once. Correct: `purge_team_channel_state()` was already **five sweeps with the team test at four single points** (the fifth, the re-offer orphan check, was already generic). Generalized to **`purge_tx_carriers(PurgeAxis)`** — `const bool all = (axis == reprovision)` **is** the second predicate, each test reading `all || <team test>`, so no branch duplicates a sweep. **+12 non-comment lines.** All 31 scenarios **BYTE-IDENTICAL**; native **881/68627/0 → 887/68692/0**.
★★★ **MY INSTRUCTION WOULD HAVE INTRODUCED A REGRESSION ON THREE ENVS — the coder caught it and QA confirmed at HEAD.** I said *"`clear_routing_state()`'s `_channel_buffer_n = 0` line should BECOME the call."* But `purge_team_channel_state()` lived inside `#if MR_FEAT_TEAM` with a **`{}` stub** (`node.h:287`), and the three `gateway_*` envs compile `MR_FEAT_TEAM 0` — **while `leave` IS dispatched on the gateway build** (`firmware_commands.cpp:675` sits *outside* the `#endif` that gates only `join`/`create`). So routing the wipe through a team-gated stub would have **silently stopped wiping the channel plane on exactly the profile that still runs the verb.** The function is now compiled on every profile (every field its predicates read is ungated).
★★ **SCOPE differs between the axes, not just the predicate.** The team purge was `*_active`-only; `clear_routing_state()` loops `_n_layers`. `_tx_queue`/`_pending_tx` are **not** channel-only — a gateway stages **bridged DMs on both leaves** — so an `_active`-only sweep would leave `_layers[1]` staged against a wiped `_id_bind`. Leaf span is axis-intrinsic; the function owns its own loop.
★★★ **AND `leaf_id` IS NOT EVEN THE WORST FIELD.** Both RTS builders also write `rin.src = _node_id` — and on this path `_node_id` is **already 0**, because `provision_apply_live` calls `reset_join_for_reprovision()` (which ends in `set_identity(unjoined_node_id, …)`) on the line **before** `clear_routing_state()` (`firmware_config.cpp:480-481`). ⇒ a survivor would air on the new leaf claiming **`src = 0`** (the reserved unprovisioned id) while its inner DATA still carried the **old** network's `origin`, and it would fly at the new leaf's SF/BW/CR since the PHY is live too. My brief had framed this as a leaf-nibble scope leak; it is worse.
**Team caller proven bit-identical THREE ways:** (a) algebraically — `all == false` reduces every predicate to `false || <original>`, the span reduces to `*_active`, and `flood_state_free(li,s)` with `li == active_layer_index()` *is* the 1-arg form; (b) **s34 is byte-identical and it is the ONLY scenario that executes the function** (probe-proven), its event line literally unchanged (`rows:1, kept:0`); (c) every pre-existing team-purge test passes untouched.
★★ **OWNER-ACCEPTED AS FINAL, 2026-07-27 ("all accepted") — DO NOT REOPEN.** The six already-packed stashes below stay unpurged on both axes. The reasoning is recorded in-source and reproduced here: their byte-0 leaf nibble froze at pack time, so a late fire carries the OLD leaf and is rejected by the new leaf's gate ⇒ **wasted airtime on the leaf just left, never mis-delivery into the one just joined** — a different failure class from the one this arc closed. ⚠ The one to watch if this is ever revisited is `_rts_duty_defer`, which can hold for **~1 hour**.
★ **A REAL DISTINCTION THE AUDIT FOUND — "already-packed" stashes, deliberately NOT purged.** Six further carriers hold **finished bytes** whose byte-0 leaf nibble froze at pack time: `_deferred_lbt[4]`, `_rts_duty_defer` (~1 h!), `_tx_stash[4]`, `_pending_offer`, `_h_forward[4]`, `_rreq_forward[4]`. A late fire therefore carries the **OLD** leaf and is rejected by the new leaf's gate ⇒ **wasted airtime on the leaf just left, never mis-delivery into the one just joined.** That is a different failure class, correctly out of scope (C1), documented in-source. ⚠ Noted honestly: `retry_stashed()` calls `_hal.tx` **unconditionally** (only the ack re-arm is guarded), so `_tx_stash` does re-transmit — but a DATA frame has no leaf nibble and needs a peer holding a matching `_pending_rx`, so it dies unheard. **Not the same class:** `_parked_sends` re-enters the **live** build path so it can never carry a stale stamp; `_pending_rx`/`_post_ack` are receive-side; `_pending_e2e_acks` self-expires into `send_failed{e2e_ack_timeout}`, which is the *correct* app signal; `_deferred` confirmed already cleared.
★ **FLIGHT COHERENCE — one real trap.** `awaiting_cts`/`awaiting_ack`/`retries_left`/`chosen_data_sf` live **inside** `PendingTx`, so `.reset()` takes them; there is no mirror flag to desync. **The exception is `_nack_wait_pending`**: its own handler is `flight_gen`-guarded, but **`layer_swap_blocked()` (`node.cpp:505`) reads the bare flag**, so a stale `true` would **deadlock a gateway's leaf-window swap**. Cleared via the existing `clear_nack_wait()` (U1), **reprovision axis only** — a team-axis drop is always an `m_broadcast`, which never awaits a CTS. **Timers** enumerated: the six `_pending_tx`-guarded ones fire inert; `kFloodRebcast` is now correctly cancelled by `flood_state_free` (★ the old `f = FloodState{}` line left it **armed**); `kChannelReoffer` by sweep 5.
★★ **COVERAGE — byte-identity sees 1 of 5 sweeps on 1 of 2 axes.** Killing the whole function moves **1/31** (only s34 — the positive control proving the chain is live). The **reprovision axis is 0/31**: an unconditional `MR_EMIT` on entry produced **0 events in 1.8 M** corpus events. Sweeps 2–5 and the new `flood_state_free` overload are **0/31 each**. ⇒ **"only the new tests"**, and they are non-vacuous: substituting the pre-slice body fails **3 of 6 new cases / 14 assertions**, hitting exactly `_tx_queue`, `_pending_tx`, `_channel_reoffer_pending`, the flood-timer cancel, the nack-wait coherence and the multi-leaf sweep. ⇒ **OWED, and it needs the SIM repo:** a `leave`/`join` verb in `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp` mirroring the new `team` verb (`reset_join_for_reprovision()` → set `cfg.leaf_id` → `clear_routing_state()`).
⚠ **OPEN RULING (in-source, pending):** the reprovision axis pushes **no per-frame `send_failed`** for dropped DMs, so a companion app's futures hang until their own timeout. It matches the pre-existing silent `_deferred_n = 0` beside it, and adding pushes would change the push stream.

**★★★★★ 2026-07-27zd (`§cts-len6-cr2` — ★★ RE-ANCHOR, ALL 31 SCENARIOS — the NAV-from-CTS "unfixable" site CLOSED — QA GO; uncommitted):** CTS byte 3 stops being an exact 8-bit `payload_len` and becomes **`[len6][cr2]`** — `len6 = ceil((inner+MAC)/4)` rounded **UP** (clamp 63), `cr2 = cr − 5` reusing the RTS's own `rts_cr_encode/decode` (U1, so there is no second convention). Consumer decodes `bytes = min(255, len6·4 + 9)` — **9 = `DATA_HDR_MAX_LEN`, retiring the flat `+13`** — and the peer's real CR. **RTS untouched**; no `wire_version` bump; no byte added. Spec: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-27-cts-len6-cr2-design.md`.
