⚠ **CORRECTION 2026-07-27 (MINE):** this table was headed "31-ROW" but listed only **30** — I omitted `s24_static_and_team_multihop`. Caught by the 27ze coder, QA-verified at **`74c6c7b8`/1354** (26w had `cdea58c7`; the event count is unchanged, so the CTS slice moved it like the rest). The row above completes the set.
★ **s23_mobile_team_multihop being byte-identical is FORENSIC CONFIRMATION, not luck:** its only CTS length is **20**, a multiple of 4, so `ceil(20/4)=5` re-encodes to `(5<<2)|0 = 20` — the identical byte. QA verified.
★★ **WIRE PROOF — QA-reproduced independently.** In s19 the symmetric difference of every `tx.hex` in the corpus is **exclusively 20 four-byte cmd-`0x2` frames**; nothing else on the wire changed anywhere. Byte-3 arithmetic checks out and rounds UP: `19 → 0x14` (`ceil(19/4)=5`, `5<<2=20`) and `23 → 0x18` (`6<<2=24`). The coder reports the same exclusivity in **21 of 31** scenarios; the other 10 are event-count movers from contention re-ordering (s18 `rx` +566, `collision` +86, `tx` +76, **no emit type gained or lost**), and **0 assertion failures in all 31 before and after**.
★ **WHY the streams moved at all — and it is NOT "no change" as the spec implied.** Over every legal `payload_len` the reservation moves **−4 to −1 bytes**: a systematic 1–4 B *reduction* (the old `+13` over-reserved by more than quantization adds back), i.e. 20–82 ms less NAV hold. Still always **≥** the true frame length. That shortened hold is the whole stream delta.
★★★ **THREE PREMISES OF MINE DISPROVEN — two of them in the spec I wrote.** (a) **The spec's shortfall figures were 25–30% TOO LARGE**: they computed "needed" as `payload_len + 13` at the peer CR, **double-counting the very header fudge this slice retires**. Corrected: SF11/38 **−180 ms** (not −245) · SF11/120 **−549** (not −614) · SF12/120 **−1196** (not −1327). Direction and conclusion unchanged; the spec and `protocol.md` are corrected. (b) **"the full legal range 0..241" was wrong** — 241 caps the *inner buffer*, but `payload_len` is inner+**MAC**, so it legally reaches **249** under CRYPTED; the sweep runs 0..255. (c) ★ **I called `CTS_LEN6_MAX` "belt-and-braces". It is LOAD-BEARING:** `r.payload_len` is an unauthenticated wire byte, and unclamped `ceil(255/4) = 64` shifts out of the 6-bit field leaving `byte3 == cr2`, **which is 0 at cr5** — colliding with the "no hint" sentinel. (The sentinel itself holds for a different reason than I gave: `pack_cts` emits byte 3 only when `payload_len != 0`, so `len6 ≥ 1` ⇒ `byte3 ≥ 4`. Empirically zero byte-3==0 CTSes across all 31.)
★ **AND A PRE-EXISTING SAFETY BUG FOUND EN ROUTE:** the old M6 clamp (`payload_len → 241`) **under-reserved a real 255-B CRYPTED frame by up to 5 B** — in the one direction NAV must never fail. Replaced with a byte-count clamp at `lora_max_frame_bytes`, with the DoS ceiling proven *identical* (a forged hint now buys exactly what an absent hint already buys).
**Invariant sweep (the whole scheme rests on it):** cr 5..8 × `payload_len` 0..255 × APP × CRYPTED, ground truth from a **real `pack_data` frame** so a header-layout change fails the test — **40306 assertions**, `decoded ≥ true_bytes` and `≤ +4` for every physically-sendable frame. ★ It found a boundary I did not anticipate: at `payload_len` 253..255 the clamp makes the decoded length point *down* (to 252) — safe and documented, since such a frame is ≥261 B and unsendable, reachable only via a forged RTS byte which the byte-count `min()` already pins to a full-frame reservation.
**NAV table (cr5 overhearer, cr8 peer, BW250k, RSF8) — every hole reaches 0:** SF10/38 −103→**0** · SF11/38 −180→**0** · SF11/120 −549→**0** · SF12/38 −410→**0** · SF12/120 −1196→**0**.
★★ **COVERAGE — the CR-sizing is INVISIBLE to the corpus, exactly as predicted.** Probes: encode **31/31** · length decode **11/31** · byte count **11/31** · fresh-CTS producer **31/31** · **CR on the wire only 2/31** (s32, s33) · ★ **CR used to size NAV: 0/31**, so the sweep + the new `test_dual_layer` test are its **sole** detectors. The sharp finding: s32/s33 *do* carry cr8 on CTSes that third nodes receive, yet perturbing the reservation there has **no observable stream effect** — the 11 scenarios where it *is* observable are uniform-cr5, where the decoded CR equals `active_cr()` anyway. ⇒ **OWED: a scenario with mixed CR *plus* a third node whose own CR differs from the cleared sender's *plus* contending traffic in that window.** A second honest gap: **CTS producer B** (the re-CTS / `_pending_rx` path) is **0/31 AND untested natively** — correct by construction, detected by nothing.
⚠⚠ **PROCESS: THE CODER CONTAMINATED THE SHARED `lus` BUILD TREE AND CAUGHT IT — a new standing hazard.** It restored probe files with **`cp -a`, which preserves mtimes**, so `/home/staszek/lora-universal-simulator/build` silently kept **stale poisoned objects** and emitted wrong streams. It found it, repaired the tree, and re-ran every probe in isolated trees with forced-fresh mtimes. ★ **QA did not trust the repair: the anchor table above comes from a `rm -rf build` FROM-SCRATCH rebuild.** ⇒ **Standing rule to add to the gate method: never restore a probed file with `cp -a`/`cp -p`; restore with a content write (or `touch` after), and when a shared build tree may have seen a probe, rebuild it from scratch rather than incrementally.**
⚠ **`node_mac_rx.cpp`'s anti-spam-ledger CTS term: its comment's REASON changed, not its status.** Byte 3's CR belongs to `c.rx_id` (the node being cleared), while that term bills the CTS *frame*, whose sender is `c.tx_id` — **wrong node; using byte 3 there would swap one error for another.** Still open, now correctly explained in-source.
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE:** native **878/27292/0 → 881/68627/0** (+3 cases / +41335 assertions, the 40306-assertion sweep dominating) · **31/31 run, 0 assertion failures each**, anchors above from a from-scratch build · `sizeof(Node)` **220592** proven positively · boards **10/10 SUCCESS, ΔRAM +0 on every one of the ten**, ΔFlash +76…+88 B uniform (three extra stores + the `min`) · warning text set **identical**, `-Wswitch` zero · sim 27 binaries pass, `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` 3 → 3. **`frames.md` + `protocol.md` §2 + the spec's figures corrected by QA.**

**★★★★ 2026-07-27zc (`§clean-team-channel` — the team channel leak CLOSED, and it was FOUR routes not one — QA GO; uncommitted):** closes the residual 27zb left open. **All 30 scenarios BYTE-IDENTICAL**; native **872/27232/0 → 878/27292/0** (+6 cases / +60 assertions).
★★★ **MY BRIEF WOULD HAVE PRODUCED A QUARTER FIX.** I named one carrier — a buffered row served on a re-offer or `CHANNEL_PULL`. The payload is **copied forward at every stage** and each stage emits independently, so `do_data_tx`'s re-stamp reaches it through **four**: (1) the `_channel_buffer` row; (2) ★ **`_flood[]` state** — `flood_rebroadcast_fire` re-floods from **`fs.body`**, its own cached copy, and **never reads the buffer** (QA verified at `node_channel.cpp:996`: `enqueue_flood_m(fs.channel_id, fs.flavor, fs.id, fs.body, …)`), so an armed backoff fires up to `flood_backoff_ms` *after* the switch; (3) a staged **`_tx_queue`** TxItem, stamped at TX time i.e. after the switch; (4) the **`_pending_tx`** flight, one frame from the air. All four are must-scrub. ⚠ **And the gate could never have caught the shortfall** — see coverage.
★ **`_channel_reoffer_pending` — a fifth structure, cleared by NOTHING today** (not `clear_routing_state`, not `clear_learned_state`). It cannot re-emit (`channel_reoffer_fire` bails at `find < 0`) but it holds 1 of **4** slots until its 10 s+jitter timer fires, so `team new` + a prompt post can hit `channel_reoffer_table_full` and lose coverage repair. Now scrubbed.
★★ **`team 0` (LEAVE) IS THE WORSE CASE, not the symmetric one.** With `team_id == 0`, `pack_m` writes `team_id` 0 onto a team-flavored frame and `same_team(0)` is false everywhere ⇒ it parses as a **plain leaf message ingested by every static node on the leaf** — a containment breach, not a leak into one team. `do_data_tx` now **REFUSES** it outright (C2), deliberately **not** `MR_FEAT_TEAM`-gated: a `!MR_FEAT_TEAM` gateway can buffer a malformed flavor-team/`team_id`-0 row off the air, and the guard makes it unservable rather than re-broadcast (costs the three `gateway_*` envs +92 B — worth it).
**Predicate, and QA's reasoning CONFIRMED at source:** `e.team_id != 0 || (e.flavor & channel_flavor_team)` — **no old-id comparison needed**. The ingest gate (`node_channel.cpp:192`) requires `_cfg.is_mobile && _cfg.team_id == m.team_id` and origination (`:302`) is gated on `_cfg.team_id != 0`; both stamp the team live at that instant, and `set_team_id()` is the **only** live writer of `_cfg.team_id` (`fw_main.cpp:603` writes it pre-`on_init`, when every table is empty). ⇒ every team row at a switch belongs to the team being left. Both signals are OR'd because `team_id` is the semantic scope while the **flavor bit** is what `do_data_tx` keys the re-stamp on — the OR means no row can be team-flavored on the wire yet survive here as "leaf". Genuine leaf rows have neither and **survive** (proven three ways in tests, plus the pre-existing `channel_buffer_count() == 1` assertion in the static-plane-survives test stays green).
**Audit — the structures deliberately NOT touched, each with its reason:** `_channel_pull_pending` is harmless **by construction** (its ids are *disjoint* from buffered rows — scheduled only on `find < 0`, cancelled on ingest — so there is literally nothing to scrub) · `_channel_pull_recent` is **kept on purpose**: it is pure suppression, and keeping it *prevents* re-pulling what we just dropped · `_per_origin_channel` stays per 27zb's ruling (bare-origin key, no plane bit, TTL'd, suppress-only) ⚠ with a noted knock-on: `do_send_channel`'s self-cap counts **buffer** rows, so the purge *relaxes* it — intended, and the untouched `_last_channel_origin_ms` keeps the 10 s floor as backstop · `seen_by[32]` is per-row and whole-row drops keep the id-spaces unmixed · the BCN digest's `dirty`/`bcn_ad_count` ride the row and an already-aired ad fails **closed** (`handle_channel_pull` serves only what it finds) · inbox `record_channel` rows are durable app history, never a re-emit source — leaving team A must not erase its messages.
★★ **COVERAGE — THE CORPUS IS TOTALLY BLIND TO THE PURGE, PROVEN.** A probe that made the purge **maximally destructive** (drop *every* buffer row, free *every* flood, clear the *whole* tx queue) moved **0 of 30**. Not a weak probe — the site is **dead to the corpus**, because its only callers are `src/firmware_config.cpp:212/658`, which the sim does not compile. ⇒ byte-identity says **nothing** about the purge; the **6 new tests are the entire detector** (per-site probes: buffer 3 tests/13 assertions RED, flood/tx-queue/in-flight/re-offer/C2-guard 1 each). ★ **Contrast the re-stamp line, which IS corpus-executed**: poisoning it (`_cfg.team_id + 7`) moved **3/30** (s22_mobile_team, s28_mixed_team_channels, s29_mixed_leaf_team) ⇒ there the 30/30 byte-identity **is** meaningful — it proves the new C2 guard never fires on a live team emit and the stamp is unchanged.
★ **A FALSE INVARIANT RETIRED (V1).** `node_mac.cpp:1222` had claimed *"we only EMIT our own team's messages … so `_cfg.team_id` is always the right one."* That is false for exactly one case — a team switch — because the ingest match held when the payload **arrived**, not when it is **re-emitted**. The comment now states the real invariant *and* the purge that enforces it, with the C2 guard making it structural rather than asserted.
⚠ **PRE-EXISTING, NOT FIXED (C1 — a different bug on the leaf axis):** `clear_routing_state()` wipes the channel buffer on `join`/`create`/`leave` but leaves `_tx_queue` / `_pending_tx` / `_channel_reoffer_pending`, so a staged channel M survives a **network** reprovision and is re-stamped with the new `leaf_id`. Documented in-code at the definition. **OPEN.**
**Design:** `Node::purge_team_channel_state()` (`node_channel.cpp:789`, audit block at `:718`), declared `node.h:284` with a `!MR_FEAT_TEAM` inert stub, called from `set_team_id()` (`node.cpp:401`). **Deliberately NOT folded into `clear_team_routing_state()`** — its other caller `clear_routing_state()` full-wipes the channel plane one line later, so folding would add dead work *and* put a tx-queue mutation on the static reprovision path that never had one. Operates on `_active` rather than a `_layers[]` loop (the channel plane is `_active`-scoped and a team-row-holding node is single-layer by Principle 11), which is what lets it **reuse** `channel_buffer_find()` / `flood_state_free()` instead of re-implementing them (U1).
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE:** native **878/27292/0** · **30/30 `cmp`-identical**, 0 assertion failures each · `sizeof(Node)` **220592** proven positively (fires at 220593) · boards **10/10 SUCCESS, ΔRAM 0 on all ten**; flash +448…+576 on the seven team-capable envs and only **+92/+96** on the three `gateway_*` (`MR_FEAT_TEAM 0`) = the purge stubs to nothing, leaving just the un-gated C2 guard · `-Wswitch` 0, MeshRoute warning set **44 → 44 `diff`-identical** · sim 27 binaries pass, warnings 3 → 3. **`protocol.md` §13 updated by QA.** ⚠ **Operational: QA reclaimed 22 GB** of completed agents' build trees from this session's scratchpad (5.4 G → **27 G free**, 97% → 83%); the 24 dispatch briefs at the scratchpad root are preserved.

**★★★★ 2026-07-27zb (`§clean-team` — a team switch now CLEARS the team plane — QA GO; uncommitted):** owner bench report: *"when creating a new team (`team new`) or joining (`team <id>`), existing team routes need to be cleared."* **All 30 scenarios BYTE-IDENTICAL** (nothing re-anchors); native **867/27149/0 → 872/27232/0** (+5 cases / +83 assertions).
★ **The mechanism already existed and named this exact hazard — the bug was an ASYMMETRY.** `Node::clear_routing_state()` (`node.cpp:348`) already wiped the team plane, commenting *"a reprovision may change team_id; a stale `_rt_team` + `_team_peer` bit would **SHADOW the fresh static plane** (`rt_find` dispatches on `is_team_peer` with **no `_rt` fallback**)."* The STATIC reprovision verbs call it (`firmware_config.cpp:475`); the team-plane equivalents **did not**.
★★★ **THE AUDIT WAS THE DELIVERABLE, AND IT WAS FAR BIGGER THAN THE 3 KNOWN LINES.** `node.h` has **7** `#if MR_FEAT_TEAM` state groups; three were cleared, and **two were cleared by NOTHING AT ALL** — not `clear_routing_state`, not `clear_learned_state`:
| team state | verdict |
|---|---|
| `_rt_team`/`_rt_team_count`, `_team_peer[32]`, `_team_liveness(_n)` | were cleared ✓ |
| ★ **`_team_keys[16]`/`_team_keys_n`** (`node.h:1412-13`) | **NEVER cleared — MIS-ADDRESSING hazard** |
| **`_rreq_seen_team`/`_rreq_last_team`** (`:1417-20`) | **NEVER cleared** (TTL-bounded) |
| `_team_local_id`, `_team_dad_pending` | identity, handled by `set_team_local_id(0)` ✓ |
★ **QA's hypothesis DISPROVED, in the worse direction.** I guessed the team key cache lived in `_id_bind` (so a team-only clear would miss it while the full clear caught it). Wrong: **`_team_keys` is its own array and NO clear reached it** — verified at HEAD, where `_team_keys_n = 0` appears **zero** times. ⇒ the stale-key gap was **wider than the team switch**: `join`/`create`/`leave` left it stale too. `is_team_peer` is **not** cover for it — `node_beacon.cpp:848` sets that bit from a multi-hop DV entry with **no key**, and at `:758` the bit is set *before* `:766`'s L2a mediation reads the cache, so a new teammate reusing an old team id draws a **spurious mediated DENY on its first beacon**. Consumers of a stale entry: `node_mac.cpp:94` (wrong DST_HASH) and `node_hashlocate.cpp:988/1019` (hash → **wrong teammate**).
★★ **A SECOND LIVE TEAM-SWITCH PATH QA NEVER NAMED: `cfg set team_id`** (`firmware_config.cpp:207`). Verified at HEAD: it was `lc.team_id = strtoul(...); b.team_id = lc.team_id;` — it cleared **nothing, not even the stale team-DAD id**, so it was *worse* than `handle_team`. Now routed through the same core switch, and `b.team_local_id` is re-read after it (else a reboot resurrects the OLD team's id as **confirmed** on the new team).
**Design:** `Node::clear_team_routing_state()` (all 5 groups) + **`bool Node::set_team_id(uint32_t)`** — the single core entry point for every live team change (`team new` / `team <id>` / `team 0` / `cfg set team_id`): no-op → `false`; else clear plane → `set_team_local_id(0)` → assign. Not `#if`-forked (both callees stub inert) so the gateway build is identical by construction. **Boot keeps its direct `cfg.team_id = nv.team_id` and must NOT clear** — nothing is stale at boot and the persisted DAD id loads right after. ★ **The STATIC plane is deliberately untouched** — a homed mobile changing team keeps its routes, id-bindings, gateway schedules, hosted mobiles and home registration; only `join`/`create`/`leave` wipe both. **A test pins that survival**, which is what stops a future "simplification" to the full wipe.
⚠ **DEVIATION — `clear_routing_state()` is NOT behaviour-identical, contrary to my brief.** I asked the coder to verify it would be; it isn't, *because* the extracted helper now also clears `_team_keys` + the team RREQ rings. That is the audit paying off: one fix, both callers.
★ **ONE LEAK DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED — OPEN FOR THE OWNER.** Team-flavored `_channel_buffer` rows are not purged, and `node_mac.cpp:1222` **re-stamps** emitted team M-frames with the *current* `team_id` — so an old team's buffered row, served on a re-offer or `CHANNEL_PULL`, is **re-broadcast into the new team**. A count reset would kill valid leaf rows, so it needs selective compaction. Flagged in-source. (Also deliberately left, all documented: parked `Plane::TEAM` sends and the plane-blind ledgers `_seen_origins`/`_per_origin_channel`/`_hash_query_seen`/`_mediated_recent` — TTL-windowed, all failing in the *suppress* direction, and clearing them would reach into the static plane. Inbox `team_id` rows are a receive log, correctly attributed, not state.)
★★ **COVERAGE IS WORSE THAN "CORPUS-INERT", AND THE SCENARIO FIX IS BLOCKED.** An `MR_EMIT` tracer in all three functions fired **0 times across all 30 scenarios** while a control tracer (`team_dad_claim`) showed 8 in the same stream ⇒ the sites are **provably unreached**, because their only callers live in `src/`, which the sim does not compile. **Native tests are the ENTIRE detector** (non-vacuity: neutering the clear fails 5 cases/22 assertions; reverting only the two newly-found tables fails 4/10). ⇒ **OWED, and it needs a SIM change first: the orchestrator has no `team` verb** (its command set is `join`/`send*`/`reqpubkey`; anything else → `"ERROR: unparsed command"`). Recipe once added (~5 lines in `NodeRuntime::onCommand` calling `set_team_id` + `team_dad_fire`, mirroring `handle_team`): derive **`s25_two_team_separation`**, make X1 homed for static-survival cover, `X1 send_hash …` at t=300s (resolves from `_team_keys`, no H flood) → **`X1 team 0x22222222`** at t=360s → the same send at t=540s must now **emit an H flood** — the sharp discriminator, since a "X3 didn't receive it" assert would also pass via the wire team gate.
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE:** native **872/27232/0** · **30/30 `cmp`-identical**, 0 assertion failures each · `sizeof(Node)` **220592** · boards **10/10 SUCCESS both passes, ΔRAM 0 on all ten**, ΔFlash +32…+176 · `-Wswitch` 0 on all 10 (the census md5 delta was proven benign — recompiling `node.cpp` in both trees gave identical warnings modulo a +66-line shift of a pre-existing `-Warray-bounds` site) · sim 27 binaries pass, warnings 3→3. **`protocol.md` §13 updated by QA.** ⚠ **Operational: the scratch volume hit 100% mid-gate**; QA reclaimed 2.2 GB of superseded run directories (now 5.5 G free at 97%) — future gates should keep only the current baseline run.

**★★★★★ 2026-07-27za (`§rts-cr-overhear` — the wrong-CR sizing class CLOSED, 6 sites + 3 proven unfixable — QA GO; uncommitted):** finishes what 27z started. **NEW ANCHORS: `s32_dual_cr_gateway` `c7bd498c` → `0242f709`/2266 (event count UNCHANGED — exactly 2 records differ) · `s33_mixed_cr_channel_overhear` `8bf87a0f` → `1a9a3cb0`/2845 (NEW scenario, QA-authored — see below).** The other **28 of 30** are `cmp`-identical, 0 assertion failures each.
★★ **A NEW SCENARIO UNBLOCKED THE WHOLE SLICE.** 27z deferred the two overhear-retune sites for one reason: *"no corpus scenario overhears a channel flood from a different-CR sender, so the conversion would be unvalidatable."* QA therefore authored **`s33_mixed_cr_channel_overhear`** — s32's dual-CR gateway topology plus gateway-originated channel posts, so an M_BROADCAST/FLOOD flies on a **cr8** layer while the overhearing leaves sit at **cr5**. It yields **36 overhear arms of which 23 have a cr8 sender** (node ids 119 = gw_4's layer-5, 120 = gw_5's layer-4). ⇒ **the deferral was a coverage problem, not a design one, and the cure was a scenario.**
**Result, QA-reproduced from its own streams:** `channel_msg_received` **26 → 35** · `channel_msg_overheard` **17 → 26** · `channel_pull_sent` **10 → 1** — a clean 1:1: nine frames the overhearers now catch are nine repair pulls that never happen (corroborated: `push[channel_recv]` 18 → 27, `tx_lbt_defer` 17 → 7, `collision` 8 → 4). ★ **AIRTIME: +1.0 % on-air (23679 → 23924 ms) for +35 % delivery ⇒ 911 → 683 ms per delivered message, a 25 % efficiency gain.** (Contrast 27z, which *cut* s32's on-air 55 %: 31330 → 14070 ms. Both directions are correct — 27z removed a retry storm; this one buys reception with slightly more air.)
★ **The ledger fix is an ACCOUNTABILITY fix, not a timing one.** `node_mac_rx.cpp` bills *"the dominant airtime a sender imposes on us"*, and costing a peer's frame at OUR CR **under-billed** every heavier-coded sender against `originator_airtime_share`. s32 shows it exactly: the two cr8 senders' `orig_airtime_ms` **rise** 600 → 821 and 320 → 443, the four cr5 senders untouched.
★★★ **SEVENTH INSTANCE OF THE SWEEP-SCOPE META-BUG — MINE, and the sharpest yet: the faulty scope was an INDIRECTION.** I published what I called an audit of *"EVERY `active_cr()` use in `lib/core`"* — 26 hits, each classified. It missed `node_mac_rx.cpp:64`, the **RTS half of the very ledger I singled out**, because that site reaches `active_cr()` *inside* `airtime_routing_ms()` (`node_mac.cpp:44`) and is therefore invisible to a grep for `active_cr()`. Billing the ledger's DATA half at the sender's CR and its RTS half at ours would have been incoherent. The coder found it by sweeping all 21 `airtime_routing_ms` sites instead. **Prior flavours: directory scope (26n) · flag scope — `-Wswitch` is blind behind `default:` (26s) · path form — absolute vs relative (26t) · build profile — `#if MR_N_LAYERS>=2` (26x). Now: a helper that hides the call. An audit that LOOKS exhaustive because it enumerates and classifies every hit is still only as good as its pattern.**
★★ **THE ONE SITE THAT CANNOT BE FIXED — and it fails in the DANGEROUS direction.** `nav_duration_cts`'s two `handle_cts` callers must size a third party's DATA at that party's CR, and **the CTS has zero free bits** (verified at `pack_cts`: nibble fully spent on `(sf−5)<<1 | already_received`; `tx_id`/`rx_id`/`payload_len` whole bytes; `parse_cts` accepts exactly 3 or 4 B). Against a heavier-CR peer the overhearer **UNDER-reserves and releases NAV while the DATA is still on the air** — i.e. the hidden-terminal collision NAV exists to prevent, failing in the one case it is for. Measured (cr5 overhearer, cr8 peer, BW250k): SF11/38 B reserves **690 ms for a 935 ms frame = −245 ms** (the same order as 27z's 246 ms DATA-wait hole); SF11/120 B −614 ms; **SF12/120 B −1327 ms**. Bounded only by LBT's physical backstop and the `payload_len==0` 255-B branch — but a NAV-enabled CTS *does* carry `payload_len`, so **the common case is exact and fully exposed**. Cost to close: a 5th conditional CTS byte (flag day, taxes every CTS in every mesh) or reclaiming the load-bearing `already_received`. **Documented at the helper and at both callers; OPEN for the owner.**
**Coverage — probes, honest:** FLOOD retune **5/30** move (validated by s33) · ledger DATA **24/30** (validated by s32+s33) · M_BCAST retune **5/30** move but **0/30 value-coverage** (executes 677× yet every sender is cr5) · ledger RTS **0/30** · NAV-from-RTS **0/30** ⇒ **native tests are the sole detector for three of the six.** Two probes moved **0/30 and the sites are genuinely DEAD**, not weakly probed: `reserve_yield_enable` and `flood_yield_grab_enable` are both compile-time `0` and the corpus emits zero `reserve_yield` events. **Owed: a scenario where a cr8 node answers a `CHANNEL_PULL`.** Closed accounting — decoding `cr_adv` from every RTS's wire hex: **8326 frames, 8312 cr5, 14 cr8** (10 FLOOD → the s33 win; 4 UNICAST+RELAY → exactly the 4 changed ledger records).
⚠ **Two declared deviations, both accepted:** the coder also converted `nav_duration_cts`'s **flood-yield** caller (`node_mac_rx.cpp:163`) — which my brief said to leave — because it holds an **RTS**, not a CTS; it is behind `flood_yield_grab_enable == 0` so it is provably inert today and becomes correct when Part B is enabled. And it converted the grep-invisible `:64` above. Both reported loudly, both backed by probes.
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE:** native **863/26770/0 → 867/27149/0** (+4 cases / +379 assertions; non-vacuity proven — reverting all six conversions fails 4 cases / 65 assertions) · **28/30 `cmp`-identical + s32/s33 re-anchored**, 0 assertion failures each, 3-run stable · `sizeof(Node)` **220592 unchanged** (a parameter is not a member), proven positively · boards **10/10 SUCCESS**, **ΔRAM 0 on all ten**, flash +48…+96 B · `-Wswitch` 0→0, per-env warning census md5 identical on all 10 · sim 27 binaries pass, `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` 3→3 · s18 delivery breakdown byte-identical (99/113 = 88%). **Docs updated by QA: `protocol.md` §2's deferral block rewritten to record what closed, the measured numbers, and the NAV-from-CTS dead end with its danger direction.**

**★★★★★ 2026-07-27z (`§rts-cr` — the RTS advertises the SENDER's coding rate — QA GO; uncommitted):** ★★ **THE SLICE THAT FINALLY CLOSED s32.** A **wire change with NO `wire_version` bump** (owner: *"we do not bump wire version, meshroute is not yet deployed"*): the RTS's **last two reserved bits** now carry `cr_adv` = `cr − 5` (byte 3 b0 = HIGH, byte 5 b0 = LOW), a **total** 2-bit mapping over CR 5..8 with **no "not advertised" state**. RTS stays 7 B / 9 B / 43 B; CTS untouched. Receiver stashes it on `PendingRx.sender_cr` and `start_pending_rx_expiry` sizes the DATA term from **that** instead of `active_cr()`.
★★★ **RESULT — the bug found in 26y is FIXED and s32's long-broken deliveries now work.** **s32 `68753f54`/3015 → `c7bd498c`/2266** (−749 events, all retry churn that no longer happens): `data_rx_timeout` **26 → 0**, `peer_suspect_mark` 22→0, `path_cascade_exhausted`/`data_ack_giveup` 1→0, RTS/CTS/DATA attempts 30→6 each, `delivered` 2→4. ★ **QA verified both cross-layer joiner DMs DELIVER from its own stream** — `cross-l4-to-l5-joiner` at t=137362 (origin 1 → dst 116) and `cross-l5-to-l4-joiner` at t=200127 (origin 101 → dst 113). ⇒ **the two assertions removed in 26v are RESTORED**, plus a new `data_rx_timeout == 0`; s32 now carries **6** assertions, 0 failures, 3-run stable, and pins BOTH the per-layer CR retune AND the mixed-CR RX-window sizing. (Assertion edits are stream-neutral — the anchor is unchanged by them.)
★ **The magnitudes I briefed were wrong; the shape was right.** Measured from the failing frame's own hex (`payload_len=38`, SF11/BW250k): the receiver armed **607 ms** for a DATA needing **853 ms** — a **246 ms shortfall** — and abandoned it **130 ms before it completed** (`gw_4 data_tx 136498` → before: `l5_j1 data_rx_timeout 137192`; after: `l5_j1 data_rx 137322`). My brief said 746/975, which was a different payload size. ★ **And the starvation was SYMMETRIC, not gateway-4-only as I claimed** — gw_5's cr8 layer-4 leaf starved the layer-4 nodes identically (`l4_j1` 6, `l4_seed` 6 of the 26 timeouts).
**Coverage — honest, and the corpus is nearly blind to the rule itself.** Direct census: of **8299 RTS frames** corpus-wide, **8297 advertise cr5** (code 0) and **2 advertise cr8**, both in s32. Probes: **P1a** (poison `tx_rts`'s advert) moves **29/29** ⇒ the advert site executes everywhere and is load-bearing; **P2** (revert the sizing to `active_cr()`) and **P3** (force the decode to cr5) each move **exactly 1/29** and are **byte-identical to each other** ⇒ decode ≡ consume, chain fully attributed. ⇒ **the regression detectors are the two new native tests**, both proven non-vacuous (reverting the consume fails **16** assertions — the 4 explicit + 12 off-diagonal of a 4×4 CR sweep, identity cases correctly still passing; killing the pack fails 4 cases / 33 assertions). ★ Byte-identity is unusually strong evidence here for a different reason: `tx` events carry the raw frame **`hex`**, so 28/29 proves the **wire bytes are literally unchanged**, not merely the behaviour.
★ **NEW FINDING — the M_BROADCAST/FLOOD advert is currently WRITE-ONLY, deliberately.** Probe **P1b** poisoned `tx_m_broadcast_rts`'s advert: **7/29 scenarios move but ONLY in the `hex`/`pkt` fields, with zero event-count change** — 854 flood/M-BCAST RTS frames carry the field and **nothing reads it**, because those paths `return` before creating a `PendingRx`. The consumers that *should* read it are the two **overhear-retune** windows (`node_mac_rx.cpp:155`, `:186`), which still use `active_cr()` — **the same bug class**. Not converted: the fix is one token, but **no corpus scenario overhears a flood from a different-CR sender**, so it would be unvalidatable. Marked in-source at both sites with the exact fix and the reason. **Owner's call.**
⚠ **Also deferred + documented in-source:** the **ACK-timeout** site (`node_mac.cpp:1287`) — its dominant term is our *own* DATA where `active_cr()` is correct; only the small `airtime_routing_ms(3)` ACK term is the peer's, and in the gateway-high-CR shape it **over**-waits (fail-safe). Closing it needs a peer-CR channel **the CTS cannot provide** — QA verified `pack_cts` has **zero** free bits (nibble = `(sf−5)<<1 | already_received`; `tx_id`/`rx_id`/`payload_len` are full bytes; `parse_cts` accepts exactly 3 or 4 B).
★ **A coder self-correction worth keeping:** it first wrote in-source that the global `radio_cr` "has NO range check anywhere" — **false**. `cfg set cr` refuses outside 5..8 fail-loud and `gateway cr0=/cr1=` is validated via `GwValErr::bad_cr` **before** the NV write. The real gap is narrow and **pre-existing**: the NV load (`fw_main.cpp:597`, `cfg.radio_cr = nv.cr` verbatim), the sim's `map_cr`, and the public `Node::set_radio_cfg()`. Not worsened here, not fixed.
**Security note (bounded, pre-existing shape):** the advertised CR is attacker-controlled but can only make a receiver **over**-wait (≤1.6× on the payload term), inflating its `_pending_rx` hold and BUSY_RX `busy_for`. It cannot *shorten* a window. Same class the code already bounds — `start_pending_rx_expiry` already clamps `payload_len` for exactly this reason ("M6: so a forged 255 can't inflate the pending-RX expiry"). No new clamp added.
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE:** native **858/26533/0 → 863/26770/0** (+5 cases / +237 assertions: 3 codec + 2 behavioural) · **28/29 `cmp`-identical + s32 re-anchored `c7bd498c`/2266**, 0 assertion failures each, 3-run stable · ★ **`sizeof(Node)` UNCHANGED at 220592** — the `uint8_t` landed in `PendingRx`'s existing 3-byte hole before `set_at_ms`, so `sizeof(PendingRx)` stays **32** and **ΔRAM = 0 on all ten boards** (no `static_assert` edit needed; proven positively and shown to bite at 220593) · boards **10/10 SUCCESS** with `-Werror=switch` active, flash +52…+144 B/env · per-env warning census md5 **identical on all 10** · sim 27 binaries pass, `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` 3 → 3. **Docs updated by QA: `frames.md` (the two byte rows + a `cr_adv` line + the flag-space paragraph, which now reads "RTS has ZERO free bits") and `protocol.md` §2 (the mechanism, the measured shortfall, both deferrals and the fleet assumption).**

**★★★★ 2026-07-27y (owner finding #3 — CR REMOVED from the gateway window-split weighting — QA GO; uncommitted):** owner ruling 2026-07-27: *"window split needs to take parameters which modify airtime — SF and BW! However CR should be ignored (clearly marked in code) — different CR settings are compatible."* **The semantic diff is 3 lines** (`lib/core/node.cpp`: `eff_cr` deleted, both layers now weigh at the global `radio_cr`), wrapped in a comment block recording the rule, the rationale, the deliberate non-goal and the measured residual. ★ **ONLY `s32` MOVED: `54643800`/3100 → `68753f54`/3015**; the other 28 are `cmp`-identical at the 26w anchors, 0 assertion failures each (QA reproduced all 29). Derived windows (period 12000): **gw_4 3188/8812 → 4395/7605 ms**, gw_5 6201/5799 → 7604/4396.
★ **MY ALGEBRA WAS DISPROVED — and the real reason for inertness is stronger than mine.** I claimed the CR factor "cancels exactly" in `w0/(w0+w1)`. It does **not**: `airtime_ms` floors twice, so `per_byte_air` is only ~linear in cr (off by ±1 ms in **156 of 320** sf×bw×cr combinations), and varying the **shared global** cr 5→8 moves the split in **544 of 576** realistic configs, by up to **93 ms of a 15 s period** (poison probe P2 confirms: global cr 5→8 moves **8/29** scenarios). The slice is inert not by cancellation but by **INPUT IDENTITY** — where neither layer overrides `cr`, `eff_cr()` already returned `radio_cr`, so the arguments to `airtime_ms` are bit-identical before and after (verified over 4096 configs). The residual global-cr sensitivity is **pre-existing, unchanged, and symmetric**, so it can never favour one layer; what the rule removes is the **per-layer** differential, which was neither symmetric nor small.
★★★ **A REAL FIRMWARE BUG THAT CONTRADICTS THE RULING'S PREMISE — found by chasing s32's joiner failure, reported NOT fixed (needs a design call).** `start_pending_rx_expiry` (`lib/core/node_mac.cpp:1307`) sizes the **receiver's** DATA wait with **`active_cr()` — the RECEIVER's own CR**: `airtime_ms(sf, active_bw_hz(), active_cr(), …)`. In s32, gw_4 transmits on layer 5 at **cr8** while the layer-5 leaves receive at the global **cr5**, so a leaf arms **~746 ms** for a frame that needs **~975 ms** (SF11/BW250k) and abandons it → `data_rx_timeout`. **⇒ "different CR settings are compatible" is TRUE at the LoRa PHY (the explicit header carries CR and the radio auto-detects it) but FALSE in this firmware's RX-window sizing.** Same pattern at `node_mac.cpp:1287` (ACK timeout) and `node_mac_rx.cpp:148/176`. ⇒ **per-layer CR does not work end-to-end today, independently of the window split.** Options: advertise the peer's CR, or size RX windows for worst-case CR4/8. **The ruling's CONCLUSION still stands** (a layer's private CR must not take its peer's window); only its stated rationale needs this fix to become true.
★ **JOINER ASSERTIONS: DO NOT RESTORE — my hypothesis was wrong.** I expected removing CR from the weighting might revive s32's cross-layer joiner deliveries. **They fail both before AND after**: no `push[msg_recv]` carries either payload in either run (the only hits are `cmd_reply` echoes). What DID improve is real and worth recording: gw_4's `xl_bridge` **207245 → 133780 ms**, `gateway_layer_window_deferred` **10 → 0**, `tx_gateway_schedule_defer` **14 → 2**, `peer_suspect_mark` **36 → 22** — and the failure **moved and became visible**: gw_4 now reaches the last mile and gives up explicitly (`send_failed`, `path_cascade_exhausted`, `data_ack_giveup` all 0→1) instead of the run ending before it got there. s32's `_desc` is corrected accordingly (verified `_desc` is stream-neutral: the md5 is unchanged by the rewrite).
**Coverage:** probe P1 (`per_byte_air` +7) moves **8/29** — the derive is genuinely executed, but note **s27 does NOT move** (its gateways do not leave both windows at 0), so the derive is narrower than "every gateway". For the **per-layer CR rule specifically the ONLY regression detector is the new native test** — s32 is the sole scenario with a per-layer `cr` and it asserts no delivery. The new test is **non-vacuous**: compiled against pre-slice behaviour it fails **19 assertions**, matching the 4+3+1 override combinations.
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE:** native **856/26494/0 → 858/26533/0** (+2 cases / +39 assertions = the two required tests: a per-layer **BW** difference DOES move the split; a per-layer **CR** difference does NOT, asserted as exact equality against the CR-neutral split) · **28/29 `cmp`-identical + s32 re-anchored**, 0 assertion failures each · `sizeof(Node)` **220592** unchanged · **boards 10/10, ΔRAM 0 on all ten**; flash −32/−24/−24 B on the three `gateway_*` envs (the removed lambda, expected direction), 0 on six, **+16 B on `production`** — reproduced with equal-length paths and a wiped build dir, so NOT the path-hash artifact, just one ARM alignment quantum under `MR_CONSOLE=0` · sim 27 binaries pass, `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` 3 → 3 unchanged.

**★★★★ 2026-07-27x (owner findings #1 + #2 — the per-layer frequency RESET + `-Werror=switch` — QA GO; uncommitted):** **#1** `NodeConfig::radio_freq_mhz` (default 0.0) + `active_freq_mhz()` + `activate_layer` now calls `_hal.set_rx_freq(active_freq_mhz())` **unconditionally**, so the three PHY lines finally read as ONE rule; `fw_main` populates it from `LORA_FREQ` / `nv.freq_mhz` (**no NV field or version bump — `nv.freq_mhz` already existed**, C4 respected) and the sim injects `_sim_freq_khz` from the scenario's post-inherit node carrier. ★ **A new C2 refusal `GwValErr::freq_inherit_no_global`** fires when exactly one layer overrides the carrier and no global exists — **without it the fix is a half-fix**, since there is then nothing to reset to and the Hal drops `mhz<=0`. **#2 `-Werror=switch` adopted** in `[common] build_flags`, the sim's `add_compile_options`, and `test/native/build_test.sh` (which bypasses CMake and needed its own copy).
★★ **MY "LIVE METAL BUG" CLAIM WAS WRONG — retracted; see the correction block in 26u.** `fw_main.cpp:632/642` pre-resolves the inherit at NV-load and both layers derive from `nv.freq_mhz`, so the asymmetry I described cannot occur on the NV path. The reachable case is the **mirror** (layer 0 inheriting with no global, layer 1 overriding) — exactly what the new refusal rejects — plus the sim, native tests, and any future config writer. The fix's value is one-rule-not-two, sim modellability, and the fail-loud refusal.
★ **`-Werror=switch` PROVEN LIVE BY QA, not accepted on report:** injecting an unhandled enumerator into `lib/core/airtime.cpp` gives `error: enumeration value 'C' not handled in switch [-Werror=switch]` and the build **FAILS**; file restored byte-identical. Coverage: `lib/core` · `lib/console` · `lib/hal` · `src/` across all 10 board envs, the native env, the sim CMake and the sim test harness. **Honest gaps, stated:** `src/` is NOT covered by `pio test -e native` (`test_build_src = no`), and the **ESP32 framework ships prebuilt** so those TUs are never recompiled with our flags (only nRF52 builds its BSP from source). ⚠ The coder's own first probe was WEAK and briefly suggested a hole — `gw_val_err_str` lives behind `#if MR_N_LAYERS >= 2`, so **verifying this flag requires a 2-layer env**; re-probed on `gateway`, it fails correctly. Blanket was chosen over `build_src_flags` (misses `lib/core`, where all five defects lived) and over per-library `library.json` (three files to keep in sync, and a NEW `lib/` dir would be silently uncovered — the "a human remembers" failure this ruling exists to kill). Vendored/framework `-Wswitch` count measured **ZERO** across all 10 envs, so the blanket is safe today; the escape lever is written into the ini comment.
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE:** native **854/26482/0 → 856/26494/0** (+2 cases / +12 assertions — a regression test was MANDATORY for a bug fix; **non-vacuity proven**: reverting only the call site + guard makes exactly those tests fail with `last_set_rx_freq` stuck on the stale carrier) · **29/29 `cmp`-identical** at the 26w anchors, 0 assertion failures each · `sizeof(Node)` **220584 → 220592 (+8)**, the `double` placed between `dv_hop_cap` and the existing `duty_cycle` so it fills the alignment padding already there (`sizeof(NodeConfig)` 256 → 264; after `radio_cr` it would have cost +16) · **boards 10/10 SUCCESS with `-Werror=switch` active, ΔRAM +8 B on every env** (QA re-measured `xiao_sx1262`: **164876 = 164868 + 8** exactly) · sim 27 binaries pass. ★ **An existing test was DELETED and that was correct** — `dual-layer freq: a layer with freq_mhz==0 does NOT retune` pinned the BUG as intended behaviour and its config is now refused; replaced by three `§layer-freq` tests. ⚠ Deferred + marked in-source: `adopt_mobile_phy` and `node_mobile.cpp:73` keep the conditional-freq shape (mobile plane, own gate), and a live `cfg set freq` on a gateway still snaps back at the next window switch because fw_main's pre-resolution leaves explicit per-layer carriers that outrank the updated global (pre-existing). Reported, not fixed: `g_freq_mhz` vs `_cfg.radio_freq_mhz` is now duplicated state.

**21 moved, 8 unmoved.** ★★ **THE ENTIRE DELTA IS ONE FIELD.** QA proved it line-by-line on s18: **271244 lines compared, 60 differ, and the ONLY differing field is `data.kind`** (56 `send_e2e_acked→e2e_acked`, 4 `send_failed→send_blocked`) — **zero other field diffs, zero event-count change in any of the 29, zero delivery change** (`dm_delivery_breakdown` byte-identical on all 9 delivery scenarios; s18 stays **99/113 = 88%, leaks 0**). Corpus-wide attribution, every moved byte accounted for: `send_failed→channel_recv` **1200** · `→send_blocked` **715** · `send_e2e_acked→e2e_acked` **224** · `→channel_sent` **85** · `→mobile_reg` **45** · `→team_reg` **35** · `→peer_key_cached` **14** · `→config_adopted` **1** = **2319 relabelled pushes**.
★ **#6 — the oracle was mislabelling 2095 corpus events as `send_failed`.** Fixed by **reuse, not a second table**: the sim now compiles `lib/console/console_json.cpp` **once** (new CMake target `meshroute_console`) and both wrapper variants reach the existing exhaustive `pushkind_name()` through a primitive-typed bridge `mrsim::pushKindName(uint8_t)` (`orchestrator/runtime/ConsoleNames.{h,cpp}`) — the `ISimHal` door pattern. ⚠ Both of my suggested routes were **impossible under the no-firmware-edit boundary**: `console_json.h:22` opens the **literal** `namespace meshroute::console`, so compiling it under `MESHROUTE_NS=meshroute_gw` is a hard compile error, not merely ugly. Bridge soundness is pinned by **twin `static_assert`s** (one per namespace: `sizeof==1` + `join_adopted==13`), **proven to bite** (poisoning 13→12 fails both TUs). `ConsoleNames.h` states a HARD LIMIT in-source: **enum mappers only** — the lib is built with the NORMAL variant's `MR_N_LAYERS=1` layout, so nothing taking a `Node`/`NodeConfig`/`Push` may cross it. ★ **Side benefit: this closes the long-standing "the sim compiles no `lib/console`" blind region** — for that one function.
★★ **#7 IS CORPUS-INERT, and that disproves BOTH my brief AND the in-source note the previous slice left.** I claimed scenarios run mixed BW. **They do not.** QA verified independently by parsing every config (not grepping): global BW takes 3 distinct values across the corpus (62.5 / 125 / 250) but there are **ZERO per-node `bw` overrides and ZERO per-layer `bw_hz` overrides**, so *within* any one scenario every node shares one BW and a mismatch cannot occur. Corroborated by the coder three ways: `drop_bw_mismatch == 0` corpus-wide; an instrumented counter shows `bw_mismatch = 0` in **all 29** while the gate is evaluated **431,246×**; and #7-alone is `cmp`-identical on **29/29**. **⇒ 100% of the re-anchor above is #6.** The false claim has been deleted from the source note.
★ **A placement call of mine the coder correctly refused.** I said to put the BW condition beside `tunedToCarrier` (`:2201`). That site sits **above** `notifyRxStart` and the CAD `notifyChannelBusy`, so a BW gate there would exempt a BW-mismatched frame from the RX→TX turnaround charge and from marking the channel busy — granting 125/250 kHz layers the isolation only a separate **carrier** earns, and destroying the *"bandwidth grants NO orthogonality"* asymmetry I told it to preserve **in the same brief**. It went beside the **SF** gate instead, suppressing exactly the `PreambleDetected` callback; energy/LBT/collision/turnaround all still fed. Rationale in-source.
**Coverage:** #6 — the transition census IS the probe (every relabelled event proves execution): **11 of 14 kinds are corpus-exercised**; **3 are corpus-dark** (`hash_resolved`, `join_refused`, `join_adopted`) and rest on `test_console_json.cpp` + `-Wswitch`; the `"unknown"` fallback fired **0** times. #7 — probe P1 (invert `bw_match`) moves **15/29**, so the site is reached and load-bearing, but the corpus has no mismatch to gate ⇒ **the extended `test_bw_mismatch` is the ONLY thing covering it**; non-vacuity proven (compiled against pre-#7 behaviour, the new assertion fails). ★ **OWED: a mixed-BW scenario** — s31/s32 add carrier and CR coverage but are 250 kHz throughout, so they add **no** BW coverage.
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE:** firmware native **854/26482/0 EXACT** · **all 29 md5s + event counts reproduced**, **0 assertion failures each** (s22's stale `send_e2e_acked` assert fixed by QA at `s22_mobile_team_meshroute.json:215` → `e2e_acked` — the ONLY functional consumer of the old spelling anywhere; the coder's sweep found **zero** in sim `tools/`/`webapp/`, and the iOS companion was **already** on the winning spelling, so the two are now aligned) · sim native suite **27 binaries all pass**, incl. `test_bw_mismatch: OK (… preamble gated on BW too)` · sim `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` **3 → 3**, same three, all pre-existing in `lib/core`, **zero from the new files** · `sizeof(Node)` **220584** · **firmware change is COMMENT-ONLY** (QA verified: filtering the diff for non-comment lines yields nothing) — `lib/console/console_json.h`'s header had asserted *"the s18/scenario-corpus oracle CANNOT see this file"* and called it **load-bearing for the gate**; #6 makes that FALSE, so V1 forced the rewrite. Boards: `xiao_sx1262` measured **RAM 164868 / Flash 465124 = ΔRAM 0, ΔFlash 0**; envs 2–10 declared not built.

**★★★★ 2026-07-26v (QA — ★ THE MULTI-LAYER / MULTI-FREQ SCENARIOS: the corpus goes 27 → 29; uncommitted):** the owner's goal was *"a realistic simulator able to test our multi layer / multi freq simulations"* — 26u built the capability, these two scenarios **prove** it. Both authored by QA (scenario JSONs are QA-owned), both derived from `s09_two_layer_gateway` (which already has the mandatory `warmup_ms: 0` — warmup bypasses all physics gates by contract). **NEW ANCHORS: `s31_dual_carrier_gateway` `45526e02`/2300 · `s32_dual_cr_gateway` `54643800`/3100**, both 0 assertion failures and 3-run stable. ⚠ Both will RE-ANCHOR when Wave-4 #6/#7 land.
★ **A schema trap worth knowing:** `"layers"` is the array the config mapper reads (`NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp:398` → `map_layers`, which maps `freq_mhz` at `:312`); **`"gateway_layers"` is `Disp::ignored`** (`:415`) — a dead key still present in s09/s31/s32. Per-node carrier uses the sim key `freq_khz` (integer); per-LAYER uses the firmware key `freq_mhz` (see 26u for why the two differ).
★★ **s31 — CARRIER ISOLATION PROVEN, and the physics is better than expected.** Layer 4 @ **868.0**, layer 5 @ **869.5**; the l4_* and l5_* leaves stay in **direct RF range in the topology matrix** yet cannot hear each other at all. Result vs s09: **`drop_freq_mismatch` 0 → 238** while **`drop_sf_mismatch` 255 → 15**. ⇒ frames formerly rejected by the *SF* gate are now rejected **earlier, at the carrier** — which is what real hardware does (a radio tuned to 868.0 never sees a 869.5 frame, so it cannot "reject it for wrong SF"). ★ **All FOUR of s09's cross-layer bridging assertions still pass**, so the gateway still bridges while the leaves are genuinely isolated. **DELIVERY WENT UP, NOT DOWN** — `rx` 385 → 396, `collision` 3 → 1, `tx_deferred` 9 → 5 — because channelization removes cross-layer contention. (The coder had predicted a possible delivery *drop*; measurement says the opposite. Recorded because it is the first hard evidence the feature models the real benefit of channelization.)
⚠ **A QA assertion of mine was WRONG and the code was right.** I asserted `drop_freq_mismatch count == 0` at `l4_j1` (recipe rationale: "a layer-4 leaf never sees a mismatch from its own-layer peers"). It fires **36×** — correctly: `nodeMatches` also matches the *receiver*, and l4_j1 IS in range of layer-5 transmitters on the other carrier, so it SHOULD register mismatches. Replaced with the correct **bidirectional** pair (`l4_j1` ≥ 1 **and** `l5_j1` ≥ 1), which is the sharper isolation proof anyway.
★★ **s32 — the CR RETUNE PROVEN, plus an OPEN DESIGN FINDING.** Gateway `tx` events now carry **cr=5 (21 tx, airtimes 19–75 ms)** and **cr=8 (76 tx, airtimes 26–63 ms)**; **before Wave-4 #4 every gateway tx read cr=5** because the sim never overrode `set_rx_cr`. ⇒ ★ **OPEN FINDING — the gateway window scheduler does NOT account for per-layer PHY airtime cost.** Measured: **any** per-layer CR increase (tried **6, 7 and 8**) makes both cross-layer *joiner* deliveries fail, while the gateway's own `xl_bridge` emits and same-layer delivery still pass. Cause is airtime budget, not the retune. The two joiner assertions are therefore **REMOVED from s32 (not carved as reds)**; s32's job is to pin the retune.
⚠⚠ **CORRECTION 2026-07-27 — MY DIAGNOSIS ABOVE WAS WRONG, and the `_desc` in `s32_dual_cr_gateway.json` repeats the same wrong claim (fixed with the 26x slice).** I wrote *"the window split is derived from SF weighting only and does NOT account for per-layer CR/BW airtime cost."* **False.** `lib/core/node.cpp:96-104` (v17) already weights each layer by its **own effective SF, BW _and_ CR**: `eff_bw()`/`eff_cr()` fall back to the global, and `per_byte_air()` = `airtime_ms(routing_sf, eff_bw, eff_cr, …, 240) − airtime_ms(…, 120)`. **The real mechanism is REDISTRIBUTION OF A FIXED PERIOD:** the derive splits `window_period_ms` between the two layers pro-rata, so a heavier CR on layer 1 **lengthens layer 1's window at the expense of layer 0's** — the cycle never grows. The cross-layer joiner exchange needs BOTH windows, so shrinking one breaks it. ⇒ the owner's 26x ruling (drop CR from the weighting) addresses exactly this, and is a *removal*, not the addition I described.

**★★★★ 2026-07-26u (Wave-4 #4 + #5 — ★ THE SIM'S PHY IS NOW FREQUENCY-SELECTIVE — QA GO; uncommitted):** owner ruling 2026-07-26, goal *"a realistic simulator able to test our multi layer / multi freq simulations."* A TX on one carrier is **NOT** received by a node tuned to another — **HARD split** on exact integer-kHz mismatch (partial adjacent-channel overlap DEFERRED: no bench data, and inventing it is the same trap as guessing a motion σ). Plus the **CR runtime-retune** gap closed. ★★ **This REVERSES the documented decision at `hal.h:87`** (*"the SIM Hal does NOT model RF frequency… so it correctly inherits the no-op"*) — that text is now explicitly marked RETIRED in-source so the override is not read as a bug.
★★★ **A REAL FIRMWARE BUG FOUND — reported, deliberately NOT fixed (QA's STOP rule), and QA CONFIRMED IT AT SOURCE.** `Node::activate_layer` (`lib/core/node.cpp:488`) calls `set_rx_freq` **only `if (L.freq_mhz > 0.0)`**, while the two lines below call `set_rx_bw(active_bw_hz())` / `set_rx_cr(active_cr())` **unconditionally with the EFFECTIVE value** — and their own comment says why: *"NOT `if (L.bw_hz>0)`: an inherit-leaf entered AFTER an override-leaf must RESET the HAL back to the global."* **Frequency has exactly the bug its neighbours' comment warns against, and cannot be fixed the same way: `NodeConfig` has `radio_bw_hz`/`radio_cr` but NO `radio_freq_mhz`** (verified — the boot carrier lives in the HAL, not the Node). ⚠⚠ **CORRECTION 2026-07-27 — MY METAL-REACHABILITY CLAIM WAS WRONG, and I had reported it to the owner as a live metal bug. Retracted, verified at source.** I wrote *"ON METAL, a gateway with `layers[0].freq_mhz=868.1` and `layers[1].freq_mhz=0` runs layer 1's ENTIRE window on layer 0's carrier — reachable today."* **It is NOT reachable via the NV path**: `src/fw_main.cpp:632/642` **pre-resolves the inherit at NV-load** — `layers[0] = nv.freq_mhz` and `layers[1] = (nv.l1_freq_mhz > 0) ? nv.l1_freq_mhz : nv.freq_mhz` — and those two lines are the ONLY firmware writers of `layers[0/1].freq_mhz` (the third hit, `node.cpp:454`, is `adopt_mobile_phy`: single-layer/mobile). Because BOTH derive from `nv.freq_mhz`, the asymmetry I described cannot occur. ⇒ my scenario-authoring advice *"state `freq_mhz` on BOTH layers — `0` does NOT mean back to the node's carrier"* is ALSO now false for the sim, since `_sim_freq_khz` is wired: `0` **does** mean the node's carrier.
★ **What IS real:** the defect *shape* was genuine and the two-implementations-of-one-rule inconsistency was genuine — and there is a **mirror case that IS reachable**: layer 0 **inheriting** (`nv.freq_mhz == 0`) while layer 1 **overrides**, with no global to fall back to. That is exactly what the new C2 refusal `GwValErr::freq_inherit_no_global` now rejects. The inherit path is also taken by the **simulator** (`map_layers` defaults `freq_mhz` to 0, so every corpus gateway), **native tests**, and any future writer (`mutable_config()`, a remote-config verb). ⇒ the fix's value is (a) one rule instead of two implementations, (b) the sim can model the reset at all, (c) the fail-loud refusal — **not** a silent metal catastrophe.
★★ **ONE PREDICATE, FOUR CONSUMERS — my brief said two and was wrong.** `SimController::tunedToCarrier()` (`SimController.h:146`) now gates: decode (`:1308` → **its own `drop_freq_mismatch` reason**, never `dropNoLink`) · preamble/energy observe (`:2201`, placed ABOVE `notifyRxStart` so it also exempts the RX→TX turnaround charge and the CAD busy-window) · **energy-LBT ask-time busy** (`:957`) · **frame-vs-frame collision** (`:2085`, comparing the two frames' stamped carriers — neither party is "the receiver"). Gating only the two I named would have left two carriers still **LBT-blocking and colliding with each other** — i.e. the feature's entire purpose defeated. Deliberate asymmetry vs the BW gate, which keeps feeding collisions because *"bandwidth grants NO orthogonality"*; a separate channel genuinely does.
★ **MY BRIEF CONTAINED A CONTRADICTION; the coder resolved it correctly.** I demanded both *"use the firmware's `protocol::mhz_to_khz`"* and *"put frequency in `JsonConfig`"* — incompatible: `test/native/build_test.sh` compiles `JsonConfig.cpp` + `SimController.cpp` with `INCLUDES="-I $REPO_ROOT -I $REPO_ROOT/third_party"` and **no MeshRoute path** (QA verified), so an MHz key there would force a **second, driftable** conversion = the U2 violation the brief forbade. ⇒ **the SCENARIO key is integer `freq_khz`** (global `simulation.radio.freq_khz`, default 868000; per-node `freq_khz`, `-1` = inherit) needing no conversion at all, while the **per-LAYER key stays the firmware's own `freq_mhz`**, canonicalized once at `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp:76` — the only TU allowed to name `MESHROUTE_NS`. Per-layer beats per-node **by construction** (`activate_layer` overwrites the seeded slot), not by a precedence rule.
★ **Wave-4 #7 RE-SCOPED (QA verified).** My brief told the coder to "leave the BW half of the preamble gate alone" — **there is no BW half there.** The BW gate already exists, in the **decode** path (`SimController.cpp:1426-1450`, `tx.bw_hz != rx_bw_hz`, §bw-gating, landed 25j, with its own `test_bw_mismatch`). It is the **preamble-detect observer** that matches SF only. ⇒ #7 = *add* a BW condition there, mirroring the decode path. Gap recorded in-source at `SimController.cpp:2209`.
**COVERAGE — measured by probe, and it corrected me again.** P1 (invert `tunedToCarrier`) → **27/27 move** ⇒ the predicate runs on **every delivery in every scenario**, so byte-identity here is a REAL inertness proof, not vacuous. P2 (detune node 1 by +100 kHz) → **27/27 move** ⇒ the mismatch branch is reachable and isolating. P3 (force cr=8) → **7/27 move** (s09, s09_metal, s10, s15, s15_metal, s16, s27) ⇒ ★ the CR retune is **reached** in the 7 gateway/multi-layer anchors — better than "corpus-dark"; it is *value*-inert only because no scenario sets a per-layer `cr`. **Still owed:** no corpus scenario sets a second carrier, and the new native test drives the retune through the **deprecated Lua engine**, so the FIRMWARE path `Hal::set_rx_freq → HalAdapter → mhz_to_khz → simSetRxFreqKhz` is covered by **nothing automated** — that is exactly what the s31/s32 scenarios below exist to close.
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE (reproduced personally):** firmware native **854/26482/0 EXACT** · ★ **27/27 `cmp`-identical to streams QA generated ITSELF pre-slice**, 0 assertion failures each — **the hard tripwire held** · sim native suite **26 → 27 binaries, ALL PASS**, `test_freq_mismatch: OK` (*default+inherit, fractional refused, hard split incl. adjacent, retune flips both ways, preamble/LBT/collision all gated, off-net silent, cr-retune preserves sf/bw*) · sim `-Wall -Wextra -Wpedantic` **3 → 3**, the same three, all pre-existing in `lib/core`, **zero attributable to sim files** · `sizeof(Node)` **220584** · **boards correctly NOT built — the sole firmware change is a `hal.h` COMMENT** (QA verified the diff: every `virtual void set_rx_*` code line is untouched). Coder's A/B: CR-half-only **0/27 moved**, carrier-half-only **0/27 moved** ⇒ each half independently stream-neutral. Line delta: sim **+433/−3** + 3 new files; firmware **+12/−4**, comments only.
⚠ **Named deviations, all accepted:** four gate sites not two · scenario key `freq_khz` not `freq_mhz` (above) · the decode gate sits **before** the fading draw as briefed, so it uses the **pre-fading** budget as its off-net-silence proxy — identical to `would_decode` whenever fading is off, i.e. every analytic scenario (documented in-source) · a Lua twin `self:set_rx_freq_khz` added as a test-harness affordance mirroring the existing `self:set_rx_bw` (NOT Lua-parity work) · **no blind window on a carrier retune** — no measured PLL settling time exists and inventing one was forbidden; every firmware call site calls `set_rx_sf` immediately before, which already arms the 1 ms window · warmup deliberately ungated (it bypasses all physics gates by contract, SF and BW equally).

**★★★ 2026-07-26t (batch A1–A4 — byte-inert firmware hardening — QA GO; uncommitted):** 5 files, **+37/−12** (logic net **+3**; the rest rationale). **A1** `lib/core/node.cpp:927` — the `default:` label REMOVED from the frame-dispatch switch (all 14 `wire::Cmd` enumerators cased). **A2** the 3 truncating kHz→Hz sites (`fw_main.cpp:562`, `firmware_config.cpp:387`/`:732`) routed through `protocol::khz_to_hz` + a `static_assert` per TU. **A3** `[[maybe_unused]]` on the telemetry-only locals. **A4** the dead `/*MR_TELEMETRY(…)*/` corpse at `node_beacon.cpp:253` deleted.
★ **WHY A1 MATTERS — the tripwire, not the dispatch.** Proven with the compiler, twice independently (QA + coder): `-Wswitch` is **blind behind any `default:`**. With the label, a future `Cmd` enumerator dispatches nowhere and warns nothing; without it the build reports *"enumeration value … not handled in switch"*. The coder proved it POSITIVELY by adding a hypothetical `FUTURE = 0xD`: the AFTER form warns, the BEFORE form emits **0**. Behaviour is identical either way (0xD/0xE fall out of the switch exactly as `default: break` did) — the label bought nothing and cost the tripwire, at the one switch where an unhandled frame type is worst. **Never re-add it; add the case** (recorded in-source).
★★ **OWED, and it is the real gap: `-Wswitch` is a WARNING, not an error — the build still succeeds.** Enforcement today is *"a human reads the census."* ⇒ **`-Werror=switch` for `lib/`+`src/` is the only thing that makes this class structurally impossible**, and it also answers the still-open `-Wswitch-enum` gate question: a count in a doc erodes, a build failure does not.
★ **A V1 FINDING FOUND BY PROBE, not by reading:** `node_beacon.cpp:250` claimed *"compute_budget_tier is draw-free; HEALTHY in every gate, so this is gate-inert"* — **FALSE.** `beacon_skipped_budget` fires **288×** in the corpus (s06 **119** + s07 **169**; tier `critical` ×**250**, `exhausted` ×**38**) — QA reproduced all four numbers from its own pre-slice streams. Comment corrected with the measurements inline. A drifted comment survived because nobody had ever poisoned that emit.
**Poison-probe matrix (5 probes, separate tree, all reverted + re-verified):** `case Cmd::Q` dropped → **21/27** move (the dispatch switch is heavily executed) · re-adding `default:` with an emit → **0/27 and 0 emissions** (★ the 0xD/0xE no-match path is **never reached** in the corpus) · `next` flipped → **4/27** (99 emissions, telemetry live) · `prior+7` → **0/27** with `addr_conflict_forced_rejoin` absent from all 27 streams (`forced_rejoin` is **corpus-dark** — a STRONG probe, so genuinely not executed) · beacon `kind` poisoned → **2/27** (which is what disproved the comment above).
**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE (every number reproduced personally):** native **854/26482/0 EXACT**, no test added · ★ **27/27 `cmp`-identical to streams QA generated ITSELF pre-slice**, 0 assertion failures each (stronger than an anchor comparison) · `sizeof(Node)` **220584** · `xiao_sx1262` SUCCESS with **RAM 164868 = the `:114` recorded value exactly (ΔRAM +0)** and **Flash 465124 = QA's own pre-slice census value (ΔFlash 0)** · **`-Wunused-variable` (ours) 2→0 per env**, `-Wswitch` 0→0. Envs 2–10 are the coder's measurement (10/10 SUCCESS, ΔRAM +0, ΔFlash 0). `static_assert` proven **non-vacuous**: inert at 125.0 and at all 10 standard bandwidths, **fires** at 32.3/64.1/64.6/65.1/128.2 kHz (32.3 → 32299 vs 32300) — and `is_valid_bw_hz`'s exact-match table would reject 32299 as `bad_bw`, so the failure mode was always a loud refusal, never a mistuned radio.
⚠ **A2 IS HARDENING, NOT A BUG FIX — do not let a future reader infer otherwise.** No live defect existed: `LORA_BW=125.0` is defined **once** (`platformio.ini:35`, no env overrides it) and truncation == rounding for **0 of 10** standard bandwidths and for every real EU/US channel centre. The value was the **invariant**: the console `bw=`/`bw0=`/`bw1=` path already rounded via `khz_to_hz` while the compile-time default truncated — two conversion paths for one quantity feeding BOTH the Node's airtime math and the radio config, which carry a documented `charge == transmit` invariant (`hal.h:92`). The class HAS bitten before — `protocol_constants.h:112` records the rounding exists *"so 62.5 kHz lands on 62500 Hz and not 62499 — the fractional-BW bug this project has already paid for once."*
★ **QA premises DISPROVEN by the coder (both correct):** (1) **A3 has 2 sites, not 3** — `node_join.cpp:399`'s `prior` is **live code** (`join_deny_id(prior)` plus the `_id_bind` scan compare against it), so it never warns and the attribute would be misleading; the real per-env count is 2 (`node.cpp:519` + `node_join.cpp:416`) × 10 envs = the 20 of 26r. The coder left `:399` alone and documented the distinction between the two same-named locals in-source. (2) The tree was **clean at HEAD**, not dirty — the owner had already committed the w4 slice (`a0cb902`, `3057c41`), so 100% of the working delta is the coder's; the rsync BEFORE was still right, just not load-bearing.
★ **METHODOLOGY ADDITION to the shared gate doc — a phantom flash delta.** A first pass read **+16 B on `production` / −16 B on `heltec_mobile`**; cause = the two snapshot trees' absolute paths differed by **one character**, which changes PlatformIO's `libXXX` path-hash directories and baked path strings. Re-measured with both trees at **equal-length paths** (same build dir, wiped between passes): **ΔFlash 0 on all 10.** ⇒ **Standing practice gains: make the two snapshot paths equal length, or reuse one path sequentially.** Also note the "warning census" total is **filter-dependent and not comparable across notes** — anchored `^(lib|src)/` gives 221/10 envs, while a loose `(lib|src)/` gives 1578 by sweeping vendored `RadioLib/src` + `CustomLFS/src`; earlier notes' totals (281) were a different filter. The −20 delta and zero-new conclusion are unaffected.
**Coverage verdicts, per item (honest):** A1 — the **compiler** is the detector, stronger than a test, but only advisory until `-Werror=switch`; byte-identity does NOT validate it (probe proved the path is never reached). A2 — **nothing automated covers the behaviour**; what covers it is the `static_assert` plus object-level evidence (4 of 5 `.o` files bit-identical; `fw_main.cpp.o` differs only in the `__DATE__`/`__TIME__` banner, identical section sizes). Both handlers still live in a TU **no native build compiles** ⇒ OWED unchanged: move them to a natively-compilable unit (precedent `src/firmware_config_parse.h`), C1-blocked here. A3 — an attribute has zero codegen, so only the **census** can catch a regression; keep it in the gate. A4 — a comment; nothing needed.

**★★★ 2026-07-26s (Wave-4 #2+#3 — the `bad_freq` FALSE-SUCCESS fix + the `-Wswitch-enum` blind spot — QA GO; uncommitted):** 3 files, **+45/−6** (+7 logic lines, the rest rationale). **THE DEFECT, now fixed:** `src/firmware_config.cpp` `gw_parse_err_str` mapped 12 of `GwParseErr`'s 14 enumerators behind `default: return "ok"`, so a **refused** `gateway freq0=0` printed **`> gateway err ok`** — a false success reported to the operator, on every gateway build up to now. Worse than the six 25m holes (those printed *nothing*). All 14 now cased, **no `default:`**, trailing return deliberately NOT `"ok"`. `gw_val_err_str` got the same treatment (declared extension beyond the brief: its table was complete but correct only *by luck* — a 14th enumerator would have re-rendered a refusal as success). Three `lib/core` switches made exhaustive: `label_of_frame` (+`beacon`), `retry_slot_of` (+`rts`/`beacon`), `enqueue_data`'s `SealOutcome` (+`cross_layer`/`ok`) — all trailing-return-preserving, so out-of-range casts behave **verbatim** (`on_radio_busy` casts a HAL `uint16` into `FrameTag`).
★ **TWO PREMISES OF MINE DISPROVEN, both by source:** (a) I called `SealOutcome::cross_layer` unreachable — **it is a genuine `n==0` outcome**: `e2e_seal_inner` (`node_hashlocate.cpp:381-382`) sets it FIRST, then returns 0 on `DATA_FLAG_CROSS_LAYER`; it cannot reach *this* call site only because no `enqueue_data` caller passes `CROSS_LAYER` (the XL paths build their `TxItem` directly and never seal). Only `ok` is unreachable, and by *arithmetic*. (b) `node.cpp`'s missing `EXT` case was **not** a silently-dropped frame type: `wire::Cmd::EXT = 0xF` is a RESERVED nibble that **nothing in either repo ever builds** (zero `cmd_byte(Cmd::EXT,…)` sites) and it is absent from `docs/frames.md`'s map — which now records `0xD/0xE` UNASSIGNED + `0xF` EXT RESERVED so the gap cannot recur.

★★★ **THE `-Wswitch-enum` RULING (measured, and the answer is NO for a build-wide zero).** Ours: **105 → 36**; residual = **one site**, `src/device_fault.h:222`, a switch over ESP-IDF's **`esp_reset_reason_t`** whose enumerator set *grows with the IDF version* — pinning it would turn a toolchain bump into a build break, so `default: return 0 /* POR */` is the correct unclassified bucket. Third-party makes build-wide zero **permanently unreachable**: **85** warnings/env on nRF52 (Adafruit BSP, TinyUSB, RadioLib) and **~307**/env on ESP32, all in files the vendoring rule forbids editing. Adoptable rule: *count only paths under `lib/` or `src/`* → `-Wswitch-enum == 0` on the 4 nRF52 envs, `== 6` on each ESP32 env, all at that one site. **Better option, NOT done (owner's call):** wrap that switch in `#pragma GCC diagnostic ignored "-Wswitch-enum"` and the rule becomes a flat **0 across `lib/`+`src/`** — an in-code reviewable annotation instead of a number in a doc that erodes.
★ **MY OPEN OBJECTION — `lib/core/node.cpp:927` keeps its `default:`, and that is the one site where it costs protection.** The coder cased `EXT` but *kept* the label (deviation, declared), reasoning that the switch's domain is the wire nibble and `0xD`/`0xE` are representable `Cmd` values with no enumerator — **factually correct** (`enum class Cmd : uint8_t`). But I proved with the compiler that `-Wswitch` is **blind behind any `default:`**: add a future `Cmd` enumerator and the no-`default:` form warns *"enumeration value not handled"* while the delivered form says **nothing**. So the frame-dispatch switch — the most protocol-critical switch in the tree — is guarded **only if `-Wswitch-enum` is adopted**, a ruling that has not been made. Removing the label is behaviour-identical (0xD/0xE fall out of the switch exactly as `default: break`) and the comment already documents the domain; it is also the idiom the coder itself applied at the other three sites. **Recommend: drop the label. 1 line, needs a re-gate.**
⚠ **THE FIX HAS NO AUTOMATED COVERAGE, and I verified the structure rather than arguing it:** the sim's `CMakeLists.txt` references `MESHROUTE_DIR}/src` **zero** times and the native build contains **0 `src/` objects** (vs 21 `lib/core`) ⇒ 0/27 corpus **by compilation**, no native reach. What *does* exist is the **enum** half — `test_dual_layer.cpp:2123-2124` has pinned `parse_gateway_cmd("…freq0=0") == bad_freq` all along. ★ **So the tested half was the enum and the untested half was the string the operator reads** — 25m's pattern exactly. A durable tripwire is **C1-blocked**: both mappers are `static` in a TU no native build compiles; OWED = move them (or their tables) into a natively-compilable unit, precedent `src/firmware_config_parse.h`, then extend `test_console_json.cpp`.

The coder's line citation was right and mine was wrong. Two scope traps in this slice alone: the **flag** (`-Wswitch` cannot see behind `default:`) and now the **path form**. Neither was visible in the finding that reported the count.

**item 4 — NV `load_stamped`:** 7 of 8 candidate load-or-seed/stamp/save rituals unified (**the review's "7 copies" is RIGHT**); the 8th (`handle_gateway`) refused after measuring that unifying it would change **16** persisted fields. ★ **THREE divergent `Blob`-seed implementations existed, not two:** `seed_blob_from_live` writes **38** fields, `fw_main.cpp`'s hand-rolled copy wrote **35** — missing exactly `node_id`, `magic`, `version`, i.e. **the precise S1/L9 field-drop shape `CODE_GUIDELINES` names `seed_blob_from_live` to prevent.** Latent, not live (all three overwritten downstream with identical values), and now structurally impossible. ★ **The `commit` half was deliberately NOT built** — the 7 save epilogues differ in *control flow* and each prints a *different user-visible string* (incl. two irregular ones); a wrapper would either change those strings or take one parameter per difference. ★★★ **UNCHECKED NV SAVES: 5, not 1 (review) or 2 (QA) — and the reason is the THIRD instance of one meta-bug.** All preserved byte-for-byte, tagged `§nv-unchecked [n/5]` with explicit `(void)` discards: `fw_main.cpp:537` (`save_faults`), `:1078` (config_adopted — a silent failure means the next reboot re-pulls the old leaf epoch), **`firmware_remote.cpp:92` (⚠ the replay floor lags the live counter, so a reboot re-accepts used admin counters)**, `:120` (rotated admin key live but volatile after `ok rotate` was already acked), `firmware_commands.cpp:48` (`peerkey_set` acks `"pinned":true` even when `/mrpeers` failed); plus `device_nv.h:223` outside the 4-file scope. **⚠ OWNER RULING OWED** — fixing any of these is a behaviour change (C2), correctly refused inside a refactor. **Why 3-A found only one:** its comment at `firmware_config.cpp:620` says "the ONLY unchecked save of 9" — true *within that one file*. **The sweep was file-scoped.** This is structurally identical to 25m (a `lib/`-scoped `-Wswitch` sweep that missed `src/`) and to QA's own item-3 coverage error. ★ **STANDING LESSON: a sweep is only as good as its scope, and the scope is never stated in the finding.** Whenever a count is quoted — "the only", "all N" — establish what was searched before believing it.

★★★★ **THE STRUCTURAL FINDING — `src/` HAS ZERO AUTOMATED BEHAVIOURAL COVERAGE.** Item 4's poison probe (COMMON §E, now mandatory) measured **0 of 27** scenarios moving, and proved that is *blindness, not a weak probe*, at the object level: with the same poison, `firmware_config.cpp.o` and `fw_main.cpp.o` change while a control TU stays bit-identical. Corroborated structurally — the sim's CMakeLists lists 19 `lib/core/*.cpp` and **zero `src/`**; `nm -C lus` finds no `mrfw::` symbols. ⚠ **AND IT DISPROVED QA'S FALLBACK CLAIM:** I wrote "the native suite is the only thing that can assert behaviour" — **wrong.** `[env:native]` sets `test_build_src = no` with `build_src_filter = +<sim_main.cpp> -<fw_main.cpp>`; QA verified independently: **0 `src/` objects in the native build vs 21 `lib/core` objects**, and `platformio.ini:62` states it outright. ⇒ **The entire firmware-integration layer — every console command handler, all NV persistence, `fw_main`'s runtime glue — is verified ONLY by "it compiles" plus manual bench testing.** Neither gate can see it. This is not a slice footnote; it is the shape of the project's test coverage, and it is why item 4 ships on board-compile plus stated algebra alone. **Candidate follow-up: make `src/`'s pure units natively testable** (item 3 already moved its parse primitives into the header-only `firmware_config_parse.h` for exactly this reason — the only `mrfw::` symbols the native build contains).

**Accepted deviations across the three:** item 3 placed its primitives in `src/firmware_config_parse.h` rather than a file-local static (correct — it is the only way console code gets native coverage at all) and widened `valid_routing_sf(int)`→`(long)`; item 4 used an `rsync` snapshot rather than `git archive HEAD` for its BEFORE tree (**correct for a dirty tree** — archiving HEAD would have attributed other in-flight slices' flash delta to it) and pinned `GIT_REV` in the scratch copy only. ★ **A REPRODUCIBILITY FINDING worth acting on (item 3): `jgromes/RadioLib @ ^7.6.0` is UNPINNED** — the live repo resolves a *mix* (7.7.0 on 3 envs, 7.7.1 on 7), and `tools/git_rev.py` bakes a **variable-length** string into the image. Both skew board-size baselines across checkouts; item 3's first baseline was invalidated by exactly this. **Recommend pinning RadioLib.** Both coders disclosed self-caused races (an edit during a board read; a probe left in a build) and discarded the affected passes rather than reporting contaminated numbers.
