**★★ 2026-07-25l (3-B item 1 — `RecentRing` template — QA GO; PURE REFACTOR, zero bytes moved; committed by the owner mid-slice as `71623bb` "dedup"):** the first firmware slice of the dedup block, and the review's highest-ranked item. **7 hand-rolled seen/mark window rings + 2 age-out sweeps** collapsed onto `lib/core/recent_ring.h` — three **non-owning free-function templates** over `Entry (&ring)[Cap]` + `uint8_t& n` (`recent_ring_hit` / `_mark` / `_age_out`, sharing one `recent_ring_cutoff` so a sweep can never drop an entry the query still honours). ★ **Non-owning is the load-bearing design choice:** a `RecentRing<Entry,Cap>` member class would give the array+count pair its own tail padding and move `sizeof(Node)`; operating on the existing members touches ZERO Node members, so layout is identical *by construction*. Measured **137 → 33 lines** of logic (+49 of rationale; review predicted ~130→~40).

★ **THE REAL DEDUP WIN, and it is bigger than the line count:** each ring wrote its key predicate **twice** — once in `…_recently`, once in `mark_…` — so **14 copies became 7 `same_key` definitions.** Adding a key field can no longer widen the window on one side only, which is precisely the missed-twin class the review ranked #1. Also fixed structurally: the team-plane ledger pair used a hand-written `sizeof/sizeof` cap, and `Cap` is now deduced from each array's own extent, so the 64-slot static and 16-slot team ledgers can no longer be crossed (`static_assert(Cap <= 255)` guards the `uint8_t` count).

★★ **THE BOUNDARY DIVERGENCE IS GONE, NOT PRESERVED — path 1 of the brief.** QA had measured two incompatible window comparisons: **Variant A** (clamped cutoff, `t_ms >= cutoff`, i.e. age **≤** ttl) at 2 sites vs **Variant B** (`(now - t_ms) < ttl`, age **<** ttl) at 5 — disagreeing by exactly one tick at `age == ttl`. **One semantic (A) held byte-identical across all 27 scenarios**, so the divergence was unobservable in the entire corpus and could simply be removed. A was kept for a stated reason: **B fails OPEN if `now` moves backwards** (unsigned underflow → huge age → suppression lost → re-flood). 7 boundary conversions shipped. ⚠ **Honest coverage, because byte-identity only proves something for code that RUNS:** `channel_pull_recently` 170 in-window hits · `q_responded_recently` 207 · `mediated_recently`/`l2c_redirected_recently`/`join_id_denied` covered by explicit native suppression asserts · `age_out_mediated` runs every tick — **but `age_out_denied_ids` is exercised by NOTHING** (1-day TTL vs ≤30-min scenario runs) and rests on the algebra alone. **OWED: a native test for it.** Related drift found, not fixed (C1): `test/test_node_join.cpp:5` claims the file covers "denied-list aging" and **no TEST_CASE drives it.**

**Coder corrections to QA's brief (all verified):** there is a **7th ring** QA missed — the DAD denied-id ring (`join_id_denied` `node_join.cpp:36` / `join_deny_id` `:43`, `DeniedId` `node.h:1276`, Node-global) · the sweeps number **2, not 3** · Variant B has **5** sites, not 4. Correctly REFUSED as non-fits, with reasons: `id_bind_age_out` (self-binding exemption + per-drop telemetry), `peer_key_age_out` (`pinned` exemption), `mobile_home_age_out` (same shape but a *binding cache* from the P2-6 family), and decisively `rreq_rate_ok` — same `{dst,ttl,t_ms}` shape but it **refuses** when full with `table_cap_hit` instead of evicting, and its cap is the RUNTIME `_cfg.cap_route_request_last`, not the array extent. `channel_origin_admit` / the R4.4 `OrigRing` are windowed *counting* quotas, not seen/mark rings.

**QA-INDEPENDENT GATE (all reproduced personally):** native **851/26327/0 — count EXACT and unchanged** (a refactor adds no tests) · **all 19 anchored scenarios byte-identical at the 25j anchors, 0 assertion failures each** (coder additionally `cmp`-proved all 27 incl. the 8 non-anchored, 3-run stable) · **`sizeof(Node)` = 220584 confirmed POSITIVELY** under the real native config · **`-Wswitch` still exactly 2** (the two known enum→string defects — no new warnings; `recent_ring.h` compiles clean standalone under `-Wall -Wextra -Wreorder`) · sim repo untouched. ★ **BOARDS 10/10 with ΔRAM = +0 on EVERY env** (164868/208428/209020/190300/233860/234452/163836/164388/207948/208540 B, all unchanged) — the layout-by-construction claim proven per board. ΔFlash **−11828 B total**, split cleanly by ISA: every nRF52/ARM env **shrinks** 3376–4096 B, every ESP32/Xtensa env **grows** 252–516 B (different inlining of the template vs the hand-rolled loops); no env moves >0.5%. The three `gateway_*` envs compile `MR_FEAT_TEAM 0`, so the team-branch `#else` arm is genuinely gated, not a blind spot.

**★★ PROCESS FAILURE — MINE, and the fix is now standing practice.** I launched a 10-env board baseline loop **into the shared `.pio` tree AFTER dispatching the coder**, instead of completing it BEFORE. Consequences, disclosed by the coder unprompted: its board envs from `gateway` onward compiled MY-era tree state, and its `pkill` of what it believed was its own build killed three of my in-flight envs (my baseline job indeed returned `?` for two envs — now explained). **That baseline was invalid on two independent counts and is discarded.** The coder's mitigation is the correct pattern and is adopted: build each pass with **`PLATFORMIO_BUILD_DIR` pointed at an isolated scratchpad dir**, and derive the BEFORE tree from a **pristine `git archive` export** rather than reverting the working tree (verified to differ from the shipped tree in exactly the 7 slice files). Its before/after table is therefore self-contained and immune to any concurrent session — which is why the ΔRAM=+0 result above is trustworthy despite my collision. **Standing rule: QA board baselines are captured BEFORE dispatch, in an isolated build dir, and verified complete (all 10 envs reporting) before they are relied on.**

**Accepted deviations:** the boundary unification (sanctioned, and flagged as a semantic change *proven* unobservable rather than assumed absent) · `DeniedId::denied_at_ms` → `t_ms` (rename inside a refactor; layout-identical, 4 sites, no reference remains) · the owner committing mid-slice (the coder correctly diffed against the true pre-slice parent `4e8ebf0`, not `HEAD`, and verified the one intervening foreign `lib/core` change was comment-only) · sim native suite and multi-seed sweeps not re-run, both declared with reasons. ★ The coder also self-corrected **two of its own vacuous checks** before reporting — a `sizeof` assert that is `#ifdef MESHROUTE_NATIVE`-guarded so a bare `g++` "compiled" meaninglessly, and a `test/` grep for private method names that wrongly concluded two rings were untested (they are reached through public entry points and have explicit asserts).

**★★★ 2026-07-25k (FADING MEASURED ON METAL — v1 parameters ready; NOT YET APPLIED, activation still deferred by owner ruling):** the measured input for the §6.1.3 fading activation. Tool: **`tools/dm_delivery_breakdown.py`'s new sibling `tools/fading_from_logs.py`** (QA-owned), which turns raw firmware console traces (`t=… ms «rx <TYPE> … sf= snr= rssi=`) into the two numbers `LinkFadingState.cpp` consumes. Source: 5 owner-supplied captures (`dumps/`, gitignored) from **older firmware** — protocol behaviour in them is explicitly OUT of scope per owner ruling and NOT interpreted.

★ **RESULT — v1: `snr_std_dev = 0.35` dB on all links, `snr_coherence_ms = 0`.** Basis: **19 directed links, 1515 usable samples**, mean SNR spanning **−5.8 … +8.3 dB**; σ **mean 0.355 / median 0.368**, range 0.19–0.52, **stdev only 0.083**. Per band: weak (−10…−4) median 0.487 (n=3) · mid (−4…+2) 0.336 (n=9) · strong (+2…+10) 0.325 (n=7) — a mild rise toward weak links but well inside the spread, so **a single global σ is what this data supports; a σ(SNR) curve is NOT yet warranted.** Coherence 0 because **zero τ fits survive a conservative test across all 19 links** (details below) ⇒ at the cadence real traffic samples, each packet sees an independent draw, which is precisely the model's i.i.d. branch. ⚠ **Semantics, stated honestly: this σ is total REPORTED-SNR JITTER, not pure channel fading** — on a static path much of it is the SX126x SNR estimator. That is the correct thing to model anyway: the protocol consumes *reported* SNR (route scores, presence tiers, SF selection), so it experiences the lump sum regardless of cause, and the sim already models quantization + the +12 ceiling separately.

★★ **OWNER RULING 2026-07-25 — receiver 105 (`M2`) EXCLUDED as a FAULTY UNIT.** The data flagged it before the ruling: node 11, transmitting the same frames, is seen with σ 0.32 / 0.33 / 0.40 by receivers 58 / 79 / 39 — but **σ 1.07 by 105, at a middling +1.17 dB**, so it is not an SNR effect. Across its 4 links 105 reads σ median 0.84 vs 0.37 for all 19 others (**2.3×**), and its capture ends in a `Guru Meditation Error` panic. The owner confirmed **M2 was STATIC**, which rules out motion and leaves a hardware/firmware fault ⇒ excluded from the v1 numbers above. **Consequence: we still have NO motion measurement** — the mobile-regime σ remains unmeasured and is owed from the next hardware session (which is also when §6.1.3(B) activation was scheduled).

**Method traps the tool encodes (each reported, never silent):** saturation — LoRa PktSnr is untrustworthy above ~+10 dB and the sim models a +12 ceiling, so samples ≥ `--clip-db` are excluded and counted (**42 of the corpus samples were all-clipped series, correctly refusing to yield a σ**) · quantization — 0.25 dB grid, σ below ~0.15 dB unresolvable · censoring — deep fades LOSE packets, biasing σ **low**; periodic series (a mobile DISCOVER retry at a dead-regular **122 s**, beacons at ~287 s) make missing slots countable · **silent log drops** (`src/console_sink.h` drops a whole line when the USB FIFO is full and claims success) are indistinguishable from frame loss, so gap counts are an UPPER bound · mixed SF never pooled (this corpus: 16 links at SF6, 3 at SF7) · per-node clocks ⇒ series built strictly within a file, never across.

⚠ **Four QA errors found and fixed while validating against real captures — worth knowing because three would have silently corrupted the link table:** (1) **`origin=` is NOT self-identifying** — H frames preserve the originator across forwards, so `N1-1.txt` shows `origin=58` 54× while actually being node **39**; using it mislabels every link in the file. (2) **`»tx BCN from=` IS self-identifying** (a node's own beacon carries its own src, and beacons are never forwarded) — excluding it made two files fall through to the filename, and the filenames are role labels (`M1-1`, `N1-1` both yield "1"), not node ids. (3) a mobile's `[whoami] id=17` legitimately differs from its on-air `from=25` (home-leased local id) — **on-air wins** for link attribution. (4) the `to=`-vs-self cross-check raised false alarms on every file because **the trace is PROMISCUOUS** (nodes log every decoded frame, not only ones addressed to them); only a total absence of `to=self` is now treated as suspicious. Also tightened: the τ fit was emitting confident nonsense (a spurious **279 s** from ρ=0.99@0.12 s fitted against noise-level ρ=0.14@538 s) and the lag buckets were labelled by their edge rather than the real mean lag; τ now requires ≥3 well-sampled decaying points with genuine short-lag correlation, and **|ρ|>1 — impossible for a true autocorrelation, an artefact of normalising a high-local-variance subset — is flagged rather than used.** Cadence-gap detection was extrapolating across capture discontinuities (reported "16062 missing slots" on a 32-minute series); it now requires a dominant modal spacing and counts only short runs.

**NOT APPLIED:** no default changed, no scenario touched, `snr_coherence_ms` still 0 and every link still `snr_std_dev` 0 — §6.1.3(B) activation remains deferred to post-bench by owner ruling, so the numbers above are the *input* awaiting that slice, not a live change.

**★★★★ 2026-07-25j (Wave-4 §6.1.2 — TX→RX TURNAROUND PLUMBING + the `§rx-window-arming` PREREQUISITE — QA GO; ★ THE LARGEST RE-ANCHOR OF THE ARC: ALL 27 CORPUS SCENARIOS MOVED; SIM-REPO ONLY, firmware untouched; uncommitted):** the §6.1.2 queue item, delivered as **three** separately-gated steps because the briefed work turned out to be unmeasurable on its own.

**Step A — `tx_fail_prob` plumbing (BYTE-IDENTITY, 27/27).** `SimRadio::startSendRaw` is **dead code — zero call sites in the repo**, so the whole tx-fail path was dead end-to-end: `JsonConfig` parsed AND range-validated `tx_fail_prob`, then nothing plumbed it. Now rolled via an extracted `SimRadio::rollTxFail()` (one implementation, called by both the live path and the vestigial `startSendRaw` — U1). Draw-free at prob 0 (the guard is load-bearing), so the corpus — which sets it nowhere — is **byte-identical, verified by `cmp` on full NDJSON across 27 scenarios, not md5 prefixes**. Dropped-TX semantics were **derived from metal, not invented** (`device_hal.cpp:36-46` — a failed arm drops the frame, no retry, no ledger debit): emit `tx_fail`, push no InFlight, debit no airtime, no `onRadioBusy`. Revived 5 dead members incl. **`SimRadio::seed()`, which was never called — every radio shared `_rng_state == 1`** (a latent Slice-C violation: had anyone enabled tx_fail_prob, all nodes would have failed on the SAME tx indices). New `RngDomain::TxFail` so enabling tx-fail cannot shift a node's firmware draws.

**★★★ Step B0 — the UNBRIEFED PREREQUISITE, and the real story of this slice (F1).** My brief asserted "the RX→TX direction is ALREADY correct — do not redo it." **HALF WRONG, and the coder proved it.** `getEarliestTxMs()` *was* honoured, but the value it returns was **armed at the wrong instant**: `notifyRxStart(airtime)` computes `clock_now + duration`, so whenever a frame's start was itself deferred by the *sender's* own RX→TX turnaround (`f.start_ms > now` — **every reply leg of every RTS/CTS/DATA/ACK handshake**), each observer's `_rx_active_until` landed at `f.end_ms − 8` and its `_earliest_tx_ms` at exactly `f.end_ms`. **Every reply therefore began with ZERO turnaround.** QA verified the arithmetic at source (`SimRadio.cpp:50-57`: `new_until = now + duration; _earliest_tx_ms = new_until + rx_to_tx_delay`). Harmless while nothing modelled TX→RX; **lethal the moment it is** — the originator is deaf until `own_end + 8`, so a reply starting at `end_ms + 0` lands inside the deaf window and every CTS/ACK dies. Fix = one expression (`just_pushed.end_ms - now`), provably inert for any non-deferred frame (`f.start_ms == now ⇒ end_ms - now == airtime`).
★ **Measured proof that Step B alone was unmeasurable:** with B but without B0 — **10 of 11 mandatory scenarios go multi-assert red** (s27 = 21 failures), s18 events +41%, `tx` +56%, and s21's tx histogram reads **`ACK 11 → 0`** — every acknowledgement destroyed. 10,708 settling drops corpus-wide, **of which essentially all were artefacts**: with B0 the corpus total is **3**. Anyone reading the B-only numbers as a realism cost would have concluded the opposite of the truth.

**Step B — the TX→RX deaf window.** The receiver's own-TX interval now extends `tx_to_rx_delay_ms` past its `end_ms` for the deafness test, in **both** arms of the existing gate (`_in_flight` scan + the `_node_last_tx_*` compacted-out fallback), read from config not a literal. Concurrent is tested FIRST so `drop_halfduplex` classification is bit-unchanged and only previously-**delivered** frames can become settling drops — that ordering is what made the forensics attributable. New **11th** drop emitter `drop_tx_settling{deaf_until_ms}` (distinct from `drop_halfduplex` — different hardware mechanism, precedent in `drop_rx_blind`'s `blind_until_ms`; merging them would have destroyed this slice's own evidence, since `drop_halfduplex` goes to **0** in s18/s17 while settling drops appear); `test_eventlog_drops.cpp` extended to all 11.

★★ **DELIVERY IMPROVED, MATERIALLY — and all of it is B0, not B.** s18 **88/113 → 99/113** (78%→88%), giveup 17→11, in-flight 8→3 · **s17 26/30 → 30/30** (the 4 long-standing `SL: giveup` failures GONE) · s15 XL **17/21 → 19/21** (81→90.5%) + channels 198→205/224 · s16 XL **74/80 → 76/80** (92.5→95%) · s15 same-layer 51→53, s16 74→76 · s09/s09_metal/s10/s19 unchanged · leaks 0 everywhere. **s18 multi-seed {1,42,100}: {97,88,99} → {104, 99, 100}** (QA reproduced both the old reference on the pre binary and the new md5s `3e1621f4` / `c9167d30` / `9771eb9c`). Mechanism: pre-B0 EVERY node's `_earliest_tx_ms` sat 8 ms early, so every node's contention floor was 8 ms too low → more collisions; correct arming spreads transmissions and delivery rises. **Attribution proven by an A/B at `settle=0`: 26 of 27 scenarios are byte-identical between A+B0 and A+B0+B ⇒ Step B is delivery-NEUTRAL on the entire delivery suite** (only twin_9node_dm moves, by 3 settling drops).

★ **THE INDEPENDENT PROOF (QA reproduced the coder's headline number from scratch):** an audit reconstructing frame windows **only from rx events** (`time_ms − airtime_ms`, avoiding `EventLog::tx`'s stamp — see the finding below) counts, in the PRE-slice s18: **0** receptions delivered while the receiver was transmitting, but **1309 delivered inside the 8 ms settling tail** — frames a real radio could not have heard. POST-slice: **0**. That is the defect measured and closed, independently of the gate that fixes it. ⚠ QA process note: my *first* audit attempt returned 0/0 because I keyed on a field `tx` events do not carry (`from` vs `node`) — a **vacuous check of exactly the kind this project makes coders prove against**. Corrected before reporting.

**ACCEPTED DEVIATIONS:** (1) ★ **Step B0 itself** — unbriefed and semantics-changing. ACCEPTED: Step B is unmeasurable without it, and the alternatives were both forbidden (weakening the deaf-window boundary = "tuning the sim to protect a number"; stopping = handing back a red corpus with the mechanism unmeasured). Revert is one expression, but Step B cannot then be accepted. (2) `RngDomain::TxFail` rather than the briefed `_node_rng[i]` — strictly more isolated, prevents tx-fail from shifting firmware draws. (3) a small extraction inside a fix (C1) — provably behaviour-preserving because `startSendRaw` is dead. (4) two permanent test fixtures in the sim repo's `test/native/` using the sanctioned `allow_deprecated_lua` opt-in (25e). (5) drifted comment repaired (V1). Boards not built (zero firmware source) and the s15/s16/s17 multi-seed sweeps not re-run — both declared.

**OWED SIBLINGS (recorded IN CODE per the owner's done-vs-missing instruction — see the quoted blocks in the coder report):** ★ **the `startSendRaw` bypass itself STILL STANDS** — the live TX path still synthesises its InFlight by hand, so `_earliest_rx_ms`/`_tx_done_at`/`_state`/`_packets_sent` never move and `isSendComplete()`/`onSendFinished()`/`getPacketsSent()` are inert in an `lus` run; the deaf window is recomputed in the delivery gate rather than read from the radio, so **the two must be kept consistent by hand**. Unifying the paths is a large refactor (C1). · **F4: CAD-mode LBT still hears preambles that arrived during a node's own TX + settling** (`SimRadio::notifyChannelBusy`'s clamp is a no-op because `_earliest_rx_ms` is never set) — inert under the default `"energy"` model; belongs to the LBT plane. · **F5: `_node_last_tx_*` holds only the LATEST TX**, so an earlier TX's settling tail can be masked when a deferred later TX overwrites the slot — quantified at **45/44596 deliveries in s18 (0.1%)**, affects the concurrent arm identically, predates this slice. · ★ **NEW QA FINDING: `EventLog::tx` is stamped with `now` (the DECISION time), not `f.start_ms` (the possibly-deferred air time)** — so tx-event timestamps can lead the real transmission by up to `rx_to_tx_delay_ms`, and **any timing analysis over the NDJSON is unreliable at that precision** (this is why the audit above uses rx events only). Post-B0 deferral is MORE common, so the discrepancy widens. Worth its own small slice.

**★★ 2026-07-25g (DELIVERY-TOOL KEYING FIXED — short ids are unique only WITHIN a layer; s10's remaining artifacts CLOSED; QA-owned, no firmware/sim code touched):** the third and last symptom of the 25f root cause. `tools/dm_delivery_breakdown.py` keyed on bare short ids, which are unique only per-layer — s10's `l4_seed` and `l5_seed` are BOTH `node_id: 1` (legal). Three consequences, all fixed: (1) `id_to_name` let the last node win, so s10's **layer-4 row rendered as `l5_seed(1)`** — a silently wrong label; (2) `xl_orig` was keyed `(origin, ctr)` with a `not in` guard, so once both cross-layer sends landed on `(1,2)` the **second origination was silently DROPPED** → `cross-layer DMs: 1/1` while the stream carried **2** `tx_enqueue_xl` and 2 matching `delivered`; (3) `delivered_oc` was likewise `(origin, ctr)`-keyed first-only, discarding the second arrival. **Fixes:** origination keyed `(origin, ctr, target_layer)` and arrival keyed `(origin, ctr, target_dst)` — both fields the events ALREADY carry, so nothing new is inferred; pairing now matches each origination to the delivery whose resolved dst lies in that origination's `target_layer` (with a `consumed` set so two originations sharing `(origin,ctr)` cannot both claim one delivery and double-count), falling back to earliest-unclaimed when a dst's layer is unknown — which reproduces the old behaviour on older streams. Plus **fail-loud aliasing**: `load_cfg` now detects short ids shared by several nodes and prints a `!! AMBIGUOUS NODE IDS` block naming them and their layers, and `fmt_node` renders every candidate (`l4_seed|l5_seed(1)`) instead of picking one. ⚠ **id 0 is deliberately EXCLUDED** — it is the reserved "unprovisioned" sentinel, so the mobiles sharing it in s27/s28/s29 are not aliased (each leases a real id at registration); QA verified those three stay silent while s10 reports precisely `id 1: l4_seed, l5_seed (layers 4, 5)`. **RESULT — s10's two remaining "artifacts" from 25f are now GONE, and they were real under-counts:** same-layer **3/3 → `4/4` (100%, four distinct rows — the 4 configured commands finally all visible)** and cross-layer **1/1 → `2/2` (100%)**. **VERIFICATION:** every other delivery scenario is **unchanged** against the values QA measured minutes earlier — s09 4/4 + XL 2/2 · s09_metal 3/3 + XL 2/3 · s15 51/51 + XL 17/21 · s16 74/74 + XL 74/80 · s17 26/30 + XL 0/36 · s18 113/88 · s19 8/8 — and a corpus scan confirms **s10 is the ONLY scenario with a genuine (non-sentinel) duplicate short id**, so it is the only one that could move. `--all`/`--pair`/`--json`/`--failures` all re-smoke-tested, and `--failures` now agrees with the table at 4/4. No md5 moves: this changes how delivery is READ, not what the sim does. ★ The 25d and 25f notes' s10 delivery figures are SUPERSEDED by this note; the table above carries the current values.

**★★★ 2026-07-25f (HARNESS `send <name>` RESOLUTION FIXED — ★ THE s10 CARVE IS CLOSED, 0 FAILURES; s09/s09_metal/s10 RE-ANCHORED — QA GO; SIM-REPO ONLY; uncommitted):** the fix for the 25d root cause. The scenario host resolved `send <name>` to the addressee's **live `protocolId()`**, which for a dual-layer gateway is re-stamped on every window swap (`lib/core/node.cpp:493` `_hal.set_protocol_id(L.node_id)` in `activate_layer` → `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp:74` → `FirmwareNode::simSetProtocolId`), so correctness was a function of **when the command fired**. Now resolved **on the sender's layer**: a multi-layer addressee contributes the `node_id` of its `layers[]` entry matching the sender's layer; **fail-loud REFUSAL** when no layer is shared, and also when the **SENDER** is multi-layer (its "current" layer is whichever window its scheduler has active — resolving against its home leaf would be well-defined but still silently wrong in the other window, i.e. the same timing accident moved to the sender side). Refusal runs as a **pre-flight in `initialize()`** (0 bytes emitted, matching the 25e choke-point reasoning) with the fire-site `default:` kept as a hard stop so the silent fallback cannot be reintroduced. **QA-INDEPENDENT GATE:** fw native **851/26327** · sim native all pass (9 suites) · **the 16 must-not-move scenarios ALL BYTE-IDENTICAL, 0 failures** (s18/s21–s30/s19/s15/s15_metal/s16/s17) · coder additionally proved the **9 non-anchored** corpus scenarios byte-identical via `cmp` against a rebuilt pre-slice binary. **RE-ANCHORED (3-run stable): s09 `371b1702`/2266 [was `0c758254`/2373, −107] · s09_metal `372445e9`/2345 [was `9f003405`/2456, −111] · s10 `8acad73f`/2266 [was `56f82b67`/2373, −107].** s10 lands at **exactly 2266 — the count the 25d note recorded for the hardcoded `send 10` probe**, i.e. the harness fix is behaviourally equivalent to that probe without hardcoding anything. Forensic attribution (same shape all three): the DM now completes (`send_deferred`/`send_deferred_giveup` 1→0; a full `rts/cts/data/ack` exchange each +1; `delivered` 3→4) **and a doomed route-discovery storm disappears** (`rreq_rx` 10→0, `rreq_forward` 5→0, `r_tx` 4→0, `rt_update` 125→114 — with an unroutable dst the origin flooded RREQs that could never resolve; with the correct dst the beacon-DV route already exists, so no discovery at all) plus airtime knock-on (`tx` 224→214, `rx` 408→385, `collision` 10→3). **★ ACCEPTANCE — THE TRIPWIRE IS GREEN: `s10` = `2266 events, 0 assertion failure(s)`.** The assert `gw_4 delivered l4-local-seed-to-gw` — which the queue wanted deleted as "stale" and which QA preserved specifically as this bug's tripwire — now fires (`t=72713 dst:10`). **Window-phase independence proven:** sweeping the send across a full 12 s grid, the pre-slice binary delivered at 60/62/64/72 s but resolved to 111 and FAILED at 66/68/70 s; post-slice it resolves to 10 and delivers at **every** phase. Delivery: **s09 3/4 → 4/4 (100%)** · **s09_metal 2/3 → 3/3 (100%)** · **s10 3/4 → 3/3 (100%)**, `!! UNSENT` gone from all three. ⚠ **s10 reads 3/3 not 4/4, and its cross-layer line reads `1/1` not `2/2` — BOTH are METRIC ARTIFACTS, not regressions, QA-verified at the event level:** `tx_enqueue_xl` is **2 before and 2 after**, and **all four payloads are `delivered`** (`l4-local-seed-to-gw`, `l5-local-seed-to-gw`, `cross-l4-to-l5-joiner`, `cross-l5-to-l4-joiner`). Root cause: **`l4_seed` and `l5_seed` both carry `node_id: 1`** (legal — different layers), so `dm_delivery_breakdown.py` keys both onto one origin; and post-fix both XL sends land on `(origin=1, ctr=2)` because the previously-given-up send now consumes ctr=1, collapsing two rows into one. ★ **OWED TOOL FIX (third symptom of one cause): the delivery tool must key on `(layer, node_id)` or the node NAME, not the bare short id — short ids are unique only WITHIN a layer.** The same aliasing already mislabels s10's layer-4 row as `l5_seed(1)`. **ACCEPTED DEVIATIONS:** (1) ★★ **the coder DISPROVED a QA premise with an A/B and was right:** my brief said a single-layer addressee should resolve to its config `node_id`; it kept the **live `protocolId()`** for that path and overrides only for multi-layer addressees. Variant B implementing my wording verbatim **moved a FOURTH scenario** (`s07_seattle_mobile_meshroute` `e66f5b9a`→`d6564f59`) — because a mobile's live id is a home-assigned lease, not its config value: QA confirmed **s27's mobiles carry `node_id: 0`** outright and s07's carry 50/51/52 while running on leased 254/253/252. Config `node_id` cannot see a DAD/join/mobile lease; a single-layer node wears exactly one id so its live value is unambiguous. The bug was specifically the ambiguity of a MULTI-id node. (2) a permanent native test `test_send_by_name_layer.cpp`, proven non-vacuous (aborts against the pre-slice source) — justified because the corpus has **zero** commands on any refusal path, so suite byte-identity pins none of the guard. **★★★ 2026-07-25d (D-1/D-2 — THE DELIVERY METRIC WAS HIDING ITS OWN FAILURES; tool fixed + s09/s09_metal/s10 delivery re-anchored; QA-owned change, no firmware/sim code touched):** surfaced by the s10 assert triage: the ONE assert the queue wanted deleted as "stale" (`gw_4 delivered l4-local-seed-to-gw`) had been **telling the truth for weeks**. ★ **ROOT CAUSE (sim harness):** `SimController.cpp:874-882` resolves a `send <name>` command to the target's **LIVE `protocolId()` at command time**, and a dual-layer gateway's id **alternates with its active window** (s10: gw_4 = 10 on layer 4 / 111 on layer 5). So a layer-4 send issued while the gateway wore its layer-5 id was addressed to an id unroutable from layer 4 → `send_deferred_giveup`. Proven both ways by throwaway probes: change ONLY the timing (70000→60000), or ONLY the address (`send gw_4`→`send 10`), and the DM delivers. The mirror `l5_seed→gw_5` passes purely by luck of window phase. **The firmware is correct and failed loud; the SCENARIO HOST mis-addressed the frame.** ★★ **D-1 — and the metric hid it:** the mis-resolved dst is absent from `configured_pairs`, so `tools/dm_delivery_breakdown.py` **dropped the row entirely** rather than counting it failed — the pair reported **3/3 = 100%** while 4 sends were intended and one never arrived. ★★ **D-2 — live in the MANDATORY suite:** `s09_two_layer_gateway` carries the identical command, fails identically (`dst:119`, giveup at the same millisecond), and reports **0 assertion failures** because someone deleted the equivalent assert instead of fixing it. s10's assert was **the only thing in the entire corpus surfacing this class** — deleting it, as the queue instructed, would have made us permanently blind. **TOOL FIX (QA, `tools/dm_delivery_breakdown.py`):** `configured_pairs` now returns COUNTS (a Counter) rather than a set, and `summarise()` injects an intended-but-unobserved send as **sent-with-0-arrived** — mirroring the idiom already used for drop-only `no_gw` pairs ("inject them so the loss is counted", U1) — plus a loud `!! UNSENT` block naming each one and the dst its origin actually addressed. ⚠ The same blind spot existed in `render_dm_failures`, which is the view **BASELINE's own gate recipe runs (`--failures`)**; fixed identically with its own mechanism line, and the two views now agree on every scenario. ★ **A false positive I caught in my own first cut and fixed:** injection must apply to **same-layer (`send <name>`) intents ONLY** — a `send_layer` record's `effective_dst` is the GATEWAY's wire id, never the final destination, so a cross-layer intent legitimately has no row under its own key and injecting it reported s10's genuinely-DELIVERED `cross-l4-to-l5-joiner` as unsent. Cross-layer keeps its dedicated honest denominator (the `cross-layer DMs: X/Y` line). **VERIFICATION:** before/after tool output diffed across all 9 delivery scenarios × BOTH modes (default + `--failures`) — **exactly three differ** (s09, s09_metal, s10, all genuine) and **s15/s15_metal/s16/s17/s18/s19 are byte-identical**, which is the proof the fix is surgical and not a re-scoring of the suite. `--all`/`--pair`/`--json`/`--failures` all smoke-tested. **RE-ANCHORED DELIVERY REFERENCES (same-layer): s09 3/3→`3/4` (75%) · s09_metal 2/2→`2/3` (67%) · s10 3/3→`3/4` (75%).** Cross-layer unchanged everywhere (s09/s10 2/2). No md5 moves — this is a change in how delivery is READ, not in what the sim does; **every scenario anchor stands.** ⚠ **OWED FOLLOW-UP (sim repo, not done):** fix the harness to resolve `send <name>` against a **plane-appropriate** id (the sender's layer matched against the target's `layers[]`) or fail loud on ambiguity, instead of the momentary `protocolId()`. Deliberately NOT patched by editing the scenarios to `send 10`: that one-token change would move s10's stream (2373→2266), raise its reference to 4/4, and **re-hide both findings**.

**★★ 2026-07-25c (F-BW-TX — TX bandwidth now FOLLOWS the retune — QA GO; SIM-REPO ONLY, firmware untouched; uncommitted):** the completion of §6.1.1. The 25b gate fixed RX isolation but a node's TRANSMIT bandwidth stayed frozen at its configured `bw`, so a dual-BW gateway could hear both layers and transmit to only one. Root cause (QA-re-verified at source): `TxParams::bw_hz` defaults to `-1` (`lib/core/hal.h:27`) and NO firmware site ever assigns it, because on metal `DeviceHal::set_rx_bw` mirrors the retune into `_def_bw` and `DeviceHal::tx` resolves the sentinel against it (`device_hal.cpp:70-71` + `:21`); the sim's `simSetRxBw` never touched `_radios[i]`, so both TX sentinel sites (`SimController.cpp:1366` warmup / `:1418` physics) fell back to `getBwHz()` = the frozen config value. **Fix = the missing mirror, 2 statements:** `FirmwareNode::simSetRxBw` (and its Lua twin `ScriptedNode::api_set_rx_bw`) now also retune their own `SimRadio` bandwidth, so the pre-existing sentinel resolution and `getEstAirtimeFor()`/`getSymbolMs()` debit airtime at the ON-AIR bandwidth for free — no new container, no new `INode` virtual (the coder found `FirmwareNode` already holds `SimRadio& _radio`, previously `(void)`-suppressed as unused). ★ RX-gate and TX-bw stay DELIBERATELY separate: `_node_rx_bw_hz` remains the decode gate's own slot and must NEVER be re-pointed at `getBwHz()` (a TX-side notion — `setRadioParams` is stamped from every outgoing frame's params, and Lua's per-frame `bw` override depends on that). **★ THE EXPECTATION CORRECTION, EMPIRICALLY SETTLED:** the 25b coder assumed this "moves airtime, hence bytes"; QA predicted byte-identity instead, and instrumentation across all 19 scenarios PROVED IT — **2777 `simSetRxBw` calls, 2777 value-preserving, ZERO changed**, on both the TX and RX axes. Every retuning scenario uses exactly ONE bandwidth for its whole run, so a retune is a no-op there; the 12 non-retuning scenarios cannot call it at all (`activate_layer` needs `n_layers==2`; the mobile paths are gated behind `retune_radio` / `scan_set_count()>1`). Only 7 scenarios retune: s09, s09_metal, s10 (82 each, 250000) · s15, s15_metal (1080 each, 62500) · s16 (174) · s27 (197). **QA-INDEPENDENT GATE (every number personally reproduced):** fw native **851/26327** · sim native all pass (22→23 tests) · **ALL NINETEEN scenarios BYTE-IDENTICAL** at the 25b anchors, 0 fails except s10's 23 known-dead · **0 `drop_bw_mismatch` anywhere in the 19** · delivery unchanged (s19 8/8 · s16 74/80 · s17 26/30 · s18 88/113 seed-42 · s15 XL 17/21 + ch 198/224 leaks 0). **NON-VACUOUSNESS — the dual-BW gateway probe is the acceptance test, QA-reproduced before AND after:** `rx gw→l5` **0 → 1** (the 250 kHz leaf can finally hear its gateway at all); gw TX bandwidth histogram **{125k:4} → {125k:2, 250k:2}** = TX now alternates in step with the 12 s window grid; `rx gw→l4` 4→2 with `drop_bw_mismatch gw→l4` 0→2 — **that drop is the POINT**, the gateway no longer illegally reaches its 125 kHz leaf during a 250 kHz window. The 4 gw TXes toward l5 account exactly: 2 bw-mismatch (125 kHz windows, correct) + 1 `drop_preamble_miss` (probabilistic, l5 was correctly tuned) + 1 `rx`. ★ **Airtime coupling confirmed** (the half most likely to rot silently): the same beacon costs **80 ms @125 kHz → 40 ms @250 kHz** (exact half; 75→37 likewise), and the native test pins it as a RELATION (`|air125 − 2·air250| ≤ 1`), not a magic constant. Protocol-level corroboration: `link_bidi_confirm` **12→24** and `link_one_way` **1→0** — a gateway that can finally talk to both layers gets its bidirectionality census confirmed both ways. **ACCEPTED DEVIATIONS:** (1) ★ the coder also mirrored the **Lua twin** `ScriptedNode::api_set_rx_bw`, which the brief named only as a test driver — **accepted, and it is MORE correct than not doing it: QA verified metal's asymmetry is DELIBERATE** — `DeviceHal::set_rx_bw` sets `_def_bw` (`:70`) and `set_rx_cr` sets `_def_cr` (`:76`), but **`set_rx_sf` does NOT touch `_def_sf`** (`:59-62`) because firmware always sets `TxParams::sf` explicitly. Mirroring bandwidth-but-not-SF is exactly device-consistent, and leaving the twin half-mirrored would have made a native test pin behaviour the firmware path lacks. (2) the permanent fixture lives in the sim repo's `test/native/` (same precedent as 25b). (3) three drifted comments repaired in files touched (V1), incl. `FirmwareNode.h`'s "unused in S2 path" and `simSetRxBw`'s stale `device_radio.h:216` citation — `_def_bw` lives in `DeviceHal`, not `DeviceRadio`. (4) boards not built — correct, zero firmware source touched (QA had built 10/10 earlier the same day). (5) s18 seeds {1,100} skipped and DECLARED — QA had already verified `{97,88,99}` independently, and byte-identity makes them inert by construction. **OWED SIBLINGS (verified at source, deliberately not fixed):** ★ the **CR axis is unplumbed end-to-end** — `simSetRxCr` does not exist, `ISimHal` declares only `simSetRxSf`/`simSetRxBw`, `NodeRuntimeWrapper.cpp:65-66` overrides only those two, so the firmware's three `_hal.set_rx_cr(...)` calls fall through to a base no-op on BOTH axes while metal mirrors `_def_cr` identically; inert today (whole corpus is `cr:5`), owed slice. · **per-node frequency** still absent (only the global `frequency_mhz`; `set_rx_freq` also never overridden) — its own slice, and note "BW/freq gating" was NEVER one slice. · **BW on the PreambleDetected gate** (`SimController.cpp:~1738`, SF-only today, its own comment admits "quasi-orthogonal *at the same BW*") — ⚠ **SHARPENED by this fix**: a dual-BW node now emits at two bandwidths, so cross-BW preamble callbacks become materially more frequent. · the warmup path's absence of physics gating (pre-existing; its sentinel site is coherent with the fix for free).

**★★★ 2026-07-23 — THE GRAND RATIFICATION (Slice C + 8 ms turnaround + antidote + fallout, ONE re-anchor; uncommitted):** the sim now runs per-node/per-link RNG streams (Slice C — real radios are independent; the shared-mt19937 draw-order coupling is GONE) at the bench-measured 8 ms turnaround (27 ms was a stale overestimate). Slice C exposed 3 REAL weakness classes, all FIXED: (1) originator route-miss → REQ_SYNC pull (s19); (2) degenerate cached gateway schedules → 5-min schedule re-advert at window-activation (s15, deterministic-livelock root); (3) ★ the R4.3 quiet-throttle was PLANE-BLIND (P2-7's flagged spot) — team members' steady re-advertisement was suppressed whenever static traffic kept `since_rx` fresh → team mediation/flood-coverage starved (s30's mediator saw ONE beacon in 400 s); fixed by a team-cadence re-advert carve-out in the two throttle gates (team-gated, static byte-inert — s18 proven identical with/without). QA retunes applied: s23 team ids PINNED 115/254 (RNG reallocation had orphaned the hardcoded ids — DV itself converges in ~30 s, the original hypothesis was wrong); s27 post-m3→1120k / post-re-m4→1130k (re-home transients: the ~386 s dead-home detection lag + the origin-namespace ctr collision — both SHELVED as real findings, below); s22 S2 host_mobiles:false (unpinned dual-offer tie-break). **MANDATORY ANCHORS (all 0-fail, 3-run stable): s18 `49e6d367`/269367 · s21 `9012a3e0`/669 · s22 `9567ca73`/1751 · s23 `9741d774`/797 · s24 `86cfd50f`/1355 · s25 `6eb042cd`/536 · s26 `998d1e9d`/692 · s27 `0be67ad7`/9446 · s28 `70ab691f`/3634 · s29 `a60d237d`/1629 · s30 `00a96ca3`/823.** Native **845/26289** · boards 10/10 · sizeof(Node) 220392. ★ SHELF (real, metal-relevant, dedicated slices owed): (i) **[CORRECTED 2026-07-24 — the user caught a mis-framing]** the e2e-ack mechanism IS plane- and crypto-agnostic (DATA_FLAG_E2E_ACK_REQ works for plaintext `-a` identically; threaded through parking node_hashlocate.cpp:1340 + delegated re-origination node_mac_rx.cpp:724 — verified) — the REAL gap is only that the ack is a POSITIVE-ONLY receipt with NO firmware deadline: ack requested + never arrives = silence forever (no awaiting-ack state exists). Fix = a small ack-deadline timer (patience-budget-derived) → `send_failed{e2e_ack_timeout}`, OR rule the deadline app-side by design — awaiting the owner's call; (ii) a mobile stamps origin=home_id so its own frames can (origin,ctr)-alias its home's delegated re-originations in relay queues after a re-home ctr reset (fix: plane/type discriminator in the gateway-hold queue, or ctr-jump on re-home); (iii) the ~386 s dead-home inbound-black-hole window under the presence cadence.

- **(historical) ★ Slice C (Wave-4 per-node RNG, UNRATIFIED — 2026-07-22 QA):** the decoupled RNG drops s19 8/8→5/8 (relay contention-starvation — **LBT ruled out**, A/B-confirmed) and s15 cross-layer mean ~90→~70% (gateway-window misalignment). Both are REAL mechanisms (not seed fragility) with a likely shared route-re-advertisement root. **Do NOT ratify Slice C references** until the reactive-pull/re-advertise antidote lands + re-measures under Slice C. Full root-cause + evidence: `docs/2026-07-20-realism-and-duplication-review.md` §"Wave-4 Slice-C investigation".
- **`mean_hops` in DENSE scenarios is cascade-inclusive, NOT the delivered-path length.** It counts *distinct nodes that data_rx'd the DM as a next-hop* — in a sparse chain (s19) that equals the path (3); in a dense mesh (**s18 ~10, s17 ~10.3**) it also counts every next-hop a cascading/contending DM touched, so it reads ~10 even for short deliveries. **Gate on relative STABILITY vs these numbers**, not the absolute value. A big jump = routing churn to investigate.
- Cross-layer is measured via `tx_enqueue_xl` + the `(origin,ctr)`-matched `delivered` (the firmware preserves origin/ctr) — the `dm_delivery_breakdown.py` "cross-layer DMs: X/Y" line is the authoritative per-scenario cross-layer number.
- Gateway scenarios were translated from `~/lora-universal-simulator/scenarios/` via `tools/translate_gateways.py` (adds the C++ `n_layers:2 + layers[]` dual-layer schema). `s18`/`s17` are the untranslated sources.

**Removed as obsolete/non-working:** `s09_two_layer_gateway_debug` (1-DM debug), `s15_three_layer_dual` (engine-untagged Lua-comparison variant), `s20_crosslayer` / `s21_multihop_xl` (0 gateways → non-functional cross-layer), `s22_location` (2-node/1-DM, too thin). Note `s21_leaf_config_divergence` + `s22_leaf_config_join` (R6) are membership-gate scenarios, not part of this delivery suite.

```
lus -e meshroute simulation/<s>.json /tmp/<s>.ndjson   # the "… N assertion failure(s)" line must be N=0
```
